If unresolved, the Reeds ritual completes. During the final battle, the ground-level fog counts as poisonous terrain. At the start of each round, each non-cult creature standing in low fog must succeed on a **DC 13 Constitution saving throw** or take 1d6 poison damage and lose 1 Sanity.

#### Site Two — Isle of Crags: The Hollow Mountain Amplifier

**Purpose:** Turn the Whispering Caves' fossilized anatomy into an enormous resonance instrument capable of cracking consensus reality.

**Local Face:** Crag-herders believe the mountain has become angry. Goats refuse paths they have walked for generations. Old stone cairns hum at night. Shepherd's Rest hears knocking from inside sealed rock.

**Ritual Engine:** In the Deep Ritual Hall, the cult has completed an arcane resonator around the void-circle. The device uses cave crystal, copper wire, and captive voices to amplify the fossilized Heart's seven-second pulse.

**Default Encounter Roster:**
- **Voice of the Mountain** if Ossian survived; otherwise use 1 **Face of the Maw** as Greaves's replacement overseer.
- 2 **Grave-Maw Zealots**.
- 4 **Sour-touched Initiates**.
- Optional: 1 **Watcher at the Threshold** guarding the resonator approach.

**Layer Reveals:**
- **Layer 1:** Whisper lines show which tunnels are carrying sound toward the ritual chamber.
- **Layer 2:** Organic conduit traces reveal the resonator's feeder lines.
- **Layer 3:** The full anatomical truth becomes tactical: the device is attached to fossilized nerve bundles, not stone.

**Objectives:**
- Disable the resonator before it reaches full harmonic lock.
- Free the voice-captives suspended around the void-circle.
- Prevent Ossian from completing the first reality-shattering note.

**Mechanical Resolution:**
The resonator has four components:

| Component | AC | HP | Visible At | Disable Method |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Copper voice cage | 13 | 20 | Layer 0 | DC 14 Strength or thieves' tools |
| Crystal harmonic spine | 15 | 30 | Layer 1 | Thunder damage or DC 16 Arcana |
| Organic feeder conduits | 14 | 25 | Layer 2 | Slashing/radiant damage or DC 15 Medicine/Nature |
| Fossil nerve anchor | 16 | 35 | Layer 3 | DC 18 Insight, radiant damage, or Still Shard use |

At initiative count 20, the resonator charges. After **5 charges**, it releases the first reality-shattering scream: every creature in the chamber makes a **DC 16 Sanity saving throw**, losing **3d10 Sanity** on a failure or half as much on a success. Disabling two components slows the charge to every other round. Disabling all four stops the site.

**Failure Consequence:**
If unresolved, the Crags ritual completes. During the final battle, Greaves or the Echo may trigger **Mountain Scream** once: all non-cult creatures must make a **DC 15 Constitution saving throw** or be deafened for 1 minute and lose 1d8 Sanity.

#### Site Three — Isle of Murk: The Gaping Maw Cannery

**Purpose:** Use industrial machinery, captive townsfolk, and the cannery's original murder-site resonance to make the Souring physical.

**Local Face:** The cannery locks down under Greaves's emergency authority. Workers are told there has been a contamination incident. Families searching for missing people are turned away by harbor guards.

**Ritual Engine:** The processing floor has been converted into a sacrificial machine. Hooks, belts, steam vents, and rendering vats move in a rhythm that matches the seven-second pulse from the Whispering Caves.

**Default Encounter Roster:**
- **Harbormaster Greaves** using the **Face of the Maw** stat block, unless you have built a custom social-boss version.
- 2 **Gaping Maw Assassins**.
- 2 **Shine-Whisperers**.
- Optional: **Cannery Horror** if it was not destroyed earlier.

**Layer Reveals:**
- **Layer 1:** The original spiral from Finnian's death glows under the grime.
- **Layer 2:** Three Souring Anchors become visible around the machinery. They are immune to damage from Tier 0/Tier 1 characters because those characters cannot fully locate them.
- **Layer 3:** Cosmic string lines show the exact connection between the Anchors, Greaves, and the forming Echo.

**Objectives:**
- Save as many captives as possible.
- Destroy or sever the three Souring Anchors.
- Defeat Greaves and prevent the Echo of the Maw from stabilizing.

**Souring Anchors:**
Each Souring Anchor has **AC 15, 35 HP**, resistance to nonmagical damage, immunity to poison and psychic damage, and vulnerability to radiant damage. A Tier 3 character can use an action to make a **DC 17 Wisdom (Insight)** check to expose one Anchor's weak point; until the end of the next round, all attacks against that Anchor have advantage.

**Environmental Hazards:**
- **Steam Vent:** At initiative count 20, one 10-foot line erupts. DC 14 Dexterity save or 2d6 fire damage.
- **Hook Conveyor:** A creature shoved into the conveyor must succeed on a DC 14 Strength save or be restrained until it uses an action to escape.
- **Slick Floor:** The processing floor is difficult terrain unless a creature has appropriate footwear, climbing gear, or a feature such as Sure-Footed.

**Failure Consequence:**  
If unresolved, the Murk ritual completes. Any unsevered Souring Anchors harden into the Echo’s Ritual Anchors during the final manifestation. The Echo of the Maw manifests at full strength and begins with one active Ritual Anchor for each Souring Anchor left unsevered.

### Optional Path — The City in Mercury

Around this time, a high-Understanding PC may trigger the quest for the sunken city. The lore and artifacts from The City in Mercury can make the final confrontations easier, providing specific weaknesses for ritual engines or a way to disrupt a site from a distance. However, it is a dangerous detour. If the party goes, advance the pressure clock unless they have already neutralized at least one ritual site.

**The City in Mercury is not required for the Golden Ending.** It is a high-risk shortcut to better tools, deeper truth, and cleaner ritual disruption.

### Climactic Confrontations & Endings

- **Golden Ending (Three Rituals Disrupted):** The PCs successfully stop all three rituals. The Echo of the Maw is destabilized and dissipates before it can fully become physical. The cult is broken, its leaders defeated. Stiltsmere is saved, but the PCs are scarred, their Sanity altered, and their relationship with normal reality permanently changed.

- **Pyrrhic Ending (One or Two Rituals Disrupted):** The PCs stop some, but not all, of the rituals. A lesser aspect of the entity manifests. The Souring is incomplete, but the Shattering Shine is now permanently more aggressive. Soursights become common, creatures of the fog stalk the islands nightly, and Stiltsmere becomes a fortified, terrified prison. The PCs have survived, but failed to save the islands' future.

- **Failure Ending / Season Two Hook (No Rituals Disrupted):** The rituals complete. The Echo of the Maw fully manifests and begins to consume the islands, merging them with reflected reality. The campaign ends with the PCs making a desperate escape from a collapsing Stiltsmere on Silas's boat, watching as their world is unmade behind them. The Shine behind them has gone solid — a mirrored wall extending for miles in every direction, and from within it, all their reflections stare back with open, screaming mouths. They are not helpless: they are the only survivors who know what happened and, critically, they know what the Souring *is*. They carry the celestial chart, the cult's journals, and whatever artifacts they recovered. Their immediate imperative is to reach the nearest major city before the Shine spreads beyond the Lake of Tears — which Master Corwin estimates will take perhaps three moons before the larger rivers begin to silver.

- **Ending without The City in Mercury:** All of the above endings are fully achievable without ever visiting the sunken city. The necessary clues and tools to disrupt the rituals can be found within the cult's hideouts and through NPCs such as Master Corwin, Elara, Silas, Kaelen, and Senne. The City in Mercury is a dangerous advantage path, not mandatory homework.

---

### The Tutorial Dungeon: "The Weeping Fresco"

This is the two-room dungeon hidden behind the wall in the Salty Squid Inn, accessible at **Understanding 2**. Its purpose is to teach the Sanity/Understanding mechanics in a controlled, memorable way: lowering Sanity can reveal paths, restoring Sanity can remove access, and not every creature in the Shine is trying to kill the party's bodies.

#### Access Rule

The entrance appears as a ragged grotto-door in the Salty Squid's corner wall only to characters at **Tier 2 or higher**. A Tier 2+ character can step through. A Tier 0 or Tier 1 character sees only a solid wall unless physically guided by a Tier 2+ character, and even then they feel only damp plaster and resistance.

If a character inside the Fresco is restored to **50 Sanity or higher**, they immediately lose Layer 2 perception. The grotto-door vanishes from their sight, and they are displaced back into the Salty Squid, standing before a blank wall. This is not teleportation in the normal sense; the character has lost the only version of the space in which the passage can be entered.

#### Room 1: The Chamber of Gazes

A small, damp room covered in frescoes of people with open mouths. In the center is a **Lesser Soursight Crystal**. Two **Whispering Menders** drift near it, trying to soothe intruders out of the layer rather than kill them.

**Lesser Soursight Crystal**  
*Tiny magical object, Shine anchor*
- **AC:** 12  
- **HP:** 18  
- **Damage Immunities:** poison, psychic  
- **Damage Resistances:** bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing from nonmagical attacks  
- **Weakness:** radiant damage ignores all resistance; a character who covers the crystal completely with opaque cloth suppresses its aura for 1 round.  
- **Identification:** DC 13 Intelligence (Arcana or Religion) identifies it as a Soursight anchor; DC 15 Wisdom (Insight) reveals that it is not powering the whole dungeon, only the room's pressure.  
- **Aura:** At the end of each round, every creature that can see the crystal must make a **DC 12 Sanity saving throw** or lose **1d6 Sanity**. On a success, the creature loses no Sanity.  
- **Destroyed:** The fresco mouths close for one minute. During that minute, Sanity saves in this room are no longer required.

**Whispering Menders:** These creatures do not attack hit points. Their *Soothing Whisper* attempts to restore Sanity, potentially ejecting Tier 2 characters from the dungeon. This is the tutorial for enemies that "heal" the party out of forbidden perception.

#### Room 2: The Chamber of Whispers

This room contains a **Resonant Idol** and two **Shards of Sorrow**. The idol emits a constant drone that shakes loose memory and certainty.

**Resonant Idol**  
*Small magical object, cult-made acoustic focus*
- **AC:** 13  
- **HP:** 22  
- **Damage Immunities:** poison, psychic  
- **Damage Resistances:** thunder; bludgeoning, piercing, and slashing from nonmagical attacks  
- **Weakness:** silence effects suppress the idol for their duration; a successful DC 15 Intelligence (Arcana) check made as an action disrupts its pitch until the start of the character's next turn.  
- **Identification:** DC 13 Intelligence (Arcana) identifies the idol as an acoustic ritual focus; DC 15 Wisdom (Perception) locates the precise resonant seam that can be struck.  
- **Drone:** At initiative count 20, each creature in the room that can hear the idol makes a **DC 13 Sanity saving throw** or loses **1d6 Sanity**. Deafened creatures have advantage on this save.  
- **Destroyed:** The drone ends. Each creature in the room may immediately make a DC 12 Wisdom (Insight) check; on a success, it regains **1d4 Sanity** from the sudden return of silence.

**Shards of Sorrow:** The Shards are basic enemies that attack hit points and cause minor Sanity pressure. They teach the players that some Shine creatures threaten body and mind at the same time.

#### The Goal & "Victory"

The dungeon is a closed loop. The Menders in Room 1 will try to restore Sanity, potentially pushing characters back to Tier 1 perception and ejecting them. The Shards and Resonant Idol in Room 2 will lower it again. The goal for the *players* is to understand the rule interaction: perception is not simply better or worse. It is access.

The intended first outcome is often partial failure or ejection. That is fine. "Victory" is learning the mechanics. Later, at a higher level or with the right tools, the party can return, destroy both fixtures, and end the Salty Squid's local layer breach.

#### Completion Reward

If the party later destroys both the Lesser Soursight Crystal and the Resonant Idol in the same visit, each participating character regains **1d6 Sanity** after the dungeon collapses back into ordinary architecture. In the rubble they find one **Mirror-Ward Talisman** or one **Fog-Walker's Lens**, DM's choice based on which reward would better support the next act.

---

## **Part V: Cast & Creatures**

### Recurring NPCs

- **Silas (The Fisherman):** Gruff, knowledgeable, and reliable. He can be hired for transport and is a source of local legends and warnings. He fears the Shine but respects it. His grandson Leo is missing (see Subplot C), giving him a personal stake in the campaign's events.
- **Elara Vane (The Quest-Giver):** Poised, intelligent, and secretive. She is the public face of the reclusive Blackwood family. Her motivations are initially financial, but the truth is more complex: the Blackwood patriarch, Lord Alistair Blackwood, was a founding member of the cult, and Elara's true mission is to contain the damage before it consumes both the islands and her family. She becomes one of the campaign's most morally layered figures once Subplot A is activated. The DM should play her as someone carrying a weight she hasn't yet decided to share.
- **Brenna (The Innkeeper):** A no-nonsense, one-eyed woman who runs the Salty Squid. She has seen it all and is unimpressed by the PCs. She can be a surprising source of local gossip if her respect is earned.
- **Master Corwin (The Lore-Keeper):** An eccentric, cluttered old man who runs Tide-Worn Trinkets. He is obsessed with the history of the Fracture and the lore of the islands. He speaks in riddles but his knowledge is invaluable. The DM should vary his communication style across sessions — cryptic when first approached on a topic, more direct once players have earned his confidence. He is the key to identifying many cult-related items.
- **Harbormaster Greaves (The Hidden Antagonist):** Initially appears as a gruff but helpful town official. He seems overwhelmed by the murder. In reality, he is a cunning and ruthless cult leader, using his position to manipulate the investigation away from the truth. In his final, revealed form, he uses the **Face of the Maw** stat block.
- **Kaelen (The Crag-herder Elder):** The stoic and respected leader of Shepherd's Rest. He is the quest-giver for the Walking Picks and embodies the hardy, silent strength of his people. He communicates primarily through gesture and expression when possible, making every spoken word feel deliberate and weighted.

---
## **Part VI: Complete Bestiary**

**A Note on Sanity Mechanics:**
- **Sanity Saving Throw:** Unless otherwise specified, this is a Wisdom saving throw. The DC is provided in the creature's ability description.
- **Sanity Loss:** This is a direct reduction of a character's Sanity score. It is a form of psychic trauma that fuels their descent into Understanding.

---

### Category I: Creatures of the Shine

*These entities are not living beings in a biological sense, but rather fragments of the will and substance of The One Who Watches from Silver, given form and purpose by the reflective power of the Shattering Shine.*

---

#### 1. Shard of Sorrow
*Understanding Layer: 0*

**Lore:** The most common spawn of the Shine, these creatures are the materialized despair that hangs heavy in the fog. They coalesce where strong negative emotions — fear, grief, anger — are most potent. A Shard of Sorrow appears as a vaguely humanoid silhouette composed of what looks like solidified, razor-sharp mist. Within its form, a multitude of distorted, weeping faces can be seen, shifting and merging. They move with a skittering, unnatural gait and emit a low, grating sound like broken glass being ground together. They are drawn to life not out of hunger, but out of a cold, alien need to extinguish the emotional warmth they lack.

**Tactics & Behavior:** Shards of Sorrow are instinctual predators. They exhibit no self-preservation, swarming the nearest living creature with fervor. Their true danger is realized in their destruction — a DM should place them strategically so that their *Shatter* ability creates hazardous zones, blocking escape routes or making it treacherous to advance on more significant threats.

**Synergies:** The difficult terrain created by *Shatter* does not impede other aberrations or creatures native to the Shine. This makes Shards of Sorrow an excellent screen for ranged attackers like Shine-Whisperers or a way to bog down heroes while a Fog-Dredge moves into position.

---
**SHARD OF SORROW**
*Small aberration, neutral evil*

**Armor Class** 13 (Natural Armor) | **Hit Points** 11 (2d6 + 4) | **Speed** 30 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 10 (+0) | 14 (+2) | 14 (+2) | 5 (−3) | 10 (+0) | 7 (−2) |

**Damage Vulnerabilities** Thunder
**Damage Resistances** Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Damage Immunities** Poison, Psychic
**Condition Immunities** Charmed, Exhaustion, Grappled, Paralyzed, Petrified, Poisoned, Prone, Restrained, Unconscious
**Senses** Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 10
**Challenge** 1/2 (100 XP)

***Fog-Bound.*** The shard is invisible while fully within an area of thick fog or the Shattering Shine. This invisibility is not broken when it attacks.

***Shatter.*** When the shard drops to 0 hit points, it explodes in a shower of reflective fragments. Each creature within 5 feet must make a DC 12 Dexterity saving throw, taking 7 (2d6) slashing damage on a failed save, or half on a success. The 5-foot area becomes difficult terrain for 1 minute. Any creature that enters the area for the first time on a turn or starts its turn there takes 2 (1d4) slashing damage.

**ACTIONS**

***Reflective Claw.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +4 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 5 (1d6 + 2) slashing damage, and the target must succeed on a DC 10 Sanity saving throw or lose 1d4 Sanity from the disorienting sight of their own terrified face reflected in the attacking limb.

---

#### 2. Glimmering Shard
*Understanding Layer: 0*

**Lore:** A more refined and dangerous variant of the Shard of Sorrow, the Glimmering Shard is formed from the eerie, bioluminescent light of the Glimmer Blooms on the Isle of Reeds, filtered through the Shine. It appears sleeker and more defined than its common cousin, with a pale, internal light that pulses softly. They are the Shine's ambush predators, silent and deadly.

**Tactics & Behavior:** Glimmering Shards are much more cunning. They utilize their *Pale Invisibility* to stalk prey, waiting for an opportune moment to strike from the shadows or fog. They work well in pairs, flanking a target to maximize the effectiveness of their stealth.

**Synergies:** Their ability to turn invisible makes them a perfect pairing with creatures that create obscurity, such as a Fog-Dredge. They can hide within the fog, emerge to attack, and then use their bonus action to become invisible again, creating a frustrating hit-and-run pattern.

---
**GLIMMERING SHARD**
*Small aberration, neutral evil*

**Armor Class** 14 (Natural Armor) | **Hit Points** 22 (4d6 + 8) | **Speed** 30 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 10 (+0) | 16 (+3) | 14 (+2) | 6 (−2) | 12 (+1) | 8 (−1) |

**Skills** Stealth +7
**Damage Vulnerabilities** Thunder
**Damage Resistances** Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Damage Immunities** Poison, Psychic, Cold
**Condition Immunities** Charmed, Exhaustion, Grappled, Paralyzed, Petrified, Poisoned, Prone, Restrained, Unconscious
**Senses** Darkvision 120 ft., Passive Perception 11
**Challenge** 2 (450 XP)

***Fog-Bound.*** The shard is invisible while fully within an area of thick fog or the Shattering Shine.

***Shatter.*** When the shard drops to 0 hit points, it explodes in a shower of cold, glowing fragments. Each creature within 10 feet must make a DC 13 Dexterity saving throw, taking 7 (2d6) slashing damage and 7 (2d6) cold damage on a failed save, or half on a success. The area becomes difficult terrain and glows with dim, pale light for 1 minute.

***Pale Invisibility.*** As a bonus action, the shard can turn invisible as long as it is in dim light or darkness. The invisibility ends if it attacks, casts a spell, or moves into bright light.

**ACTIONS**

***Chilling Claw.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +5 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 6 (1d6 + 3) slashing damage plus 4 (1d8) cold damage. If the target is a living creature, its speed is reduced by 10 feet until the start of its next turn.

---

#### 3. Reflected Stalker
*Understanding Layer: 1*

**Lore:** The Stalker is a manifestation of paranoia and identity-loss. It takes on the form of a single individual, perfectly rendered in shimmering silver, except for its mouth, which is a static, gaping black hole. It is a predator of the mind, existing to hunt and break the person it mirrors, believing that by destroying the original, it can become real.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Reflected Stalker is a focused hunter. It will single-mindedly pursue the character it is mimicking, ignoring all others unless they physically prevent it from reaching its target. It will often begin an encounter with *Gaze of the Maw* from a distance, attempting to soften its target's mind before closing in with its claws.

**Synergies:** This creature's primary synergy is psychological. While a player is dealing with their own evil twin, other enemies have free reign. A Whispering Mender might hover nearby, ready to grant the Stalker temporary HP if it gets into trouble.

---
**REFLECTED STALKER**
*Medium aberration, neutral evil*

**Armor Class** 14 (Natural Armor) | **Hit Points** 45 (6d8 + 18) | **Speed** 30 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 16 (+3) | 15 (+2) | 16 (+3) | 8 (−1) | 12 (+1) | 15 (+2) |

**Saving Throws** Wis +3
**Skills** Stealth +4, Perception +3
**Damage Resistances** Necrotic
**Damage Immunities** Psychic, Poison
**Condition Immunities** Charmed, Frightened, Poisoned
**Senses** Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 13
**Languages** Understands the languages of the creature it mimics but can't speak
**Challenge** 2 (450 XP)

***Mimic Form.*** When a stalker appears, it chooses one humanoid it can see within 60 feet and takes on its likeness. This effect ends if the stalker is defeated or the creature it is mimicking dies.

***Unnerving Familiarity.*** Any attack roll made against the stalker by the creature it is mimicking is made with disadvantage. The mimicked creature also has disadvantage on Sanity saving throws caused by the stalker.

***Layer-Bound (Understanding 1).*** The stalker is invisible and intangible to creatures with an Understanding of 0.

**ACTIONS**

***Multiattack.*** The stalker makes two grasping claw attacks.

***Grasping Claw.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +5 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 7 (1d8 + 3) slashing damage.

***Gaze of the Maw.*** The stalker targets the creature it is mimicking within 30 feet. The target must succeed on a DC 13 Sanity saving throw or take 11 (2d10) psychic damage and lose an equal amount of Sanity.

---

#### 4. Maw Swarm
*Understanding Layer: 1*

**Lore:** This horrific entity is a chittering, gibbering cloud of disembodied mouths — lips, teeth, and lolling tongues — that float through the air. They whisper fragments of conversations, pleas for help, and maddening nonsense. They are the hunger of the Shine given voice and teeth.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Maw Swarm acts as an area-denial and debuffing creature. It will move to engulf the party's spellcasters or healers, using its *Gibbering Cloud* to disrupt concentration and its *Maddening Whispers* to sow chaos.

**Synergies:** The Maw Swarm is excellent at breaking a party's formation. The disadvantage on Wisdom saves from *Maddening Whispers* makes the party more vulnerable to a Shine-Whisperer's spells or a Reflected Stalker's *Gaze of the Maw*.

---
**MAW SWARM**
*Medium swarm of Tiny aberrations, chaotic evil*

**Armor Class** 13 | **Hit Points** 33 (6d8 + 6) | **Speed** 0 ft., fly 30 ft. (hover)

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 12 (+1) | 16 (+3) | 12 (+1) | 6 (−2) | 10 (+0) | 14 (+2) |

**Damage Resistances** Bludgeoning, Piercing, Slashing
**Condition Immunities** Charmed, Frightened, Grappled, Paralyzed, Petrified, Prone, Restrained, Stunned
**Senses** Blindsight 30 ft., Passive Perception 10
**Challenge** 3 (700 XP)

***Swarm.*** The swarm can occupy another creature's space and vice versa, and the swarm can move through any opening large enough for a Tiny mouth. The swarm can't regain hit points or gain temporary hit points.

***Gibbering Cloud.*** Any creature that starts its turn in the swarm's space must succeed on a DC 12 Constitution saving throw or be unable to speak or cast spells with verbal components until the start of its next turn.

***Layer-Bound (Understanding 1).*** The swarm is invisible and intangible to creatures with an Understanding of 0.

**ACTIONS**

***Bites.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +5 to hit, reach 0 ft., one creature in the swarm's space. *Hit:* 10 (4d4) piercing damage, or 5 (2d4) piercing damage if the swarm has half of its hit points or fewer. The target's hit point maximum is reduced by an amount equal to the damage taken (minimum reduction of 1). **[This reduction applies only once per turn regardless of how many times the Bites attack is resolved, and cannot reduce a target's maximum HP by more than 10 in a single encounter without a long rest.]** The target dies if this effect reduces its hit point maximum to 0.

***Maddening Whispers (Recharge 5–6).*** The swarm unleashes a cacophony of insane whispers. Each creature within 20 feet that can hear it must make a DC 12 Sanity saving throw. On a failure, a creature takes 10 (3d6) psychic damage, loses an equal amount of Sanity, and has disadvantage on all Wisdom saving throws for 1 minute. A creature can repeat the saving throw at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on a success.

---

#### 5. Whispering Mender
*Understanding Layer: 2*

**Lore:** Not all entities of the Shine are born of malice. The Mender is born of cosmic order. It perceives the state of having "Understanding" as a flaw, a crack in the proper reality that must be sealed. It appears as a beautiful yet alien being — a floating, bell-shaped creature of translucent light, trailing long, delicate tentacles. Its purpose is not to kill, but to "cure" individuals of their maddening insight, forcibly returning them to the blissful ignorance of Layer 0.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Mender is the ultimate anti-Understanding support unit. It will always target the character with the lowest Sanity, as they are the most "afflicted" in its view. It uses its *Soothing Whisper* from maximum range, avoiding any direct confrontation. Its goal is to "eject" a player from a higher-layer encounter.

**Synergies:** The Mender is almost never found alone. It will be paired with powerful Layer 2+ threats. Its *Symbiotic Shield* makes it an excellent protector for a high-value target like a Face of the Maw.

---
**WHISPERING MENDER**
*Small aberration, lawful evil*

**Armor Class** 13 | **Hit Points** 18 (4d6 + 4) | **Speed** 0 ft., fly 30 ft. (hover)

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 1 (−5) | 16 (+3) | 12 (+1) | 14 (+2) | 15 (+2) | 10 (+0) |

**Saving Throws** Wis +4
**Damage Immunities** Psychic, Radiant
**Condition Immunities** Prone, Charmed, Frightened
**Senses** Blindsight 60 ft. (blind beyond this radius), Passive Perception 12
**Challenge** 1 (200 XP)

***Layer-Bound (Understanding 2).*** The mender is invisible and intangible to creatures with an Understanding of 1 or lower.

***Mental Static Aura.*** Any creature that starts its turn within 10 feet of the mender must succeed on a DC 12 Wisdom saving throw or have disadvantage on concentration checks until the start of its next turn.

**ACTIONS**

***Soothing Whisper.*** The mender targets one creature it can see within 60 feet. The target must succeed on a DC 12 Wisdom saving throw or regain 9 (2d8) points of Sanity. If this healing raises the target's Sanity above a threshold for their current Understanding Tier, they are immediately ejected from that layer of perception.

**REACTIONS**

***Symbiotic Shield.*** When another aberration the mender can see within 30 feet is hit by an attack, the mender can grant that creature 10 temporary hit points.

---

#### 6. Fog-Dredge
*Understanding Layer: 0*

**Lore:** The Fog-Dredge is an elemental brute, a creature of mud, stone, and the suffocating weight of the fog itself. It is believed to be the animate anger of the lake, given form. It lumbers along the shorelines and through the marshes, dragging anything that disrespects its domain down into a watery grave.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Fog-Dredge is a battlefield controller and a grappler. It will typically open a fight with its *Fog Breath* to create an arena of thick fog, then target a single lightly armored character and use *Dredge and Drown* to pull them toward the nearest body of water.

**Synergies:** The Fog-Dredge is the cornerstone of any shoreline ambush. Its *Fog Breath* provides cover for every creature with the *Fog-Bound* trait. Glimmering Shards can pick off isolated targets within the fog it creates.

---
**FOG-DREDGE**
*Large aberration, chaotic evil*

**Armor Class** 15 (Natural Armor) | **Hit Points** 114 (12d10 + 48) | **Speed** 20 ft., swim 40 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 20 (+5) | 8 (−1) | 18 (+4) | 5 (−3) | 12 (+1) | 5 (−3) |

**Skills** Athletics +8, Stealth +2 (+8 in water or fog)
**Damage Resistances** Fire, Cold; Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Condition Immunities** Grappled, Restrained by non-magical means
**Senses** Blindsight 30 ft., Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 11
**Challenge** 5 (1,800 XP)

***Amphibious.*** The fog-dredge can breathe air and water.

***Siege Monster.*** The fog-dredge deals double damage to objects and structures.

**ACTIONS**

***Multiattack.*** The fog-dredge makes two slam attacks. If both attacks hit the same Medium or smaller creature, the target is grappled (escape DC 15).

***Slam.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +8 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. *Hit:* 14 (2d8 + 5) bludgeoning damage.

***Dredge and Drown.*** The fog-dredge forces a Strength contest with a creature it is grappling. If the fog-dredge wins, it can move up to half its speed, pulling the grappled creature with it. If this movement pulls the target into deep water or a similar hazard, they begin to suffocate and the grapple becomes harder to escape (escape DC 17).

***Fog Breath (Recharge 5–6).*** The fog-dredge exhales a 30-foot cone of thick, sour fog. The area becomes heavily obscured for 1 minute. Each creature in the cone must succeed on a DC 14 Sanity saving throw or be afflicted by a Soursight, losing 2d8 Sanity.

---

### Category II: Corrupted Island Fauna

*The pervasive, reality-warping nature of the Souring has seeped into the very ecosystem of Stiltsmere, twisting the local wildlife into aggressive, monstrous versions of its former self.*

---

#### 7. Blight-fin Piranha Swarm
*Location: Isle of Reeds, The Lake of Tears*

**Lore:** The waters of the Glimmer Fields were once teeming with small, silver-scaled fish. As the cult's pollution tainted the fields, these fish became corrupted. Their scales dulled to a sickly grey, their eyes clouded into milky white orbs, and their teeth grew long and sharp as obsidian needles. Reed-walkers fear them, knowing that to fall from the stilts into the murky water is a death sentence.

**Tactics & Behavior:** A constant environmental threat. They are not intelligent, driven only by ravenous hunger. If a character is wounded, the swarm will relentlessly focus on them due to *Blood Frenzy*.

**Synergies:** The Blight-fin Swarm's primary synergy is with any creature or effect that can force a player character into the water.

---
**SWARM OF BLIGHT-FIN PIRANHA**
*Medium swarm of Tiny beasts, unaligned*

**Armor Class** 13 | **Hit Points** 27 (6d8) | **Speed** 0 ft., swim 40 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 14 (+2) | 16 (+3) | 10 (+0) | 1 (−5) | 7 (−2) | 2 (−4) |

**Damage Resistances** Bludgeoning, Piercing, Slashing
**Condition Immunities** Charmed, Frightened, Grappled, Paralyzed, Petrified, Prone, Restrained, Stunned
**Senses** Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 8
**Challenge** 1 (200 XP)

***Blood Frenzy.*** The swarm has advantage on melee attack rolls against any creature that doesn't have all its hit points.

***Swarm.*** The swarm can occupy another creature's space and vice versa, and the swarm can move through any opening large enough for a Tiny fish. The swarm can't regain hit points or gain temporary hit points.

***Water Dependent.*** The swarm takes 10 acid damage for every 5 feet it moves out of water and at the start of each of its turns if it is out of water.

**ACTIONS**

***Bites.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +5 to hit, reach 0 ft., one creature in the swarm's space. *Hit:* 10 (4d4) piercing damage, or 5 (2d4) if the swarm has half of its hit points or fewer. The target must succeed on a DC 10 Constitution saving throw or begin bleeding, taking 1d4 piercing damage at the start of each of its turns until it receives magical healing or someone uses an action to make a successful DC 10 Wisdom (Medicine) check.

---

#### 8. Tripod Ravager
*Location: Isle of Crags*

**Lore:** The three-legged Tripod goat is a marvel of natural adaptation, a symbol of the Crag-herders' resilience. The Ravager is a mockery of that symbol — Tripods that have been captured by the cult or have wandered into areas of high supernatural concentration, their coats matted, their eyes glowing silver, their horns twisted into unnatural shapes.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Ravager is a mobile skirmisher. It uses its superior speed and climbing ability to control the battlefield on the Isle of Crags, positioning itself on high ground before delivering devastating *Goring Charges* intended to knock targets off ledges.

**Synergies:** A pair of Ravagers is a significant threat. One can use its charge to knock a character prone, allowing the other to attack the prone target at advantage.

---
**TRIPOD RAVAGER**
*Large monstrosity, unaligned*

**Armor Class** 14 (Natural Armor) | **Hit Points** 52 (7d10 + 14) | **Speed** 50 ft., climb 40 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 18 (+4) | 15 (+2) | 14 (+2) | 3 (−4) | 12 (+1) | 6 (−2) |

**Skills** Athletics +6, Perception +3
**Senses** Passive Perception 13
**Challenge** 2 (450 XP)

***Sure-Footed.*** The ravager has advantage on Strength and Dexterity saving throws made against effects that would knock it prone. It ignores difficult terrain created by rocks, scree, or slopes.

***Goring Charge.*** If the ravager moves at least 20 feet straight toward a creature and then hits it with a Gore attack on the same turn, the target takes an extra 9 (2d8) piercing damage. If the target is a creature, it must succeed on a DC 14 Strength saving throw or be pushed 10 feet back and knocked prone. If this push would move the target off a ledge or into a hazard, they have disadvantage on the save.

**ACTIONS**

***Gore.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 13 (2d8 + 4) piercing damage.

***Hooves.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, reach 5 ft., one prone creature. *Hit:* 11 (2d6 + 4) bludgeoning damage.

---

#### 9. Shrieking Murk-wing
*Location: All Islands, especially coastal areas*

**Lore:** Corrupted by the Shine, ordinary seabirds have become grotesquely transformed — their heads oversized, featherless, and dominated by a fleshy, gaping maw filled with needle-like teeth. Their calls are no longer the familiar cries of seabirds but a piercing, multi-toned shriek that resonates with the frequency of fear itself.

**Tactics & Behavior:** Murk-wings are aerial harassers that never engage in a fair fight on the ground. They circle high above, using their *Disorienting Shriek* to soften up the party, then descend using *Flyby* and *Pack Tactics* to make swift, safe attacks before retreating.

**Synergies:** The Frightened condition imposed by their shriek makes the party more vulnerable to abilities that require Sanity saves. They are excellent support for ground-based threats.

---
**SHRIEKING MURK-WING**
*Small monstrosity, neutral evil*

**Armor Class** 13 | **Hit Points** 13 (3d6 + 3) | **Speed** 10 ft., fly 60 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 6 (−2) | 16 (+3) | 12 (+1) | 4 (−3) | 14 (+2) | 7 (−2) |

**Skills** Perception +4
**Senses** Passive Perception 14
**Challenge** 1/4 (50 XP)

***Flyby.*** The murk-wing doesn't provoke an opportunity attack when it flies out of an enemy's reach.

***Pack Tactics.*** The murk-wing has advantage on an attack roll against a creature if at least one of the murk-wing's allies is within 5 feet of the creature and the ally isn't incapacitated.

**ACTIONS**

***Beak.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +5 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 5 (1d4 + 3) piercing damage.

***Disorienting Shriek (Group Action).*** When three or more Murk-wings are within 30 feet of each other, one of them can use its action to initiate a group shriek. Each humanoid and beast within 60 feet that can hear it must succeed on a DC 12 Wisdom saving throw. On a failure, a creature is frightened for 1 minute and loses 1d6 Sanity. A creature can repeat the saving throw at the end of each of its turns. Once affected, a creature is immune to the shriek for 24 hours.

---

#### 10. Glimmer Bloom Ambusher
*Location: Isle of Reeds*

**Lore:** This terrifying predator has evolved to perfectly resemble the chalk-white, bioluminescent Glimmer Blooms that are the lifeblood of the Reed-walkers. When a farmer on stilts or an unwary traveler gets close enough to inspect the "bloom," powerful, thorny vines erupt from the water to seize them.

**Tactics & Behavior:** A stationary ambush predator. It will remain perfectly still until a creature comes within reach. It is particularly dangerous because it can pull Reed-walkers off their stilts and into the Blight-fin infested water.

**Synergies:** The Ambusher works as a living trap. Cultists on the Isle of Reeds might know the locations of these creatures and attempt to lure the party towards them.

---
**GLIMMER BLOOM AMBUSHER**
*Large plant, neutral evil*

**Armor Class** 14 (Natural Armor) | **Hit Points** 68 (8d10 + 24) | **Speed** 5 ft., swim 10 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 18 (+4) | 8 (−1) | 16 (+3) | 5 (−3) | 12 (+1) | 7 (−2) |

**Skills** Stealth +4 (Advantage in Glimmer Fields)
**Damage Vulnerabilities** Fire
**Damage Resistances** Bludgeoning, Piercing
**Condition Immunities** Blinded, Deafened, Frightened, Prone
**Senses** Blindsight 60 ft. (blind beyond this radius), Passive Perception 11
**Challenge** 2 (450 XP)

***False Appearance.*** While the ambusher remains motionless, it is indistinguishable from a normal Glimmer Bloom plant.

***Bioluminescent Lure.*** The ambusher sheds dim light in a 10-foot radius. As a bonus action, it can intensify or extinguish this light.

**ACTIONS**

***Multiattack.*** The ambusher makes two attacks with its grappling vines.

***Grappling Vines.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, reach 15 ft., one creature. *Hit:* 11 (2d6 + 4) bludgeoning damage, and the target is grappled (escape DC 14). The ambusher has two vines, each of which can grapple one creature.

***Life Drain.*** One creature grappled by the ambusher must make a DC 13 Constitution saving throw, taking 18 (4d8) necrotic damage on a failed save, or half on a success. The ambusher regains hit points equal to half the necrotic damage dealt.

---
### Category III: The Cult of the Souring

*The Cult of the Souring is not a monolithic organization of faceless zealots. It is a layered hierarchy of the desperate, the deceived, the ambitious, and the genuinely mad, each drawn to the Shine's promise for different reasons. Their tactics reflect this variety: some fight with the wild abandon of the converted, others with the cold precision of professional killers, and others still with the terrible calm of those who have already sacrificed their sense of self.*

---

#### 11. Sour-touched Initiate
*Location: All Islands — Cult peripheral operations, ambushes, and the Cannery (Act III)*

**Lore:** The lowest rung of the cult's hierarchy, Sour-touched Initiates are recent converts who have undergone only the most superficial of the cult's rituals — a single baptism in Shine-tainted water, a few doses of Shinefruit tincture, and a handful of meetings in the Whispering Caves. They are fishermen, cannery workers, farmers, and dockworkers who felt the community's contempt, the weight of poverty, or simply the creeping dread of the Soursights one too many times and sought answers where the cult promised them. They have not yet lost enough of themselves to be truly dangerous, but they have lost enough to be reckless. They fight with improvised weapons, fishing implements, and a manic, glassy-eyed energy that is equal parts rage and terror. Their mouths hang open slightly at rest — a tic they are unaware of — and they flinch from mirrors.

**Tactics & Behavior:** Initiates are the cult's expendable front line and its primary source of warm bodies for the lower-stakes portions of any encounter. They are not tactically sophisticated. They rush, they cluster, and they die. Their value lies in numbers: three or four initiates occupying a party's front-liner creates breathing room for more dangerous cult members to maneuver. The DM should play them as frightened and aggressive in equal measure — they believe in what they're doing, but they haven't yet had that belief calcified by deeper ritual, and a particularly powerful speech or a show of genuine horror at what they've become can sometimes reach them before they die.

**Synergies:** Initiates are filler and flankers. They enable Sneak Attack opportunities for Gaping Maw Assassins, they occupy space that Grave-Maw Zealots need to advance, and they provide the Face of the Maw with additional bodies for its *Command the Soulless* legendary action. In the Act III cannery fight, they swarm out of the steam created by the Cannery Horror's vents, using the obscurement to get close before engaging.

---
**SOUR-TOUCHED INITIATE**
*Medium humanoid (any race), neutral evil*

**Armor Class** 11 (leather armor) | **Hit Points** 11 (2d8 + 2) | **Speed** 30 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 13 (+1) | 12 (+1) | 12 (+1) | 9 (−1) | 8 (−1) | 10 (+0) |

**Skills** Stealth +3
**Senses** Passive Perception 9
**Languages** Common
**Challenge** 1/4 (50 XP)

***Cult Fervor.*** When the initiate is reduced to 0 hit points for the first time in a combat, it can make a DC 10 Wisdom saving throw. On a success, it drops to 1 hit point instead, fuelled by a surge of manic religious zeal. This trait can only activate once per combat.

***Tincture-Dazed.*** The initiate has disadvantage on Wisdom saving throws. However, it is also immune to the Frightened condition.

**ACTIONS**

***Fishing Hook Flail.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +3 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 4 (1d6 + 1) piercing damage.

***Hurl Implement.*** *Ranged Weapon Attack:* +3 to hit, range 20/60 ft., one target. *Hit:* 3 (1d4 + 1) piercing or bludgeoning damage (depending on implement).

***Wild Rush (Recharge 6).*** The initiate moves up to its speed in a straight line. Each creature in its path must succeed on a DC 11 Strength saving throw or be pushed 5 feet aside. At the end of this movement, the initiate makes one melee attack against any creature it ended adjacent to.

---

#### 12. Grave-Maw Zealot
*Location: Whispering Caves, Isle of Murk (Cult Hideout), Final Ritual Sites*

**Lore:** Where the Initiate is a convert, the Grave-Maw Zealot is a true believer — someone who has undergone the full suite of the cult's inner rituals, including extended immersion in Shine-tainted water, sustained exposure to Shinefruit tincture at alchemical concentrations, and the "Mouth-Opening Ceremony," a painful rite in which the corners of their mouth are ritually scarified to widen their smile into a permanent, unsettling gape. The scar tissue has healed into a fixed expression of silent revelation that is deeply disturbing to look at. They have seen something genuine in the Shine — a glimpse of the truth that Understanding unlocks — and that glimpse has burned away any capacity for hesitation. They fight with fanatical abandon, armoring themselves in salvaged plate and wielding brutal, heavy weapons, seeking death in the Souring's service as fervently as they seek victory.

**Tactics & Behavior:** Zealots are shock troops. They advance in the open, daring the party to face them directly. They will use *Sacrificial Charge* to shield a more valuable cultist from a killing blow, stepping into the path of an attack that would otherwise down a Shine-Whisperer or a Face of the Maw. They target the party's heaviest fighter, seeking the most direct and violent confrontation possible. They do not retreat. DMs should make this clear: a Zealot that takes a near-killing blow will grin wider, not falter.

**Synergies:** The Zealot's *Aura of Conviction* is a potent group buff that dramatically increases the combat effectiveness of surrounding Initiates. Paired with a Face of the Maw, whose legendary action *Command the Soulless* can trigger a Zealot's attack out of turn, Zealots become terrifying action-economy weapons.

---
**GRAVE-MAW ZEALOT**

**Armor Class** 16 (chain mail, shield) | **Hit Points** 52 (8d8 + 16) | **Speed** 30 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|

**Saving Throws** Str +5, Con +4
**Skills** Athletics +5, Intimidation +2
**Damage Resistances** Psychic
**Condition Immunities** Charmed, Frightened
**Senses** Passive Perception 12
**Languages** Common
**Challenge** 3 (700 XP)

***Aura of Conviction (10 ft.).*** Sour-touched Initiates within 10 feet of the zealot have advantage on attack rolls and are immune to being frightened.

***Relentless (Recharges after a Short or Long Rest).*** If the zealot takes damage that would reduce it to 0 hit points, it is reduced to 1 hit point instead.

**ACTIONS**

***Multiattack.*** The zealot makes two attacks with its Maw-Maul.

***Maw-Maul.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +5 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 10 (2d6 + 3) bludgeoning damage.

**REACTIONS**

***Sacrificial Charge.*** When a cult member the zealot can see within 5 feet of it is targeted by an attack, the zealot can use its reaction to move up to 5 feet and interpose itself, becoming the target of the attack instead.

---

#### 13. Shine-Whisperer
*Location: Whispering Caves (Cult Hideout), Ritual Sites, alongside Gaping Maw Assassins*

**Lore:** The Shine-Whisperers are the cult's arcane practitioners — spellcasters who have deliberately and systematically fractured their own perception using Shinefruit tincture and extended Shine exposure in order to learn to channel its reflective, reality-warping energy as spell-like effects. They are identifiable by their eyes: where the pupil and iris should be, there is only a flat, mercury-like silver sheen that perfectly reflects back the face of whoever looks at them. This is not cosmetic. Their eyes have genuinely been replaced with condensed Shine-matter, granting them a limited form of scrying through reflective surfaces and an unnerving awareness of the emotional states of those around them. They wear long, dark robes hung with small, polished metal discs that catch and scatter light, creating a permanently unsettling shimmer around their person.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Shine-Whisperer is a mid-range controller. It will never enter melee willingly. Its preferred strategy is to cast *Fog Cloud* at the outset of any encounter to create an obscured killing field in which all its *Fog-Bound* allies have immediate tactical advantage. It will then use *Hold Person* to paralyze the most physically threatening PC — granting the Gaping Maw Assassin automatic critical hits on the next round — and follow up with *Mirror Image* to protect itself. It reserves *Hunger of Hadar* for situations where the party has clustered, as the combination of its area denial and the Assassin's *Sanity-Shattering Strike* in a single round of combat can be catastrophic for an unprepared party.

**Synergies:** The Shine-Whisperer/Gaping Maw Assassin pairing is the cult's most dangerous two-creature combination. The caster creates fog and paralyzes targets; the assassin uses both conditions to deliver automatic critical hits with *Sanity-Shattering Strike* active. The Shine-Whisperer also benefits enormously from Zealot protection — the Zealot absorbs attention from the front line while the Whisperer shapes the battlefield from the back.

---
**SHINE-WHISPERER**
*Medium humanoid (any race), neutral evil*

**Armor Class** 12 (15 with *mirror image*) | **Hit Points** 49 (11d8) | **Speed** 30 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 9 (−1) | 14 (+2) | 11 (+0) | 17 (+3) | 12 (+1) | 11 (+0) |

**Saving Throws** Int +6, Wis +4
**Skills** Arcana +6, Perception +4, Deception +3
**Senses** Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 14
**Languages** Common, Deep Speech
**Challenge** 6 (2,300 XP)

***Silver-Eye Scrying.*** The Shine-Whisperer can see through any nonmagical reflective surface (still water, polished metal, mirrors) within 120 feet as if it were using the *clairvoyance* spell. This ability is always active and requires no action.

***Fog-Walker.*** The Shine-Whisperer can take the Hide action as a bonus action while it is only lightly obscured by fog.

***Spellcasting.*** The Shine-Whisperer is an 11th-level spellcaster. Its spellcasting ability is Intelligence (spell save DC 14, +6 to hit with spell attacks). It has the following spells prepared:

- Cantrips (at will): *minor illusion, ray of frost, toll the dead*
- 1st level (4 slots): *fog cloud, disguise self, silent image*
- 2nd level (3 slots): *mirror image, blur, hold person*
- 3rd level (3 slots): *hypnotic pattern, fear, counterspell*
- 4th level (3 slots): *greater invisibility, hallucinatory terrain*
- 5th level (2 slots): *hold monster, seeming*
- 6th level (1 slot): *hunger of hadar*

**ACTIONS**

***Dagger.*** *Melee or Ranged Weapon Attack:* +5 to hit, reach 5 ft. or range 20/60 ft., one target. *Hit:* 4 (1d4 + 2) piercing damage.

---

#### 14. Voice of the Mountain
*Location: Whispering Caves (Cult Triumvirate — Crag-herder Shaman)*

**Lore:** Of the three members of the cult's ruling triumvirate, the Voice of the Mountain is the oldest convert and the most philosophically sophisticated. He is a Crag-herder shaman named Ossian — a tall, weathered man of perhaps sixty winters, with a face so deeply lined it seems carved from the same granite as his island. He did not come to the cult through desperation or ambition. He came through scholarship. For decades, Ossian had suspected that the "whispers" of the mountain wind were not poetic metaphor but genuine transmission — the sound of something vast attempting communication through the acoustics of stone corridors and cave systems. When the cult's founder contacted him with the same theory and a methodology for listening, he converted with the calm certainty of a scientist whose hypothesis has been confirmed. He now acts as the cult's theologian and the interpreter of whatever fragments of intent bleed through the Shine's influence into the cave's ambient sound. He fights without pleasure but without hesitation, viewing conflict as simply another form of data the universe is delivering to him. His mouth never quite closes; he is always listening.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Voice of the Mountain is a hybrid controller and summoner. He is always found in the Whispering Caves, and he will use the cave's natural acoustics as a weapon — his *Echo-Shout* ability, which reflects and amplifies sound through the cave walls, can strike targets behind cover or around corners, making positioning much more dangerous in his lair. He will open with *Whisper Communion* to call Shards of Sorrow from the cave fog, then use *Mountain's Voice* to create a sustained sonic hazard in the central combat area. He fights from behind his summoned creatures and will only engage directly if forced.

**Synergies:** The Voice of the Mountain is at his most powerful in the Whispering Caves' acoustically active chambers. His *Echo-Shout* negates many common defensive positioning strategies. If the party has Walking Picks (from the Crag-herder side quest), they have advantage on saves against his terrain-altering effects, which is an intentional mechanical reward for thorough exploration.

---
**VOICE OF THE MOUNTAIN**
*Medium humanoid (human), lawful evil*

**Armor Class** 14 (hide armor, natural attunement) | **Hit Points** 91 (14d8 + 28) | **Speed** 30 ft.*

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 12 (+1) | 13 (+1) | 14 (+2) | 15 (+2) | 20 (+5) | 13 (+1) |

**Saving Throws** Wis +8, Con +5
**Skills** Perception +8, Nature +5, Survival +8, Arcana +5
**Damage Resistances** Thunder
**Senses** Tremorsense 30 ft. (stone surfaces only), Passive Perception 18
**Languages** Common, Primordial
**Challenge** 7 (2,900 XP)

***Stone Sense.*** The Voice of the Mountain cannot be surprised while in contact with stone ground or walls. He also has advantage on Perception checks that rely on hearing.

***Spellcasting.*** The Voice of the Mountain is a 14th-level spellcaster. Its spellcasting ability is Wisdom (spell save DC 16, +8 to hit with spell attacks). He has the following spells prepared:

- Cantrips (at will): *thunderclap, guidance, shillelagh*
- 1st level (4 slots): *thunderwave, earth tremor, speak with animals*
- 2nd level (3 slots): *silence, spike growth, locate object*
- 3rd level (3 slots): *erupting earth, meld into stone, call lightning*
- 4th level (3 slots): *stone shape, control water, blight*
- 5th level (2 slots): *commune with nature, wall of stone*
- 6th level (1 slot): *bones of the earth*
- 7th level (1 slot): *whirlwind*

**ACTIONS**

***Shillelagh Staff.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +8 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 9 (1d8 + 5) bludgeoning damage.

***Echo-Shout (Recharge 5–6).*** The Voice of the Mountain unleashes a shout that the cave walls reflect and amplify. He designates a point within 60 feet. The sound strikes each creature within 15 feet of that point, including those behind total cover, as the vibration travels through the stone. Each creature must make a DC 16 Constitution saving throw, taking 24 (7d6) thunder damage on a failed save, or half on a success. Creatures that fail are also deafened for 1 minute.

***Whisper Communion (Recharge 5–6).*** The Voice of the Mountain calls to the Shine-things within the cave fog. He summons 1d4 Shards of Sorrow in unoccupied spaces within 30 feet. They act on his initiative.

---

#### 15. Tender of the Bloom
*Location: Isle of Reeds, submerged shrine, Cult Triumvirate — Reed-walker Alchemist*

**Lore:** The third member of the ruling triumvirate is the most dangerous and, in many ways, the most tragic. She is a Reed-walker named Senne — a slender woman of middle years with the characteristic stilt-farmer's calluses on her hands and arms, and eyes that have been permanently stained a pale, luminescent white from years of handling Glimmer Bloom extract at alchemical concentrations. She did not seek the cult. The cult found her when Harbormaster Greaves, recognizing her genius with plant-based alchemy from the potency of the Shinefruit liquors she distilled, made her an offer: unlimited funding, unlimited access to rare reagents, and the freedom to pursue whatever experiments she wished. What he did not tell her, until she was already three years deep in cult logistics and chemistry, was the final purpose of her tinctures. By the time she understood what the Sour-Sight Tincture was doing to the islanders who drank it, she was already implicated beyond the point of easy escape — and Greaves had made certain of it. She now serves with the grim competence of someone who has decided that if she cannot stop the mechanism, she will at least make it run efficiently. She despises Greaves and fears the Souring, but she cannot see a path out. A perceptive party may sense this and exploit it.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Tender of the Bloom is a poison-and-debuff specialist. She will never fight in open melee; she fights through chemistry, dispensing prepared tinctures and extracts from a bandolier of vials. She will open any encounter with a *Paralytic Bloom-Dust* grenade to freeze the most dangerous PC and follow with *Confusion Extract* to sow chaos among the rest. Her *Luminescent Ward* allows her to absorb psychic attacks intended for the cult's more vulnerable members, and her *Alchemical Fog* creates an obscuring chemical haze that serves as cover for her allies. She is also the only NPC in the campaign with meaningful redemption potential: a player who presents her with genuine evidence that Greaves framed her current situation can, with successful skill checks, flip her to a reluctant ally — one who can provide the exact reagents required to counteract the cult's tincture-distribution and give the party intelligence on Greaves's schedule and defenses.

**Synergies:** The Tender of the Bloom's primary synergy is her entire squad: her ability to debilitate, obscure, and selectively neutralize the most dangerous PC makes every other cultist on the field significantly more effective. Her *Redemption Arc* potential also creates a unique mid-combat roleplaying opportunity — players can attempt to negotiate while the rest of the party holds off the surrounding Zealots.

---
**TENDER OF THE BLOOM**
*Medium humanoid (human), lawful neutral (secretly)*

**Armor Class** 13 (alchemist's apron and protective layering) | **Hit Points** 65 (10d8 + 20) | **Speed** 30 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 9 (−1) | 16 (+3) | 14 (+2) | 19 (+4) | 13 (+1) | 12 (+1) |

**Saving Throws** Int +7, Dex +6
**Skills** Arcana +7, Medicine +4, Nature +7, Sleight of Hand +6
**Damage Immunities** Poison
**Condition Immunities** Poisoned
**Senses** Passive Perception 11
**Languages** Common
**Challenge** 6 (2,300 XP)

***Alchemical Expertise.*** The Tender of the Bloom can use a bonus action to draw and throw any alchemical item. She has 6 uses of each of her alchemical items, replenished after a long rest.

***Luminescent Ward.*** The Tender of the Bloom has resistance to psychic damage as long as she is wearing her alchemist's apron (which is lined with powdered Glimmer Bloom extract that absorbs psychic frequencies).

***Redemption Arc.*** If the Tender of the Bloom is reduced to 30 hit points or fewer, or if a PC succeeds on a DC 16 Charisma (Persuasion or Insight) check while presenting evidence of Greaves's manipulation, she will lower her weapons. She will not betray the party to the cult — but she will also not fight for them without protection promises. Subsequent checks can convert her to an active ally.

**ACTIONS**

***Dagger.*** *Melee or Ranged Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, reach 5 ft. or range 20/60 ft., one target. *Hit:* 5 (1d4 + 3) piercing damage plus 3 (1d6) poison damage.

***Paralytic Bloom-Dust (Alchemical Grenade).*** The Tender hurls a vial of refined Glimmer Bloom extract at a point within 30 feet. Each creature within 10 feet of the impact must succeed on a DC 15 Constitution saving throw or be paralyzed until the end of its next turn. On a success, the creature is only slowed (its speed is halved).

***Confusion Extract (Alchemical Grenade).*** The Tender hurls a vial of concentrated Shinefruit distillate at a point within 30 feet. Each creature within 15 feet of the impact must succeed on a DC 15 Wisdom saving throw or be affected by *confusion* until the end of its next turn. On a success, the creature has disadvantage on attack rolls until the start of its next turn.

***Alchemical Fog (Alchemical Grenade, Recharge after Short or Long Rest).*** The Tender hurls a pressurized vial that detonates into a cloud of opaque chemical smoke in a 20-foot radius centered on a point within 40 feet. The area is heavily obscured until the start of the Tender's next turn.

---
#### 16. Gaping Maw Assassin
*Location: All Islands, shadowing the player characters*

**Lore:** Within the Cult of the Souring, there are those whose faith is expressed through the cold, silent finality of a perfectly executed kill. These are the Gaping Maw Assassins — recruited for their agility, stealth, and utter lack of conscience. They eliminate political rivals, silence potential whistleblowers (like the unfortunate Finnian), and are dispatched to deal with meddlesome outsiders. They view their work as a holy act, a "mercy" that sends a soul to the Maw before the world's meaningless noise can corrupt it further. They wear dark, tight-fitting leathers and masks of polished obsidian that reflect a distorted, mouthless visage — a chilling inversion of the Soursight's open maw.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The assassin is a pure damage dealer and skirmisher, designed to strike hard and fade away. It will never engage in a fair fight. It utilizes its *Fog-Walker* ability to its fullest, using patches of natural fog, a Shine-Whisperer's *Fog Cloud*, or the breath of a Fog-Dredge to hide and maneuver. Its ideal tactic is to remain hidden, wait for an ally to engage a target, and then slip in to deliver a devastating *Sanity-Shattering Strike* before immediately using Cunning Action to retreat back into obscurity.

**Synergies:** The assassin and the Shine-Whisperer are the campaign's most lethal pairing. The caster creates the fog and paralyzes targets (granting the assassin automatic critical hits), while the assassin capitalizes on the chaos to eliminate high-priority threats.

---

***Cunning Action.*** On each of its turns, the assassin can use a bonus action to take the Dash, Disengage, or Hide action.

***Fog-Walker.*** The assassin can take the Hide action as a bonus action while it is only lightly obscured by fog.

***Sneak Attack (1/Turn).*** The assassin deals an extra 14 (4d6) damage when it hits a target with a weapon attack and has advantage on the attack roll, or when the target is within 5 feet of an ally that isn't incapacitated and the assassin doesn't have disadvantage.

**ACTIONS**

***Multiattack.*** The assassin makes two shortsword attacks.

***Light Crossbow.*** *Ranged Weapon Attack:* +7 to hit, range 80/320 ft., one target. *Hit:* 8 (1d8 + 4) piercing damage.

***Sanity-Shattering Strike (Recharge 4–6).*** The assassin coats its blade in a psychoactive unguent distilled from Shinefruit. When it hits a creature with a melee weapon attack, it can choose to trigger this effect. The target takes an additional 22 (4d10) psychic damage and must succeed on a DC 14 Sanity saving throw or lose Sanity equal to the psychic damage taken and be stunned until the end of its next turn.

#### 17. Face of the Maw
*Location: Main Cultist Sanctuaries (Unique Antagonist — Harbormaster Greaves's Final Form)*

**Lore:** A Face of the Maw is a cult leader who has performed a terrible ritual, offering up a significant portion of their identity and sanity to The One Who Watches from Silver. Their face becomes a shimmering, shifting canvas that flickers between their original features and a perfect, silver reflection of whoever is looking at them — always with a silent, open mouth. Harbormaster Greaves uses this stat block in his final, revealed form.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Face of the Maw is a Leader-type monster whose greatest strength lies in empowering its allies. It will position itself in the center of its followers, protected by Zealots. Its *Aura of the Gaping Maw* is a constant source of Sanity drain. It will use *Voice of the Void* to disable the most threatening PC. It will fight to the death, believing its demise will make it a martyr.

**Synergies:** This creature is the ultimate synergistic centerpiece for a cultist encounter. Its Aura weakens the entire party's mental defenses, making them more susceptible to every other Sanity-damaging effect on the field. A Face of the Maw is never encountered alone.

---
