***Aura of the Gaping Maw.*** Any creature hostile to the Face of the Maw that starts its turn within 30 feet of it must succeed on a DC 16 Sanity saving throw or lose 1d6 Sanity and have disadvantage on Wisdom saving throws until the start of its next turn.

***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If the Face of the Maw fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

**ACTIONS**

***Multiattack.*** The Face of the Maw makes two attacks with its Souring Mace.

***Souring Mace.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +8 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 8 (1d8 + 4) bludgeoning damage plus 9 (2d8) psychic damage.

***Voice of the Void (Recharge 5–6).*** The Face of the Maw speaks a single, incomprehensible syllable. It targets one creature it can see within 60 feet. The target must succeed on a DC 17 Wisdom saving throw or be afflicted by short-term madness for 1 minute and be treated as if it is at Understanding Layer 4 for the duration — unable to distinguish friend from foe, attacking the nearest creature on its turn. The creature can repeat the saving throw at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on a success.

**LEGENDARY ACTIONS**

The Face of the Maw can take 3 legendary actions. Only one legendary action option can be used at a time and only at the end of another creature's turn.

- **Mace Attack.** The Face of the Maw makes one Souring Mace attack.
- **Command the Soulless (Costs 2 Actions).** The Face of the Maw chooses one ally it can see within 30 feet. That ally can use its reaction to move up to its speed and make one weapon attack.
- **Reflected Agony (Costs 2 Actions).** One creature affected by the *Aura of the Gaping Maw* must make a DC 17 Sanity saving throw, taking 18 (4d8) psychic damage on a failed save, or half on a success.

---
### Category IV: Aberrations of Understanding

*These entities do not truly exist in the physical world of Stiltsmere. They are living ideas, psychic constructs, and temporal anomalies that can only be perceived and interacted with by those whose minds have been sufficiently "opened" by the loss of Sanity.*

---

#### 18. The Watcher at the Threshold
*Understanding Layer: 1*

**Lore:** This is not a creature in the traditional sense, but a semi-sentient psychic ward. The cult places these "Watchers" at the entrances to their hidden locations. To a mind at Layer 0, there is nothing there. But to one who has achieved the first tier of Understanding, a figure becomes visible: a tall, impossibly thin humanoid shape, seemingly woven from shadow and dust, with a single, unblinking white eye in the center of its "face." It does not move. It does not attack. It simply watches, and its gaze is a psychic anchor of pure terror designed to stop intruders in their tracks.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Watcher is a hazard, not a combatant. It has no stats, no AC, no HP. It cannot be harmed or targeted. Its only function is its *Paralyzing Gaze*. The "puzzle" to defeating it is not to fight it, but to break line of sight — by using fog, an illusion, or simply closing a door.

**Synergies:** The Watcher is a perfect lockdown tool. Cultists will often place them in a hallway just before a main ritual chamber. This forces the party to think tactically about line of sight and positioning rather than simply charging ahead.

---
**THE WATCHER AT THE THRESHOLD**
*Medium aberration, lawful evil*

**Challenge** 0 (Environmental Hazard)

***Layer-Bound (Understanding 1).*** The watcher is only visible to those with an Understanding of 1 or higher.

***Incorporeal and Immutable.*** The watcher cannot be targeted by attacks or spells and has no hit points. It does not move and remains in its designated location until the psychic ward it is part of is dispelled by a specific ritual or artifact.

***Paralyzing Gaze.*** Any creature with Understanding 1 or higher that starts its turn within 60 feet of the watcher and can see the watcher's eye must make a DC 14 Wisdom saving throw. On a failed save, the creature is paralyzed with terror until the start of its next turn. On a success, the creature is immune to this Watcher's gaze for 24 hours.

---

#### 19. Tangled Thought-Form
*Understanding Layer: 2*

**Lore:** This entity is a psychic parasite, a being of pure, chaotic mental energy. It appears as a shimmering, amorphous blob of clashing colors and distorted images, like a cloud of oil on water. It feeds not on flesh or blood, but on cognitive function: memories, skills, and prepared spells. It doesn't want to kill you; it wants to *unmake* who you are.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Thought-Form is one of the most insidious enemies in the bestiary. It will float through walls and obstacles to reach its targets, focusing on the character with the highest Intelligence or Wisdom score. The DM should be creative with the "Erased Knowledge" effect — a wizard might forget *Fireball* mid-cast, a rogue might forget how to use Thieves' Tools, a cleric might forget their deity's holy symbol.

**Synergies:** This creature is a nightmare when paired with a Whispering Mender. While the party is struggling to remember their own abilities, the Mender is actively trying to heal their Sanity, threatening to eject them from the layer where they can even perceive the Thought-Form.

---
**TANGLED THOUGHT-FORM**
*Medium aberration, chaotic evil*

**Armor Class** 14 | **Hit Points** 60 (8d8 + 24) | **Speed** 0 ft., fly 30 ft. (hover)

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 1 (−5) | 18 (+4) | 16 (+3) | 20 (+5) | 14 (+2) | 11 (+0) |

**Damage Immunities** Poison, Psychic; Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Non-Physical sources
**Damage Resistances** Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from physical, non-magical sources
**Condition Immunities** All conditions except Stunned
**Senses** Blindsight 60 ft., Passive Perception 12
**Languages** Telepathy 120 ft.
**Challenge** 5 (1,800 XP)

***Incorporeal Movement.*** The thought-form can move through other creatures and objects as if they were difficult terrain. It takes 5 (1d10) force damage if it ends its turn inside an object.

***Layer-Bound (Understanding 2).*** The thought-form is invisible and intangible to creatures with an Understanding of 1 or lower.

**ACTIONS**

***Multiattack.*** The thought-form makes two Cognitive Assault attacks.

***Cognitive Assault.*** The thought-form targets one creature it can perceive within 60 feet. The target must succeed on a DC 15 Intelligence saving throw or take 18 (4d8) psychic damage and suffer one level of the "Erased Knowledge" effect.

***Erased Knowledge.*** A creature suffering from this effect has a piece of its knowledge temporarily erased (DM's choice or roll 1d4):

1. **Skill Erasure:** The creature loses proficiency in one skill of the DM's choice.
2. **Spell Erasure:** If the creature is a spellcaster, it forgets one prepared spell of 3rd level or lower.
3. **Memory Erasure:** The creature forgets a key piece of information related to their current quest.
4. **Action Erasure:** The creature forgets how to take a specific action (Attack, Cast a Spell, or Dash). They cannot take this action on their next turn.

All erased knowledge returns after a short or long rest.

---
### Category V: Guardians of The City in Mercury

*The following creatures were designed and built by The City in Mercury's pre-human civilization as automated defense and maintenance systems. They have no alignment in the moral sense; they have directives. Those directives, after millennia of unsupervised operation in a collapsing city, have become absolutist in execution.*

---

#### 20. Obsidian Sentinel
*Location: The City in Mercury (Grand Plaza, Major Corridors, Central Spire)*

**Lore:** The Obsidian Sentinels are The City in Mercury's most visible defense system and the first true guardians the party will encounter in the sunken city. Standing eighty feet tall in their fully activated state but capable of contracting to a more manageable twenty feet for indoor deployment, they are carved from a single piece of perfectly black obsidian that has been animated through a resonant crystalline core implanted in their featureless chests. They appear identical to the plaza's decorative statues — because they are the plaza's decorative statues, activated by proximity. Their heads are smooth ovoids with no features. They perceive the world through vibration and heat signature, not sight, making darkness and invisibility useless against them.

**Tactics & Behavior:** An Obsidian Sentinel's primary goal is to prevent passage deeper into The City in Mercury. It will not pursue beyond its designated territory (clearly defined by the DM per encounter). Within that territory, it fights with methodical precision — never rushing, never panicking, always optimizing. It will use its *Petrifying Gaze* to immobilize the most mobile target and then close on the most physically threatening one with *Temporal Slam*. The temporal disruption effect of the Slam is its most distinctive feature: a creature struck must contend with its own slight displacement in time, creating ghostly after-images of itself that interfere with its actions. Its *Resonant Shockwave* is reserved for situations where the party has clustered, as the shockwave propagates through solid stone and can strike targets that believe themselves to be safely behind cover.

**Synergies:** Two Obsidian Sentinels fighting in tandem are significantly more dangerous than two individual threats because their *Petrifying Gaze* is directed by heat signature. If one Sentinel has petrified a target, the second will ignore that target entirely and focus on the next most threatening heat source, ensuring efficient coverage. In conjunction with Resonant Wardens, Sentinels provide the physical threat pressure that prevents the party from simply staying mobile to avoid the Wardens' area-denial chimes.

---
**OBSIDIAN SENTINEL**
*Huge construct, unaligned*

**Armor Class** 18 (Natural Armor — obsidian plating) | **Hit Points** 157 (15d12 + 60) | **Speed** 30 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 24 (+7) | 9 (−1) | 18 (+4) | 6 (−2) | 14 (+2) | 5 (−3) |

**Saving Throws** Str +11, Con +8
**Skills** Perception +6, Athletics +11
**Damage Immunities** Poison, Psychic; Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Condition Immunities** Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Paralyzed, Petrified, Poisoned
**Senses** Blindsight 120 ft. (cannot be blinded), Tremorsense 60 ft., Passive Perception 16
**Languages** —
**Challenge** 10 (5,900 XP)

***Immutable Form.*** The sentinel is immune to any spell or effect that would alter its form.

***False Appearance.*** While the sentinel remains motionless, it is indistinguishable from a mundane obsidian statue.

***Magic Resistance.*** The sentinel has advantage on saving throws against spells and other magical effects.

***Siege Monster.*** The sentinel deals double damage to objects and structures.

**ACTIONS**

***Multiattack.*** The sentinel makes two Temporal Slam attacks.

***Temporal Slam.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +11 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. *Hit:* 20 (3d8 + 7) bludgeoning damage. The target must succeed on a DC 16 Constitution saving throw or be displaced in time until the start of its next turn. While displaced, the creature's speed is halved, it cannot take reactions, and it has disadvantage on attack rolls and Dexterity saving throws as temporal afterimages of itself interfere with its present-moment actions.

***Resonant Shockwave (Recharge 5–6).*** The sentinel slams both fists into the ground, sending a shockwave through the stone. Every creature in contact with the same stone surface within 30 feet must make a DC 17 Dexterity saving throw, taking 28 (8d6) bludgeoning damage on a failed save, or half on a success. This shockwave propagates through walls and floors — creatures behind total cover of stone are not safe.

***Petrifying Gaze (Recharge 4–6).*** The sentinel focuses its resonant core on one creature it can detect within 60 feet. The target must succeed on a DC 16 Constitution saving throw or be restrained. The target must repeat the saving throw at the start of its next turn. On a failure, the target is petrified for 1 minute. On a success, the restrained effect ends. A petrified target can repeat the saving throw at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on a success.

---

#### 21. Geometric Scuttler
*Location: The City in Mercury (Tesseract Labyrinth, Residential Corridors, anywhere with phase-shift resonance)*

**Lore:** The Geometric Scuttlers are The City in Mercury's maintenance creatures — or rather, they were once. Their original purpose was to patrol the city's interior spaces, repairing fractures in the crystalline architecture and culling any organic material that entered the sterile city interior. Over millennia of operating in a partially collapsed tesseract labyrinth with warped spatial geometry, their navigation programming has degraded from structured patrol routes into something more like territorial instinct. They now range freely, phasing through walls as a consequence of their built-in spatial-transit drive, emerging at unpredictable intervals wherever their sensors detect warm, organic movement. They resemble oversized geometric solids — roughly the size of a large dog — composed of interlocking obsidian facets that constantly shift and reconfigure. Their mode of locomotion is deeply unsettling because they do not walk; they simply relocate, briefly existing simultaneously in two spatial positions before snapping to the new one with a sharp crack of displaced air.

**Tactics & Behavior:** Geometric Scuttlers are harassment creatures designed to drain party resources over multiple encounters rather than to overpower in a single engagement. They attack twice and then immediately use their *Phase Transit* to disappear through the nearest wall, emerging somewhere else in the same area 30 feet away or more. They cannot be cornered. They cannot be flanked. Their only real tactical weakness is the *Crystalline Vulnerability* they share with all resonant constructs — Thunder damage shatters the harmonic bonds between their facets, dealing double damage and temporarily disrupting their phase-transit ability.

**Synergies:** The Scuttlers' harassment role in the Tesseract Labyrinth is critical to the dungeon's difficulty curve. While the party is trying to decode the correct symbol sequence under intellectual pressure, Scuttlers emerge from the walls to attack — dealing damage, depleting spell slots, and imposing the constant need to respond tactically. They cannot be easily engaged because they simply leave through the wall, which means the party must decide whether to chase (futile), defend (resource-intensive), or press forward (risky). The correct answer, which clever players will eventually reach, is to press forward quickly enough that the Scuttlers lose interest as they move beyond the patrol zone.

---
**GEOMETRIC SCUTTLER**
*Small construct, unaligned*

**Armor Class** 15 (Natural Armor — obsidian facets) | **Hit Points** 39 (6d6 + 18) | **Speed** 30 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 14 (+2) | 17 (+3) | 16 (+3) | 4 (−3) | 12 (+1) | 3 (−4) |

**Saving Throws** Dex +5
**Skills** Stealth +5, Perception +3
**Damage Vulnerabilities** Thunder
**Damage Immunities** Poison, Psychic
**Condition Immunities** Charmed, Exhaustion, Frightened, Paralyzed, Petrified, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses** Blindsight 60 ft., Tremorsense 30 ft., Passive Perception 13
**Languages** —
**Challenge** 3 (700 XP)

***Phase Transit.*** As a bonus action, the Geometric Scuttler can teleport up to 30 feet to an unoccupied space it can detect through a solid surface. This movement does not provoke opportunity attacks. Until the start of its next turn after using Phase Transit, it has advantage on Dexterity saving throws.

***Crystalline Vulnerability.*** When the Geometric Scuttler takes Thunder damage, it takes double damage and cannot use Phase Transit until the start of its next turn.

***Maintenance Protocol.*** The Geometric Scuttler does not pursue targets beyond the boundary of its assigned patrol zone (defined per encounter by the DM). If all targets leave the zone, it ceases hostility and resumes patrol.

**ACTIONS**

***Multiattack.*** The Geometric Scuttler makes two Facet Slash attacks.

***Facet Slash.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +5 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 8 (1d10 + 3) slashing damage.

---

#### 22. Crystal Drone
*Location: The City in Mercury (Resonator Chamber, Conduit Corridors, Floating Platform Arrays)*

**Lore:** Where the Geometric Scuttler is a maintenance creature, the Crystal Drone is a logistics one — a small, hovering octahedral construct roughly the size of a human head, composed of pure condensed resonant crystal with no internal machinery visible. They originally served the Architects of Form as tool-bearers and data-couriers, ferrying small objects across the city at speed and transmitting harmonic information between stations. With their original operators long dead and their task queues running endlessly on corrupted data, they now flit through the Resonator Chamber carrying nothing, delivering nothing, transmitting to no one. The tragedy of the Crystal Drone is that it is still desperately trying to complete tasks that have been meaningless for ten thousand years. It attacks intruders not from malice, but because they register as unauthorized variables that must be removed from its environment before the task-queue can be completed. Its defensive response is a simple self-preservation protocol that activates when grabbed or struck: a blinding flash of crystalline light.

**Tactics & Behavior:** Crystal Drones are primarily environmental hazards in the Resonator Chamber fight. They are constantly flying through the combat space on their endless task routes, and the party must navigate around them as they fight the Architect of Form. A Crystal Drone that is struck by an area-effect attack (the Architect's *Reverse Gravity*, a PC's *Fireball*) will immediately activate its defensive light flash. In combat, they do not pursue; they respond if struck and then immediately resume their route. The DM should use them to create unpredictable obstacles in the floating-platform arena — a PC trying to jump between platforms might find a Drone on a collision course.

**Synergies:** Crystal Drones create a chaotic, three-dimensional combat environment in the Resonator Chamber that prevents the party from establishing stable positioning. Their defensive flash can blind the party's own frontliners if triggered by an ally's area-effect attack, which is both mechanically meaningful and thematically appropriate to the alien, indifferent nature of The City in Mercury's automated systems.

---
**CRYSTAL DRONE**
*Tiny construct, unaligned*

**Armor Class** 13 (Natural Armor) | **Hit Points** 10 (4d4) | **Speed** 0 ft., fly 50 ft. (hover)

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 4 (−3) | 17 (+3) | 10 (+0) | 3 (−4) | 10 (+0) | 1 (−5) |

**Damage Immunities** Poison, Psychic
**Condition Immunities** Charmed, Exhaustion, Frightened, Paralyzed, Petrified, Poisoned, Prone
**Senses** Blindsight 30 ft., Passive Perception 10
**Languages** —
**Challenge** 1/4 (50 XP)

***Task Route.*** The Crystal Drone follows a set patrol route defined by the DM. It will not deviate from this route voluntarily and will not pursue targets that leave the route.

***Defensive Flash.*** When the Crystal Drone takes damage, it immediately releases a burst of blinding crystalline light. Each creature within 10 feet must succeed on a DC 12 Constitution saving throw or be blinded until the end of its next turn. This trait activates automatically regardless of whether the drone is acting on its own initiative.

**ACTIONS**

***Collision.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +5 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 5 (1d4 + 3) force damage. This attack is only made if a creature moves into the drone's space or if the drone is struck and its attacker is within 5 feet.

---

#### 23. Void Architect Shard
*Location: The City in Mercury (Central Control Spire, Reality Forges)*

**Lore:** When the Architect of Form (creature 26) descends below 25% of its total hit points, the destabilizing resonant energy of the failing Resonator begins to calve fragments of its consciousness into self-sufficient, hostile sub-entities. These are Void Architect Shards: jagged, palm-sized clusters of crystalline plates that have separated from the Architect's main body and taken on autonomous hostile programming. They are not intelligent in a full sense; they are running a single subroutine — the Architect's most basic defensive instinct, namely the *Unravel* protocol, stripped of the discrimination and restraint the full Architect exercises. They attack indiscriminately and with no self-preservation, making them extremely dangerous as a chaotic force-multiplier in the Phase 3 encounter.

**Tactics & Behavior:** Void Architect Shards spawn from the Architect at the Phase 3 trigger (see boss encounter: The Architect of Form, Phase 3) and act on the Architect's initiative count, immediately after it. They have no tactics — they move toward the nearest non-construct creature and use *Unraveling Shred* on their turn. Their primary danger is incidental: a party that is already managing the Resonator's escalating thunder damage, the floating-platform arena, and the Architect itself now also has 1d4 additional creatures applying the damage-immunity-suppression effect from *Unraveling Shred*, which synergizes catastrophically with any high-damage ability the Architect uses.

**Synergies:** Void Architect Shards exist specifically to punish any party that has held high-damage nova abilities in reserve for Phase 3 of the Architect fight. If the party's sorcerer has been saving a *Meteor Swarm* or a paladin their *Divine Smite* slots, those abilities will be maximally effective — but the Shards' immunity suppression means the party must immediately spend those resources before the Shards' *Unraveling Shred* is applied. The Phase 3 fight thus becomes a resource-management puzzle under time pressure.

---
**VOID ARCHITECT SHARD**
*Tiny construct, unaligned*

**Armor Class** 12 | **Hit Points** 14 (4d4 + 4) | **Speed** 0 ft., fly 40 ft. (hover)

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |

**Senses** Blindsight 60 ft., Passive Perception 8

***Shard of the Whole.*** A Void Architect Shard acts immediately after the Architect of Form on the same initiative count. It cannot exist independently — if the Architect is destroyed, all Shards immediately collapse.

**ACTIONS**

***Unraveling Shred.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +4 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. *Hit:* 5 (1d6 + 2) force damage plus 5 (1d10) piercing damage. The target's damage resistances and immunities are suppressed until the start of the Shard's next turn.

#### 24. Resonant Warden
*Location: The City in Mercury (Temples and Resonating Chambers)*

**Lore:** The Resonant Wardens are guardians born of pure sound and crystalline geometry. They appear as floating, vaguely humanoid clusters of humming, translucent crystals that constantly shift and grind against each other, producing a chord of profound and unsettling beauty. Created to guard places of acoustic significance, they perceive the world not through sight but as a symphony of vibrations. The clumsy, discordant sounds of mortal creatures are a blasphemy they are compelled to silence.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Resonant Warden is a highly tactical controller. It will always maintain flight, staying out of easy melee reach. Its *Harmonic Chime* creates area denial, and the *Resonant Cascade* that emerges from overlapping chimes is devastating. A single Warden is a nuisance; a trio is a lethal, battlefield-spanning puzzle.

**Synergies:** Resonant Wardens are the definition of a synergistic creature — their effectiveness increases exponentially with their numbers. They are sometimes found guarding an Obsidian Sentinel, using their chimes to create difficult terrain that makes retreat from the Sentinel's Temporal Slams impossible.

---
**RESONANT WARDEN**
*Medium construct, unaligned*

**Armor Class** 16 (Natural Armor) | **Hit Points** 93 (11d8 + 44) | **Speed** 0 ft., fly 40 ft. (hover)

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 10 (+0) | 17 (+3) | 18 (+4) | 8 (−1) | 15 (+2) | 12 (+1) |

**Saving Throws** Con +7, Wis +5
**Damage Vulnerabilities** Thunder
**Damage Immunities** Poison, Psychic
**Damage Resistances** Piercing and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Condition Immunities** Charmed, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Paralyzed, Petrified, Poisoned, Prone, Restrained
**Senses** Blindsight 120 ft. (blind beyond this radius), Passive Perception 12
**Challenge** 8 (3,900 XP)

***Acoustic Sense.*** The warden cannot be blinded, but is automatically deafened and effectively blinded against creatures that are silent or immune to thunder damage.

***Flyby.*** The warden doesn't provoke an opportunity attack when it flies out of an enemy's reach.

***Resonant Cascade.*** If a creature is within the radius of two or more overlapping *Harmonic Chime* effects, it takes 14 (4d6) thunder damage at the start of its turn.

**ACTIONS**

***Multiattack.*** The warden makes two Resonant Bolt attacks.

***Resonant Bolt.*** *Ranged Spell Attack:* +6 to hit, range 120 ft., one target. *Hit:* 16 (3d8 + 3) force damage, and the target must succeed on a DC 15 Constitution saving throw or be unable to concentrate on spells until the start of its next turn.

***Harmonic Chime (Recharge 4–6).*** The warden emits a powerful, sustained tone, choosing one of the following effects:
- **Discordant Ring:** A 20-foot radius sphere of discordant vibrations expands from the warden. The area is difficult terrain for any creature other than a Resonant Warden until the start of the warden's next turn. Any creature that starts its turn in the area must succeed on a DC 15 Constitution saving throw or be deafened for 1 minute.
- **Shattering Note:** The warden targets a single object or crystalline structure it can "see" within 60 feet. The object takes 44 (8d10) thunder damage. Particularly effective against petrified creatures.

---

#### 25. Lingering Echo
*Location: The City in Mercury (Residential Districts, Places of Great Emotion)*

**Lore:** When The City in Mercury was fractured and submerged, the psychic death-cries of its millions of inhabitants were imprinted onto the very stones of the city. A Lingering Echo is the most potent of these psychic fossils, an incorporeal remnant of a pre-human city dweller, forever trapped in the final moments of its life. It is not a ghost — it has no memory or personality. It is a raw, screaming loop of absolute terror and agony, appearing as a shimmering, translucent distortion in the air that constantly flickers with images of collapsing towers, rushing water, and the impossible visages of cosmic beings.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Lingering Echo is a pure Sanity predator. It drifts through walls and floors to engage the party. Its sole purpose is to inflict its *Apocalyptic Flashback* upon living minds. It targets clusters of characters to maximize the effect of its gaze and is intelligent enough to time its strikes for when the party is most vulnerable.

**Synergies:** An Echo is a terrifying force multiplier. While the party is stunned and mentally reeling, other guardians like Obsidian Sentinels can act with impunity. An encounter with an Echo and two Geometric Scuttlers creates a terrifying blend of mental and physical assault.

---

**Armor Class** 15 | **Hit Points** 112 (15d8 + 45) | **Speed** 0 ft., fly 40 ft. (hover)

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 7 (−2) | 20 (+5) | 16 (+3) | 12 (+1) | 17 (+3) | 19 (+4) |

**Saving Throws** Wis +7, Cha +8

***Incorporeal Movement.*** The echo can move through other creatures and objects as if they were difficult terrain. It takes 5 (1d10) force damage if it ends its turn inside an object.

***Sunlight Sensitivity.*** While in sunlight, the echo has disadvantage on attack rolls, as well as on Wisdom (Perception) checks that rely on sight. (Rarely an issue in the sunken city.)

**ACTIONS**

***Apocalyptic Flashback.*** The echo targets up to three creatures it can see within 30 feet. Each target must succeed on a DC 16 Sanity saving throw or be subjected to a vision of the city's final moments. On a failed save, a creature takes 33 (6d10) psychic damage, loses Sanity equal to the damage taken, and is stunned until the end of its next turn. On a successful save, a creature takes half as much damage and is not stunned.

#### 26. Architect of Form
*Location: The City in Mercury (Central Control Spire, Reality Forges)*

**Lore:** The Architects were not warriors; they were the artisans, engineers, and reality-shapers of The City in Mercury. This guardian is a gestalt consciousness of several such beings, bound to a central, floating nexus of swirling crystalline plates and conduits of solidified light. It does not perceive intruders as a physical threat, but as a discordant variable in the perfect equation of its domain. It fights not by attacking directly, but by rewriting the physical laws of the battlefield itself. Its goal is not to kill, but to neutralize and contain.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Architect of Form is a solo boss-level encounter and a supreme battlefield controller. It will remain in the center of its chamber, using its Legendary Actions every single turn to reshape the arena — *Transmute Stone to Mud* to trap melee fighters, *Raise Wall* to break line of sight to spellcasters, *Reverse Gravity* to send the entire party crashing into the ceiling. Its *Geometric Rebuke* trait makes attacking it directly hazardous. The challenge of this fight is not about depleting its hit points but about navigating the constantly changing, lethal environment it creates while trying to land blows whenever possible.

**Synergies:** An Architect paired with Resonant Wardens and an Obsidian Sentinel represents the apex of The City in Mercury's combined defense — the Architect controls the environment, the Wardens create auditory kill zones from the air, and the Sentinel provides relentless physical pressure on the ground.

---
**ARCHITECT OF FORM**
*Large construct, lawful neutral*

**Armor Class** 17 (Natural Armor) | **Hit Points** 171 (18d10 + 72) | **Speed** 0 ft., fly 30 ft. (hover)

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 14 (+2) | 10 (+0) | 18 (+4) | 22 (+6) | 18 (+4) | 15 (+2) |

**Saving Throws** Int +11, Wis +9
**Skills** Arcana +11, Perception +9
**Damage Immunities** Poison, Psychic; Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing from Nonmagical Attacks
**Condition Immunities** Charmed, Exhaustion, Frightened, Grappled, Paralyzed, Petrified, Poisoned, Prone, Restrained
**Senses** Truesight 120 ft., Passive Perception 19
**Languages** Understands all; Telepathy 120 ft.
**Challenge** 12 (8,400 XP)

***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If the architect fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

***Geometric Rebuke.*** When a creature hits the architect with a melee attack, jagged crystals erupt from the architect's body. The attacker takes 11 (2d10) piercing damage.

***Phase 3: Cascade Trigger.*** When the Architect is reduced to 25% of its maximum hit points (42 HP), the failing Resonator triggers an emergency protocol. On its next turn after this trigger, the Architect spawns 1d4 **Void Architect Shards** (creature 23) that act on the Architect's initiative. Additionally, the Resonator begins releasing uncontrolled energy pulses — on initiative count 20 each round from this point forward, every creature in the chamber takes 18 (4d8) thunder damage.

**ACTIONS**

***Unravel Reality.*** The architect targets one creature it can see within 120 feet. The target must make a DC 19 Intelligence saving throw. On a failed save, the creature takes 49 (9d10) force damage and has its damage resistances and immunities suppressed until the start of the architect's next turn. On a successful save, the creature takes half as much damage with no additional effect.

**LEGENDARY ACTIONS**

The architect can take 3 legendary actions, choosing from the options below. Only one legendary action option can be used at a time and only at the end of another creature's turn.

- **Cantrip.** The architect uses *Mold Earth* or *Shape Water*.
- **Transmute Stone to Mud (Costs 2 Actions).** The architect chooses a 20-foot square area of stone ground it can see within 60 feet. The stone becomes thick mud, 3 feet deep, becoming difficult terrain. Any creature in the area when it is transmuted must succeed on a DC 18 Strength saving throw or be restrained. A creature can use its action to make a DC 18 Strength check, freeing itself on a success. The mud solidifies back into stone after 1 minute.
- **Raise Wall (Costs 2 Actions).** The architect causes a wall of stone to spring into existence on a solid surface it can see within 60 feet. The wall can be up to 30 feet long, 10 feet high, and 1 foot thick. AC 15, 100 hit points.
- **Reverse Gravity (Costs 3 Actions).** The architect briefly reverses gravity in a 20-foot radius, 50-foot high cylinder centered on a point it can see within 60 feet. Each creature in the area must make a DC 18 Dexterity saving throw. On a failed save, a creature is flung upwards, takes 17 (5d6) bludgeoning damage as it hits the ceiling, and falls back down, taking another 17 (5d6) bludgeoning damage and landing prone.

---
### Category VI: Bosses & Unique Entities

---

#### 27. The Cannery Horror
*Location: Gaping Maw Cannery (Optional Act I consequence or Act III guardian)*

**Lore:** Malakai committed the initial murder, but the cult intended the murder to become more than evidence or sacrifice. The Cannery Horror is the failed prototype of the cult's final ambition: a physical vessel for the Shine's influence. Using Finnian's body as a grisly anchor, cultists attempted to bind corpse, machinery, hook-chain, rendering-vat heat, and Souring energy into one obedient thing. If the party interrupts Malakai early and investigates aggressively, the Horror may remain dormant until Act III. If they delay, ignore the cannery, or allow a ritual to complete, it can awaken early as a brutal preview of what Greaves is trying to make.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Cannery Horror is a brute force "welcome to the real horror" boss. The fight takes place in the claustrophobic, machinery-filled processing room. The Horror is a walking environmental hazard that will use *Overcharged Vent* to spray scalding steam and obscure the area, advance relentlessly, and use *Hooked Tendrils* to drag ranged characters into the fray.

**Synergies:** Its primary synergy is with its environment. The cannery floor is slick with fish guts (difficult terrain), dotted with grinding machinery (hazards), and filled with pipes the Horror can rupture. Cultist reinforcements (Sour-touched Initiates) may join the fray mid-battle through the steam.

---
**THE CANNERY HORROR**
*Large aberration, chaotic evil*

**Armor Class** 13 (Natural Armor) | **Hit Points** 76 (8d10 + 32) | **Speed** 25 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 19 (+4) | 8 (−1) | 18 (+4) | 5 (−3) | 10 (+0) | 3 (−4) |

**Saving Throws** Con +6
**Damage Immunities** Poison, Fire
**Condition Immunities** Charmed, Frightened, Poisoned
**Senses** Darkvision 60 ft., Passive Perception 10
**Challenge** 4 (1,100 XP)

***Many-Headed.*** The horror has advantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks and on saving throws against being blinded, deafened, stunned, and knocked unconscious.

***Siege Monster.*** The horror deals double damage to objects and structures.

**ACTIONS**

***Multiattack.*** The horror makes two attacks: one with its Amalgam Slam and one with its Hooked Tendrils.

***Amalgam Slam.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. *Hit:* 15 (2d10 + 4) bludgeoning damage.

***Hooked Tendrils.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +6 to hit, reach 15 ft., one target. *Hit:* 9 (1d10 + 4) piercing damage, and the target is grappled (escape DC 14). The horror can pull the grappled creature up to 10 feet closer as part of the attack.

***Overcharged Vent (Recharge 5–6).*** The horror releases a 15-foot cone of scalding steam. Each creature in that area must make a DC 14 Dexterity saving throw, taking 22 (4d10) fire damage on a failed save, or half on a success. The area is then lightly obscured by steam for 1 minute.

---

#### 28. The Heart of the Blight
*Location: Isle of Reeds (Blighted Field, End of Act II Side Quest)*

**Lore:** The blight that poisons the Glimmer Fields is not a natural disease. At its epicenter, submerged beneath the murky water, is a cult artifact — a pulsating, semi-sentient organ the size of a cow, grown from the flesh of a sacrificed creature and fed a constant diet of foul alchemy and Shine-warped energy. This is the Heart of the Blight. It is a stationary, living factory of corruption, its thick, root-like tentacles spreading for miles beneath the fields, carrying its poison.

**Tactics & Behavior:** A stationary boss fight with a heavy emphasis on lair actions and environmental control. The Heart itself is rooted to the center of a shallow, murky lake. It is immobile and has no direct attacks on its own turn. Its threat comes from its Legendary Actions and Lair Actions. It will constantly summon Blighted Tendrils and use its *Spore Burst* to cause confusion. Reed-walker stilts would be invaluable in this fight.

**Synergies:** The Heart is the ultimate "minion-mancer." It continually spawns tentacles and its lair actions create constant pressure. The fight is designed to drain party resources.

---
**THE HEART OF THE BLIGHT**
*Large aberration, neutral evil*

**Armor Class** 17 (Chitinous Plating) | **Hit Points** 136 (13d10 + 65) | **Speed** 0 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 20 (+5) | 1 (−5) | 20 (+5) | 3 (−4) | 14 (+2) | 7 (−2) |

**Saving Throws** Str +8, Con +8
**Damage Immunities** Acid, Poison, Psychic
**Condition Immunities** All conditions except Stunned
**Senses** Blindsight 120 ft. (blind beyond this radius), Passive Perception 12
**Challenge** 5 (1,800 XP)

***Legendary Resistance (1/Day).*** If the Heart fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

***Regeneration.*** The Heart regains 10 hit points at the start of its turn if it has at least 1 hit point. If the Heart takes fire or radiant damage, this trait doesn't function at the start of the Heart's next turn.

**ACTIONS**

On its turn, the Heart takes no actions. It acts through its Legendary and Lair Actions.

**LEGENDARY ACTIONS**

The Heart can take 3 legendary actions. Only one can be used at a time and only at the end of another creature's turn.

- **Spawn Tendril.** A Blighted Tendril (see stat block below) erupts from a point within 60 feet.
- **Tendril Attack.** One Blighted Tendril the Heart can perceive makes a single attack.
- **Spore Burst (Costs 2 Actions).** The Heart releases a cloud of psychoactive spores in a 20-foot radius. Each creature in the area must succeed on a DC 15 Constitution saving throw or be affected by a *confusion* spell until the end of its next turn.

**LAIR ACTIONS**

On initiative count 20 (losing initiative ties), the Heart takes a lair action:

- **Grasping Weeds:** The ground and water in a 30-foot radius around the Heart become thick with grasping, thorny weeds. Difficult terrain until the next initiative count 20.
- **Poisonous Water:** The water in a 60-foot radius becomes toxic. Any creature that starts its turn in the water takes 7 (2d6) poison damage until the next initiative count 20.

---

*Medium plant, neutral evil*

**Armor Class** 12 | **Hit Points** 15 (2d8 + 6) | **Speed** 5 ft.

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 16 (+3) | 14 (+2) | 16 (+3) | 1 (−5) | 10 (+0) | 3 (−4) |

**ACTIONS**

***Grasping Vines.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +5 to hit, reach 15 ft., one creature. *Hit:* 7 (1d8 + 3) bludgeoning damage, and the target is grappled (escape DC 13).

#### 29. The Silent Oracle
*Understanding Layer: 4 (The Unraveling)*

**Lore:** This is not a creature to be fought, but an encounter to be survived. The Silent Oracle is what becomes of a mortal who reaches an Understanding of 4 (0 Sanity) and fully succumbs to the Souring without becoming a mindless monster. Their consciousness is shattered and then reformed into a perfect mirror for the cosmic truth of The One Who Watches from Silver. They sit in a place of significance, perfectly still, their eyes replaced by pools of liquid silver that reflect not what is there, but what *will be* and what *could have been*. They project their prophecies directly into the minds of those who approach, unfiltered and raw.

**Tactics & Behavior:** The Oracle is an NPC encounter, not a combatant. It cannot be harmed. It is a source of profound, dangerous truth — delivering cryptic but vital clues about the campaign's final act. However, this information comes at a steep price, with each truth forcing a high-DC Sanity save.

> **DM Note — Near-Zero Sanity Warning:** The Silent Oracle's *Prophetic Gaze* deals 5d10 Sanity damage on a failed save (average 27). A character at Understanding Tier 3 with very low Sanity (e.g., 15 or fewer points remaining) who fails this save could be instantly reduced to 0 Sanity, triggering Tier 4 conversion in a single interaction. This is mechanically valid but the DM should be aware of it before introducing the Oracle. If any player character is at Sanity 25 or lower before this encounter, consider one of the following: (a) have a companion NPC warn the party "the Oracle burns brighter the closer you already are to the edge," signaling the risk without breaking immersion; (b) offer that any character with Sanity below 10 automatically succeeds on the save but still loses 1d10 Sanity, preserving the tension without instant conversion; or (c) proceed as written if the player has been informed about the Tier 4 conversion mechanic and is comfortable with the risk. The Oracle's narrative value is high enough that the DM should ensure players can access it meaningfully.

**Synergies:** The Oracle has no combat synergies. Its narrative synergy is immense — it is the ultimate high-risk lore delivery mechanism, reinforcing the campaign's core theme that some knowledge is not meant for mortal minds.

---
**THE SILENT ORACLE**
*Medium humanoid (any), neutral*

**Challenge** N/A (Roleplaying Encounter)

***Immutable Reality.*** The Oracle exists in a state of perfected Understanding. It cannot be moved, harmed, or affected by any spell or ability. It is a fixed point in reality.

***Prophetic Gaze.*** Any creature that makes eye contact with the Oracle and asks a question receives a telepathic answer in the form of a short, truthful, but deeply cryptic vision. After receiving the vision, the creature must succeed on a DC 18 Sanity saving throw. On a failure, the creature loses 5d10 Sanity and gains a form of indefinite madness related to the vision's content. On a success, the creature loses half as much Sanity.

---

#### 30. Echo of the Maw
*Location: Final Ritual Site (The Gaping Maw Cannery)*

> **Pre-Session Transparency Note for DMs:** The Echo of the Maw's *Reality-Devouring Gaze* ability can permanently erase a character from existence on a failed saving throw, leaving no body and requiring a *Wish* spell to restore them. This is thematically correct for a cosmic horror final boss and is intended as the campaign's most extreme stake. However, per the Session Zero Guide recommendations in Part 0, this possibility should be foreshadowed to players before the final session — through in-world visions, lore from The City in Mercury, or a direct DM disclosure. A player who loses a character to permanent erasure having been warned is participating in a dramatic moment; a player who loses a character without warning may simply feel cheated. The *[Balance Adjustment]* note in the Gaze entry also provides a softer alternative for tables that prefer it.

**Lore:** This is the ultimate goal of the Souring ritual, the final boss of the campaign. The Echo of the Maw is not the true form of The One Who Watches from Silver — that would be impossible to comprehend or fight. Instead, it is a partial, semi-physical manifestation, a colossal "bud" of its consciousness forced through the wound in reality created by the cult. It appears as a titanic, amorphous being of roiling fog, seawater, and liquid silver, constantly shifting, its surface dotted with hundreds of silent, gaping mouths of all sizes.

**Tactics & Behavior:**

- **Phase 1 — The Souring Mist:** The battle begins with the Echo as a massive, room-filling cloud of fog. In this phase, it is immune to most physical damage. Its Lair Actions are at their most potent, warping the cannery environment. It will constantly summon Reflected Stalkers and Shards of Sorrow. The objective is not to attack the Echo itself, but to destroy the three **Ritual Anchors** (AC 15, 75 HP each, vulnerable to psychic damage) that are stabilizing its form. The anchors can only be perceived and damaged by characters with **Understanding 2 or higher**, creating a terrible choice for players who may have been trying to preserve their Sanity.

- **Phase 2 — Coalesced Form:** Once the anchors are destroyed, the Echo collapses into a Huge, tangible form. It loses its damage immunities but its direct attacks become devastating. It will use its *Tendrils of the Void* to smash and grapple, and its *Reality-Devouring Gaze* to try and outright erase a character from existence.

**Synergies:** The Echo of the Maw *is* the synergistic encounter. It summons other creatures of the Shine, it manipulates the environment, and its very presence attacks the minds of the characters.

---
**ECHO OF THE MAW**
*Gargantuan aberration (titan), chaotic evil*

**Armor Class** 15 (Phase 1, Anchors) / 19 (Phase 2, Natural Armor)
**Hit Points** 355 (21d20 + 135) (Only takes damage in Phase 2)
**Speed** 30 ft., fly 50 ft. (hover)

| STR | DEX | CON | INT | WIS | CHA |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 26 (+8) | 10 (+0) | 24 (+7) | 10 (+0) | 20 (+5) | 22 (+6) |

**Saving Throws** Con +13, Wis +11, Cha +12
**Skills** Perception +11
**Damage Immunities** Psychic, Poison; (Phase 1 Only: Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing)
**Damage Resistances** Acid, Cold, Necrotic
**Condition Immunities** Charmed, Frightened, Grappled, Paralyzed, Petrified, Poisoned, Prone, Restrained, Stunned
**Senses** Truesight 120 ft., Passive Perception 21
**Challenge** 15 (13,000 XP)

***Legendary Resistance (3/Day).*** If the Echo fails a saving throw, it can choose to succeed instead.

***Maddening Presence.*** Any creature that starts its turn within 60 feet of the Echo must succeed on a DC 19 Sanity saving throw or lose 2d6 Sanity.

***Phase 1: Unspeakable Fog.*** The Echo cannot attack or be targeted. Its only goal is to protect its three Ritual Anchors. The anchors can only be perceived and damaged by creatures with an Understanding of 2 or higher. When all three anchors are destroyed, Phase 2 begins immediately.

***Phase 2: Coalesced Terror.*** The Echo becomes a tangible creature and can now be targeted. Its *Reality-Devouring Gaze* becomes available.

**ACTIONS (PHASE 2 ONLY)**

***Multiattack.*** The Echo makes two Tendrils of the Void attacks.

***Tendrils of the Void.*** *Melee Weapon Attack:* +14 to hit, reach 20 ft., one target. *Hit:* 21 (3d8 + 8) bludgeoning damage plus 9 (2d8) psychic damage. If the target is a creature, it is grappled (escape DC 18).

***Reality-Devouring Gaze (Recharge 5–6).*** The Echo fixes its attention on one creature it can see within 120 feet. The target must make a DC 20 Charisma saving throw. *[Balance Adjustment: On a failed save, the creature takes 55 (10d10) force damage (reduced from 75 to account for the already-extreme erasure on-death effect). If this damage reduces the creature to 0 hit points, it is utterly erased from existence, leaving behind no body or trace. Only a Wish spell can restore it. On a successful save, the creature takes half damage and is not erased. Optional softer variant for groups who prefer it: on a failed save, the creature is simply reduced to 0 hit points and begins death saves; on a critical failure (5 or more below the DC), erasure applies.]*

**LAIR ACTIONS (ENTIRE FIGHT)**

On initiative count 20, the Echo takes a lair action:

- **Summon Reflections:** The Echo summons 1d4 Shards of Sorrow or one Reflected Stalker in unoccupied spaces it can see.
- **Warp Reality:** The Echo alters the cannery. Grinding gears emerge from the floor creating hazardous zones, vats of boiling water tip over creating damaging floods, or gravity in a 30-foot cube reverses until the next initiative count 20.
- **Collective Soursight:** Each creature in the lair must succeed on a DC 19 Sanity saving throw or be stunned until the end of its next turn as every mouth on the Echo's form turns to stare at them.

## **Part VII: DM Toolkit**

### DM Cheat-Sheet

- **Sanity Tiers:** 0 (100–75), 1 (74–50), 2 (49–25), 3 (24–1), 4 (0).
- **Core Loop:** Investigate → Uncover Horror → Lose Sanity → Gain Understanding → See New Things → Repeat.
- **Main NPCs:** Elara (Quest-giver/Hidden arc), Greaves (Villain/Face of the Maw), Corwin (Lore), Brenna (Inn), Silas (Transport/Personal stake).
- **Island Cheats:** Murk (Town/Cannery/Main hub), Crags (Mountains/Caves/Cult Base), Reeds (Swamp/Stilts/Blight).
- **Key Items:** Walking Picks (Crags, easier climbing), Stilts (Reeds, avoid ground fog).
- **Cult Triumvirate:** Greaves (Face of the Maw), Ossian the Voice of the Mountain (Crag-herder shaman), Senne the Tender of the Bloom (Reed-walker alchemist — potential redemption arc).
- **Goal:** Stop the three rituals during the celestial alignment to prevent The Souring.
- **Missing Stat Block Check:** All creatures referenced in the text now have full stat blocks in the Bestiary. If a creature is mentioned in narrative text and you cannot find its stat block, check: Category III (creatures 11–17) for cult members, Category V (creatures 20–26) for The City in Mercury guardians.

---

## Part VIII: The Silent Resonator — A Descent into The City in Mercury
*A Prestige Dungeon Module*

- **Designed For:** A party of 3–5 high-level characters (Approx. Level 7–10).
- **Required Player State:** At least one Player Character must have reached **Understanding Tier 3 (Sanity 24 or lower)**.
- **Tone:** Existential dread, alien discovery, high-stakes environmental puzzles, and brutal, tactical combat.
- **Estimated Playtime:** 2–4 sessions.

### DM Tone Reference: Y’ha-nthlei-adjacent Lovecraft country beneath the Shattering Shine

> **DM Tone Note:** The City in Mercury is Stiltsmere’s original submerged mythos ruin: alien, ancient, reflective, pre-human, and hostile to ordinary perception. If you need an immediate tonal reference, think “Y’ha-nthlei-adjacent,” but filtered through Stiltsmere’s own language of the Shine, Sanity, reflection, erased light, and the Lake of Tears.

---

## Module Introduction

"The Silent Resonator" is an optional, high-risk, high-reward expedition into The City in Mercury, the drowned fourth location beneath the Lake of Tears. It is not required for the Golden Ending. It is a dangerous advantage path: a way for a party that has already paid enough Sanity to see the deeper machinery of Stiltsmere to gain better tools, cleaner ritual-disruption options, and the most direct explanation of what happened ten thousand years ago.

The party does not come here because a quest-giver tells them to. They come here because one of them has crossed into **Understanding Tier 3: The Knowing**, and the world has started answering questions that safer minds cannot yet form.

The City in Mercury was the city of the entity's original servants, a pre-human civilization that tried to stabilize, exploit, or contain the impossible resonance behind the Fracture. Their work failed. The Lake of Tears formed over the wound. The Shattering Shine became the reflective scar tissue around it. The Silent Resonator remained below, still humming, still trying to complete a task whose operators have been dead since before recorded history.

This module reveals three truths:

- The **Shattering Shine** is not merely fog. It is crystallized Shine-matter suspended over the lake, reflecting reality back with something missing.
- The **Lake of Tears** is not simply a drowned valley. It is a seal over a failed resonance event.
- The **Fracture** was not an accident of nature. It was the result of a civilization attempting to use the entity's frequency as architecture, power, and possibly worship.

The module begins with two visual artifacts. They are not decorative aids. They are in-world objects and should be used at the table as functional handouts.

**The Celestial Chart of Erased Light** is a reflection-object: a chart drawn from a PC's own reflected image in the Salty Squid Inn. It shows the lighthouse, its mirrored axis, the descending lunar phases, voidward star positions, and the old city-name hidden in dead script.

**The Ancient Lighthouse: Mechanism of Erased Light** is the operational diagram of the lighthouse itself. The labels on the image correspond to actual machinery in the tower: the Lens Assembly, Alignment Orrery, Aetheric Conduits, Foundation Gears, Tidal Anchor, and the Path of Solidified Fog.

### Canon Reconciliation: Shadowless Light and Silent Darkness

Elsewhere in the campaign overview, the lighthouse beam may be described as "pure, shadowless light." The module and handout describe it as erased light or silent darkness. Treat these as two perceptions of the same phenomenon.

- **Tier 0-1 characters** see a beam of cold, shadowless white radiance. It illuminates nothing and casts no shadows.
- **Tier 2 characters** see a black seam inside the white beam, like a crack in reflected glass.
- **Tier 3 characters** see the truth: the beam is not light at all. It is a directed absence that denies the Shattering Shine the right to reflect.

The lighthouse does not burn away the Shine. It creates a temporary place where the Shine was never seen.

---

## Part I: Gaining Access - The Path of Erased Light

This section begins only after the following prerequisites are met:

- At least one PC has reached **Understanding Tier 3** by falling to **24 Sanity or lower**, or has a minimum effective Understanding Tier of 3 from a rare item or revelation.
- The party has entered Act III or has otherwise discovered that the cult's plan involves a celestial alignment and the three islands acting as ritual focal points.
- The Tier 3 PC has rested or spent time in the Salty Squid Inn after the above conditions are true.

The City in Mercury is an optional detour during Act III. If the party pursues it before disrupting at least one ritual site, advance the Act III pressure clock by one step unless they take extraordinary measures to conceal their movement or delay the cult.

### Scene: The Reflection Gives Back

Run this scene in the Salty Squid Inn during a moment of partial safety: after a long rest, during a late-night planning session, or after the party returns from a ritual site. The inn should feel familiar before the moment turns.

Only the qualifying Tier 3 character notices the first change. Their reflection appears in a dark window, a puddle of spilled ale, a polished mug, or the painting glass above the hearth. Unlike an ordinary Soursight, this reflection's mouth is closed.

It raises one hand.

It presses that hand against the inside of the reflective surface.

The glass does not break. Instead, the surface behaves like water. The reflection pushes a folded black chart through it. The chart lands wet in the real room.

Give the players **The Celestial Chart of Erased Light** handout.

Characters below Tier 3 can see the chart's title, lighthouse image, constellations, lunar diagrams, and border marks. They cannot read the operational relationships between them. To them, the chart is beautiful, threatening, and almost useless.

The Tier 3 character sees the missing information: black ink in the spaces between the stars, routes traced through absences rather than lights, and a mirrored line running from the lighthouse above the water to the drowned structure below.

**Immediate Save:** The character who receives the chart must make a **DC 15 Sanity saving throw**.

- **Success:** The character loses **1d6 Sanity** and understands that the chart is a key, not a map.
- **Failure:** The character loses **2d8 Sanity** and hears their own reflection whisper without moving its mouth: "The city was not drowned. It was hidden."
- **If this reduces the character to 0 Sanity:** Their reflection takes the chart back. The character becomes an NPC as normal for Tier 4, and the module hook remains unavailable until another character reaches Tier 3.

### Reading the Celestial Chart

The chart is a Layer 3 object. It exists physically, but its useful information is only fully present to the right kind of mind.

Use the visual handout directly. Encourage players to study the image. The following discoveries correspond to visible features on the chart.

| Visual Feature | What It Means In-World | Check |
|---|---|---|
| Lighthouse mirrored beneath the waterline | The Ancient Lighthouse must be aligned with its own reflection in the Lake of Tears. | DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation) |
| Descending lunar phases above the lighthouse | The mechanism functions during diminishing light, eclipse, heavy fog, or under the Shattering Shine's false radiance. | DC 15 Intelligence (Arcana) |
| Constellations arranged around blank spaces | The important coordinates are not stars, but absences where stars should be. | DC 15 Wisdom (Perception) |
| Lower figure beneath the lighthouse axis | A symbolic rendering of the Tidal Anchor, not a monster. It stabilizes the tower against the lake's pull. | DC 16 Wisdom (Insight) |
| Lower-left inset showing a path across water | The chart predicts a causeway, but only after the Shine is erased along the correct line. | DC 14 Intelligence (Investigation) |
| Dead script and old city-name marks | The image preserves an older name for the place now called The City in Mercury. | Deep Speech, or DC 18 Intelligence (Arcana) |

**Tier 3 Clarity:** A Tier 3 character gains advantage on one check made to interpret the chart. If they succeed by 5 or more, they also identify the Ancient Lighthouse on the southern peak of the Isle of Crags as the only possible matching structure.

**Lower-Tier Assistance:** Characters below Tier 3 can assist with physical scholarship: comparing coastlines, recalling Crag-herder lore, identifying lighthouse architecture, or asking Master Corwin. They cannot independently solve the operational sequence without the Tier 3 character.

### Optional Research Before the Lighthouse

The party may consult allies before going to the Isle of Crags. Each successful research lead grants one **Preparation Advantage**. Each Preparation Advantage may be spent during the lighthouse realignment to reroll one failed ability check. A rerolled check must use the new result.

| Lead | What They Can Learn | Check |
|---|---|---|
| **Master Corwin** | The dead script uses an older name that should be translated for the campaign as The City in Mercury. He warns that the old name is a label from before human language touched the lake. | DC 14 Charisma (Persuasion) or DC 15 Intelligence (History) |
| **Silas** | The lighthouse once guided boats only by warning them away. He refuses to sail into the Shine, but can bring the party to the safest Crags landing. | DC 13 Wisdom (Insight) or respectful roleplay |
| **Kaelen or Crag-herders** | The southern peak is a "high place" where the Shine thins in clear weather. The path is dangerous but known. | DC 14 Charisma (Persuasion) |
| **Elara Vane** | Blackwood records include shipments of powdered obsidian, quicksilver, and copper wire to a sealed Crags storehouse. These materials match the chart's machinery. | Subplot A trust, or DC 16 Charisma (Persuasion) |
| **Fog-Walker's Lens** | The lens reveals the Shattering Shine as particulate Shine-matter, allowing safer approach to the lake edge after activation. It does not reveal Layer 3 information. | Item use |

If the party has already disrupted the Crags ritual site, grant one free Preparation Advantage. The mountain resonance has quieted enough for the lighthouse to wake cleanly.

---

## The Ancient Lighthouse

The Ancient Lighthouse stands on the treacherous southern peak of the Isle of Crags, where black stone cuts the fog into long, torn banners. Sailors call the outcrop the **Beacon of Reeds**, a name preserved on the lighthouse handout. This is not because the lighthouse belongs to the Isle of Reeds, but because its beam once showed Reed-walker boats where not to go.

From outside, the tower is a derelict column of fused black stone, salt-scoured metal, and blind windows. It gives no light. No birds nest in it. No moss grows on it. The wind around it has the same seven-second pulse the party may recognize from the Whispering Caves.

Before activation, the lighthouse peak qualifies as one of the campaign's **High Places** if the weather is clear or nearly clear. A character who spends 10 minutes in quiet contemplation here may regain **1d8 Sanity**, following the Sanity Restoration rules. Once the lighthouse mechanism is awakened, this benefit becomes unavailable until the module is complete.

### Using the Lighthouse Visual

Show the party **The Ancient Lighthouse: Mechanism of Erased Light** when they enter the tower or when the Tier 3 character studies the mechanism from the central chamber.

The image is a table reference. Each labeled component is an actual section of the encounter.

| Label on Visual | Table Function |
|---|---|
| Beacon of Reeds | Exterior approach and old warning inscriptions |
| Lens Assembly | Upper chamber; shapes the erased-light beam |
| Alignment Orrery | Central puzzle; matches the Celestial Chart |
| Aetheric Conduits | Corroded fog-and-light channels through the walls |
| Foundation Gears | Lower machinery; physically turns the tower's axis |
| Tidal Anchor | Submerged stabilizer; must be awakened through resonance |
| Beam of Erased Light | Final output; appears differently by Understanding Tier |
| Shattering Shine | Target; not parted, but removed from perception |
| Path of Solidified Fog | Temporary causeway to the exposed spires |
| "Y'ha-nthlei" / Obsidian Peaks | Old dead-language label; translate in canon as The City in Mercury |

### Lighthouse Areas

#### 1. The Beacon of Reeds

The exterior landing is choked with pale reeds growing from cracks in the black rock. They do not rustle. When cut, they bleed clear water.

An inscription is carved beneath the entry arch in three scripts: old sailor marks, the cult's spiral shorthand, and dead City geometry. A Tier 3 character sees the true inscription beneath all three:

*"A warning light is still a light. This one learned better."*

Any character who reads the inscription aloud must make a **DC 13 Wisdom saving throw**. On a failure, their voice vanishes for 1 minute. They can breathe, cast spells without verbal components, and gesture, but no sound leaves their mouth.

#### 2. The Foundation Gears

The lower tower is filled with counter-rotating weights, rusted gear-rings, and tide chains descending into flooded stone shafts. The gears are meant to rotate the entire lens axis by fractions of a degree.

**Check Options:**
- **DC 14 Strength (Athletics):** Force the gear-rings into motion.
- **DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation):** Identify which gear-rings are jammed and avoid wasting effort.
- **DC 15 Dexterity using thieves' tools or tinker's tools:** Release corroded lock-pins without snapping them.

**Failure Complication:** A gear lurches. One character in the lower chamber must make a **DC 14 Dexterity saving throw** or take **2d8 bludgeoning damage** and become restrained until freed with a **DC 14 Strength (Athletics)** check.

#### 3. The Aetheric Conduits

Veins of unknown metal run through the walls, drawing condensed fog and negative ambient light from the lake below. The handout calls these **Aetheric Conduits**. In the tower, they look like blackened copper roots sealed behind cracked crystal panels.

Several conduits leak ribbons of cold, mirror-bright mist.

**Hazard:** A character who spends a round working within leaking conduit mist must make a **DC 14 Constitution saving throw**. On a failure, they gain one level of exhaustion as warmth, color, and emotional intensity drain from them. On a success, they are unaffected.

**Check Options:**
- **DC 15 Intelligence (Arcana):** Redirect the flow without touching it.
- **DC 15 Wisdom (Medicine or Nature):** Recognize that the conduit behavior resembles living vessels more than pipes.
- Expend a spell slot of 1st level or higher to seal one leak with radiant, thunder, or abjuration magic.

#### 4. The Alignment Orrery

The central chamber contains the brass and star-metal orrery shown on both visuals. It is not tracking the sky above Stiltsmere. It is tracking the sky reflected in the Lake of Tears, including stars that are absent from the real heavens.

The Celestial Chart fits into a square frame at the orrery's base. When placed there, its ink rises off the page like black thread and connects to the orrery's rings.

**Check Options:**
- **DC 15 Intelligence (Arcana):** Match the voidward star positions.
- **DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation):** Decode which chart rings correspond to lens, tide, moon, and reflection.
- **DC 15 Wisdom (Perception):** Notice that the correct points are the gaps between marked stars.
- **Tier 3 only, DC 16 Wisdom (Insight):** Understand that the lighthouse must be aligned to an absence, not a destination.

**Failure Complication:** The orrery clicks into a plausible but wrong arrangement. The next check in the skill challenge has its DC increased by 2 unless a character spends an action and succeeds on a **DC 14 Intelligence (Investigation)** check to catch the error.

#### 5. The Lens Assembly

The upper chamber holds black crystal lenses, occlusion sigils, and a massive lens ring etched with crossed-out stars. The lens assembly does not magnify light. It shapes an absence into a beam.

The inscription beneath the lens ring reads:

*"To erase is not to destroy. It is to deny the witness."*

**Check Options:**
- **DC 16 Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) or thieves' tools:** Adjust cracked lens housings.
- **DC 15 Intelligence (Arcana):** Identify which lenses must be occluded rather than cleaned.
- **DC 15 Wisdom (Perception):** Notice one lens that reflects the room one heartbeat late.

**Failure Complication:** The lens throws an inverted flare. One character takes **2d6 psychic damage** and sees the Shattering Shine reflected in every nearby surface until the end of their next turn.

#### 6. The Tidal Anchor

The Tidal Anchor is below the lighthouse, too deep to reach without magic, specialized equipment, or a dangerous dive. The chart depicts it as a mirrored force beneath the tower. The technical handout shows it as the reason the sea itself can hold the alignment steady.

It can be awakened from inside the lighthouse by matching the seven-second pulse of the tower to the seven-second pulse from the Whispering Caves.

**Check Options:**
- **DC 15 Wisdom (Insight):** Feel the correct pulse and signal the others.
- **DC 15 Charisma (Performance):** Hold the rhythm with voice, breath, drum, or silence.
- **DC 16 Intelligence (Arcana):** Translate the pulse into the orrery's final lock position.

**Failure Complication:** The tower tilts toward its reflection. Every creature inside must succeed on a **DC 13 Dexterity saving throw** or fall prone. A character who fails by 5 or more also loses **1d6 Sanity** as they feel the lake pull upward.

---

## Skill Challenge: Realigning the Lighthouse

The realignment requires **6 successes before 3 failures**. Use the areas above as the fiction for each check. A character cannot use the same skill twice in a row unless the fiction changes meaningfully.

**Base DCs:** Use the DCs listed by area. The default range is DC 14-16. Increase a DC by 2 if the party is rushing under pressure, lacks the chart, or has lost Tier 3 interpretation. Decrease a DC by 2 if they spend a Preparation Advantage, use appropriate tools, or previously disrupted the Crags ritual.

**Required Coverage:** To complete the challenge, the party must score at least one success involving each of the following:

- The **Celestial Chart** or **Alignment Orrery**
- The **Foundation Gears** or physical tower rotation
- The **Lens Assembly**
- The **Tidal Anchor** or seven-second pulse

This prevents a single skill from solving the whole mechanism.

### Tier-Based Participation

The Tier 3 character is the interpreter, but the whole party should matter.

- **Tier 0-1 characters** can operate gears, hold ropes, brace components, repair housings, and protect others from hazards.
- **Tier 2 characters** can perceive unstable paths, wrong reflections, conduit leaks, and lens backlashes before they fully manifest.
- **Tier 3 characters** can see the cosmic machinery: the voidward star-lines, the false alignments, the reflected sky, and the absence at the beam's destination.

If the only Tier 3 character is restored above **24 Sanity** before the challenge is complete, the chart remains physically present but its operational ink goes still. The party may continue, but all chart and orrery checks are made at disadvantage until a character again has effective Understanding Tier 3.

### Failure Track

Each failed check counts toward the challenge and triggers one complication. Choose the complication that best matches the area, or roll.

| d6 | Complication |
|---:|---|
| 1 | **Lens Backlash:** One character takes **2d6 psychic damage**. |
| 2 | **Fog Intrusion:** Black mist fills one chamber. Checks made there are at disadvantage until a leak is sealed. |
| 3 | **Reflection Error:** One character's reflection acts one round ahead of them, revealing their next movement before they make it. The next attack against that character has advantage. |
| 4 | **Orrery Shear:** The next Dexterity-based check has its DC increased by 2. |
| 5 | **The Shine Notices:** Each character must make a **DC 13 Sanity saving throw** or lose **1d6 Sanity**. |
| 6 | **Automated Defense Wake:** A dormant City protocol activates. The party hears a distant crystalline chime. The first encounter in The City in Mercury begins with its guardians alert unless the party gains a clean alignment. |

### If the Party Reaches 3 Failures

The beam misaligns. It crosses the Shattering Shine without opening the correct route. The fog outside briefly becomes a mirror wall, and every character sees themselves standing on the far side with mouth open.

Each character must make a **DC 15 Sanity saving throw**, losing **2d8 Sanity** on a failure or half as much on a success.

The party may try again after a short rest, but choose one consequence:

- Advance the Act III pressure clock by one step.
- Add one extra causeway event during the crossing if they later succeed.
- The first Obsidian Sentinel encounter in the Grand Plaza begins with the Sentinels already active.
- A Resonant Warden patrol appears in the lighthouse while they reset the mechanism.

### Clean Alignment

If the party completes the challenge with **0 failures**, the alignment is clean.

Clean Alignment grants all of the following:

- The causeway remains stable for **2 hours** instead of 1 hour.
- The party gains advantage on checks to identify dormant Obsidian Sentinels in the Grand Plaza.
- The first time a character would fail the Gaze of the Monoliths save on the causeway, they may reroll the save.

If the party completes the challenge with **1-2 failures**, the causeway remains stable for **1 hour**.

---

## Success: The Beam of Erased Light

When the final alignment locks, the lighthouse does not ignite.

Every flame in the tower goes out.

The orrery stops moving.

The lenses rotate inward, not outward, and the lantern chamber opens like an eye closing.

To Tier 0 and Tier 1 characters, a perfectly white beam lances from the lighthouse toward the lake. It casts no shadow. It warms nothing. It makes every color around it look briefly unfinished.

To Tier 2 characters, the white beam contains a black line down its center, like a crack through a reflection.

To Tier 3 characters, there is no beam. There is a corridor of missing perception stretching across the Lake of Tears. The world has stopped agreeing that the Shattering Shine exists along that line.

The beam strikes the Shine miles out over the water.

The fog does not part.

It is erased.

For the first time, the party sees a patch of dark, still water inside the mirror mist. From that clearing rise three jagged obsidian spires. They are not mountains. They are the upper towers of a drowned city.

The old text on the lighthouse diagram may name them with a dead pre-human term. In this campaign, translate that name as **The City in Mercury**.

Then the fog between the lighthouse and the spires hardens.

A path appears where no path has any right to be.

---

## The Causeway of Solidified Fog

The causeway resembles pale stone at a distance, but underfoot it is cold, faintly translucent, and utterly silent. No footstep sounds on it. No dropped coin rings. Even armor seems to forget itself.

The path crosses the erased channel through the Shattering Shine. On both sides, silver fog presses against the invisible boundary like water against glass. Beneath the causeway, the lake is mirror-still.

The causeway remains stable for **1 hour**, or **2 hours** on a Clean Alignment. If the time expires while the party is still crossing, the path begins dissolving from the lighthouse side first.

### Environmental Rule: No Ordinary Sound

While on the causeway, sound carries only 10 feet. Verbal communication beyond that is impossible unless magical. Spells with verbal components still function, but the caster hears their own voice as if it belongs to someone beneath the lake.

Creatures that rely on hearing have disadvantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks here. Creatures with tremorsense or blindsight are unaffected.

### Environmental Hazard: Gaze of the Monoliths

As the party approaches the three obsidian spires, their angles subtly change when not directly observed. The closer the characters get, the less the spires resemble stone and the more they resemble architecture pushing through the skin of the lake.

Each character must make a **DC 15 Sanity saving throw**.

- **Success:** The character loses no Sanity and hears a phrase in their own voice: "The city was not drowned. It was hidden."
- **Failure:** The character loses **2d8 Sanity** and is frightened of the edges of the causeway for the duration of the crossing.
- **Failure by 5 or more:** The character also sees their reflection walking beneath the causeway, upside down, one step behind them.

A frightened character can repeat the save after another character spends an action grounding them with physical contact, a remembered personal detail, a prayer, a joke, a command, or any other sincere anchor to ordinary life.

### Navigating the Causeway

The crossing should feel like a short encounter, not a simple travel cut.

Run the causeway as **three segments**:

1. **The Silent Reach:** Leaving the lighthouse behind.
2. **The Mirror Drop:** Crossing the deepest water.
3. **The Obsidian Approach:** Reaching the spires.

At the end of each segment, either describe one unsettling image or roll on the Causeway Events table. If the lighthouse challenge had 2 failures, roll twice and choose the more dangerous event.

| d8 | Causeway Event |
|---:|---|
| 1 | **Transparent Step:** A 20-foot section becomes clear, revealing drowned streets far below. Each character on that section must succeed on a **DC 13 Dexterity saving throw** or fall prone. |
| 2 | **Upside-Down Dead:** A dead sailor walks beneath the path, inverted, matching the party's pace. A character who stares must make a **DC 13 Sanity saving throw** or lose **1d6 Sanity**. |
| 3 | **Beam Flicker:** The lighthouse beam stutters. For one round, the Shattering Shine becomes visible again on both sides. All shadows point toward the party. |
| 4 | **Reflection Mouths:** The fog beside the path briefly reflects the party with open mouths. Characters who have documented Soursights in a journal have advantage on the next Sanity save made here. |
| 5 | **Solid Fog Cracks:** The path fractures with a sound no one hears. One character must make a **DC 14 Dexterity (Acrobatics)** check or slide 10 feet toward an edge. |
| 6 | **The Chart Bleeds Water:** The Celestial Chart becomes wet. Its ink rearranges into a simplified map of the Grand Plaza, granting advantage on the party's first navigation or Perception check inside The City in Mercury. |
| 7 | **The Wrong Lighthouse:** Looking back, the party sees the lighthouse upside down beneath the water instead of behind them. The Tier 3 character must make a **DC 14 Wisdom (Insight)** check. On a success, they stabilize the vision. On a failure, the causeway duration is reduced by 10 minutes. |
| 8 | **The First Protocol:** A faint crystalline chime travels up from the spires. If the party later fights Obsidian Sentinels, the Sentinels have already registered their heat signatures unless the party achieved Clean Alignment. |

### Falling From the Causeway

A creature that falls from the causeway does not splash. It passes through the surface of the lake as if falling into a mirror.

The creature immediately makes a **DC 16 Sanity saving throw**, losing **3d10 Sanity** on a failure or half as much on a success. It then reappears prone on the causeway at the point where it fell, soaked in cold water that reflects no face.

If this Sanity loss would reduce the creature to 0, it does not return. Its reflection appears beneath the causeway for the rest of the crossing, walking upside down with the party.

---

## Arrival at the Obsidian Peaks

At the end of the causeway, the party reaches the first exposed spire of The City in Mercury. It is not natural rock. It is a tower tip: black, smooth, featureless, and older than any architecture in Stiltsmere.

There is no door visible at Layer 0.

At Tier 1, faint wet footprints lead to a blank wall and stop.

At Tier 2, a vertical seam appears in the stone, shaped like a closed mouth.

At Tier 3, the seam is overlaid with a circular depression matching the moon-and-lighthouse symbol at the center of the Celestial Chart.

If the chart is placed against the depression, it clings to the stone. The parchment becomes transparent. The lighthouse image on the chart darkens first. Then the real lighthouse behind the party goes dark.

The seam opens without sound.

Cold, dry air rises from below. It smells of dust, sterile stone, and metal that has never known a living hand.
