The causeway behind the party remains until its duration expires. After that, return requires either reactivating the lighthouse from the City side, finding another exit deeper in the dungeon, or completing the Resonator sequence.

The party enters **Area 1: The Grand Plaza**.

---

## Layer and Prop Notes for the DM

This access sequence should visibly use the campaign's Layer System.

Recommended table elements:

- **Layer 1:** Wet footprint strips at the Salty Squid reflection scene and at the Obsidian Peaks entrance.
- **Layer 1:** Wrong shadow near the lighthouse entry arch.
- **Layer 2:** Hidden door or vertical seam overlay for the first spire.
- **Layer 2:** Soursight face overlays in the causeway fog.
- **Layer 3:** Reality fracture lines around the lighthouse lens and orrery.
- **City-specific:** Prepare CiM-1, CiM-2, and CiM-3 before running the next sections.

Use the Celestial Chart handout first. Use the Ancient Lighthouse handout second. The chart should feel mysterious and incomplete until the players stand inside the mechanism and recognize its components on the lighthouse diagram.

### What the Visuals Make Canonical

The attached visuals establish the following table-canon details for this module:

- The Celestial Chart is recovered from a reflection and is a real object, not a dream.
- The chart is a calibration key, not a location map.
- The lighthouse contains a real mechanical orrery, lens assembly, conduit network, foundation gear system, and submerged tidal anchor.
- The beam works by erased light: a denial of reflection rather than ordinary illumination.
- The causeway is condensed Shine-fog made temporarily solid by the beam's passage.
- The exposed "peaks" are the upper architecture of The City in Mercury.
- The old label on the visual can be treated as a dead-language name, but the campaign's canonical location name remains **The City in Mercury**.

### Pacing Reminder

The City in Mercury should feel tempting, costly, and optional.

If the party chooses this path during Act III, remind them through the world rather than through direct warning: fog thickens behind them, distant bells stop ringing, Silas refuses to wait forever, or a trusted NPC sends word that one ritual site has grown worse.

They are not doing the wrong thing by coming here.

They are spending time to buy knowledge.

In Stiltsmere, that is always a fair trade. It is never a safe one.

---

### Part II: The Antechamber — The Plaza of Still Faces

#### Area 1: The Grand Plaza

A cyclopean plaza of perfectly smooth, black stone that stretches for what feels like a mile in every direction. The plaza is lined with hundreds of colossal statues, each at least eighty feet tall. They depict vaguely humanoid figures with impossibly slender limbs, elongated torsos, and large, smooth, featureless ovoid heads. They are not poised for action but for observation, their bodies angled as if patiently waiting for something. The silence here is absolute.

**Features:**
- **The Statues:** A successful **DC 17 Wisdom (Perception)** check reveals that the smooth heads of the statues bear a faint, reflective sheen, as if mirroring the viewer.
- **Inscriptions:** The bases of the statues are covered in geometric, spiraling script. A character who can read Deep Speech or succeeds on a **DC 18 Intelligence (Arcana)** check can decipher fragments: "...wardens against the dissonance...", "...the Resonator must remain stable...", "...silence the outer frequency...". This is the party's first clue that the city's purpose was one of containment, not worship.

**The Encounter: The Silent Vigil**

Two of the statues — indistinguishable from the others — activate as the party reaches the center of the plaza. They step down from their plinths with a silent, fluid grace that is utterly horrifying, cracks of pure white light appearing on their obsidian forms.

- **Creatures:** 2x **Obsidian Sentinels** (creature 20).
- **Tactics:** The Sentinels work in tandem, covering different targets with their *Petrifying Gaze* while alternating *Resonant Shockwaves*. The vast, open plaza means there is no cover. The party must use clever positioning and focused fire to take them down one at a time.
- **Balance Notes:** For level 7–8 parties, use one Sentinel plus environmental pressure; for level 9–10, use two.

**Loot:** One of the Sentinels leaves behind a fist-sized, perfectly smooth obsidian sphere — an **Obsidian Key**, required to open the gate at the far end of the plaza.

---

### Part III: The Labyrinthine Halls — The Tesseract Labyrinth

#### Area 2: The Tesseract Labyrinth

The Obsidian Key opens the massive gate, revealing a single, perfect cubical room, 50 feet on each side, with no visible exit. In the center floats a softly glowing dodecahedron crystal with twelve faces, each displaying a different, complex geometric symbol.

**The Puzzle: Navigating by Unseen Axes**

This room is a projection of a four-dimensional space (a tesseract) into a three-dimensional one. The players are, in effect, inside a complex, shifting maze where the exits are in directions they cannot perceive.

- **Mechanic:** Touching one of the twelve symbols causes the room to "shift" along an unseen axis. When a symbol is pressed, every character must make a **DC 14 Constitution saving throw** to avoid being disoriented (stunned until the end of their next turn). The shift changes the room's layout — a solid wall might now have an archway leading to an identical-looking room, or the floor might become a wall with gravity reorienting accordingly.

- **The Goal:** Find the correct sequence of three symbols to press, which will align the "exit" archway with three-dimensional space.

> **DM Note — Puzzle Reset Conditions:** Players may press symbols speculatively without having found all the clues. An incorrect sequence does not create an unsolvable state. Each wrong sequence simply moves the labyrinth to a new configuration that includes the same three clue-bearing rooms, which may now be accessible from different starting positions. There is no failure state short of death — the labyrinth can always be solved as long as the characters can move and search. The DM should track which configuration the labyrinth is currently in (note the position of the three clue rooms on scratch paper) and adjust which archways are accessible each time a symbol is pressed. Approximately 1d4+2 incorrect sequences before the correct one will create appropriate tension without genuine puzzle-lock.

**Default Symbol Set:** If you want a ready-to-run version rather than inventing twelve symbols at the table, use the following three-symbol solution. The other nine faces may be described as beautiful but inert geometric variants.

| Sequence | Symbol | Meaning | Visible Clue | Mechanical Effect |
|---:|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Open Mouth** | Passage / hunger / threshold | Wall score marks form a mouth-like diamond | Opens the first false archway. |
| 2 | **Broken Circle** | Failed containment | Shattered crystal retains this face | Rotates gravity and reveals the second clue-room. |
| 3 | **Eight-Sided Eye** | Architect perception | The script says six, but one glyph means eight | Aligns the true exit with three-dimensional space. |

A character at **Understanding 3** can perceive faint cosmic-string lines connecting the correct symbols in sequence. This does not solve the puzzle automatically, but it grants advantage on checks made to interpret the clues.

**The Clues:**
1. **First Symbol:** In one of the first rooms the party can access, a successful **DC 18 Wisdom (Perception)** check reveals nearly invisible score marks on one wall forming the first correct symbol.
2. **Second Symbol:** Deeper in the labyrinth, a room contains a smaller, shattered version of the central crystal. It has only one intact face, showing the second correct symbol.
3. **Third Symbol:** Another chamber's walls are covered in the spiraling script, repeating a single phrase: "The Warden's form has *six* sides." However, one single glyph in the entire wall is different — the glyph for the number eight. The correct symbol is the one with eight sides (an octagon). The clue requires both observation and a logical leap.

**The Hazard: The Inhabitants**

- **Creatures:** 3x **Geometric Scuttlers** (creature 21).
- **Tactics:** The Scuttlers are not encountered all at once. They are persistent hunters who phase through walls at unexpected moments, attack once or twice, and retreat. They harry the party, drain resources, and add a constant layer of pressure to the puzzle-solving process. As noted in the Scuttler stat block, they will cease hostility if the party moves beyond their patrol zone — which is the intended solution.

**The Exit:** Once the correct three symbols are pressed in sequence, the final archway opens onto a wide, stable-looking bridge leading into the heart of the city.

---

### Part IV: The Final Chamber — The Resonator Core

#### Area 3: The Chamber of the Silent Resonator

A vast, spherical cavern, a thousand feet in diameter. In the exact center, suspended over a chasm of pure, bottomless blackness, is a single, perfect crystal the size of a house, glowing with brilliant white light. The hum it emits is a physical force that vibrates in bones and teeth and soul. Arcane energy arcs from the colossal crystal to three smaller, black stone pylons on floating platforms around it. And guarding the Resonator is an **Architect of Form** (creature 26).

**The Boss Encounter**

**Phase 1: Shatter the Containment Pylons**
- **Objective:** The Architect is immune to all damage at the start of the fight. The party must destroy the three **Containment Pylons** (AC 16, 100 HP each, resistant to all damage except Force and Thunder).
- **The Battlefield:** The pylons are on three separate floating platforms, 60 feet from the central platform where the party starts, requiring flight, jumping, or clever magic to reach.
- **The Architect's Role:** It will relentlessly use Legendary Actions to thwart the party — *Raise Wall* to block ranged attacks against pylons, *Transmute Stone to Mud* on occupied platforms, *Reverse Gravity* to fling characters into the chasm. Its action will be *Unravel Reality* on the greatest threat to a pylon.

**Phase 2: Confront the Guardian**
- **Objective:** Once the third pylon is destroyed, the Resonator flashes red and a wave of force erupts. The Architect is now vulnerable to damage.
- **The Architect's Role:** It now focuses its full attention on the party. Legendary Actions are used aggressively to separate and disable party members. The platforms begin to slowly drift, making positioning a constant challenge.

**Phase 3: Resonator Destabilization**
- **Trigger:** When the Architect reaches 25% HP (per its stat block's *Phase 3: Cascade Trigger* trait). It spawns 1d4 Void Architect Shards and the Resonator begins pulsing uncontrolled energy — 18 (4d8) thunder damage to every creature in the chamber on initiative count 20. This is a race against time.

---

### Part V: The Aftermath & Rewards

With the Architect's destruction, the Resonator's hum becomes a deafening, piercing shriek. The entire chamber begins to shake.

**The Choice**

The party has moments before the Resonator explodes.

1. **Destroy the Resonator (The Safe Path):** A few decisive blows against the cracked crystal will shatter it. The party has just enough time to flee before the entrance collapses.

2. **Attempt to Recalibrate It (The High-Risk Path):** A control dais near the central crystal might stabilize the energy flow. This requires a final skill challenge.

   - **Skill Challenge (DC 19, 3 successes before 2 failures):**
     - **Intelligence (Arcana):** To understand the alien control interface.
     - **Wisdom (Insight) or Investigation:** To deduce the correct harmonic sequence from the failing energy patterns.
     - **Dexterity (Sleight of Hand):** To manipulate the delicate crystalline controls without shattering them.

   - **Success:** The Resonator subsides into a stable hum. A fist-sized shard breaks off and floats toward the party.
   - **Failure:** The Resonator explodes. Each creature must make a **DC 20 Dexterity saving throw**. Success: blasted out of the collapsing entrance, significant damage but alive. Failure: caught in the full collapse of The City in Mercury, lost forever.

**The Lore Reward (Always, if the party survives)**

- The City in Mercury was not a city of worshippers, but a prison — or rather, a buffer zone — built by an ancient race to contain a "dissonant frequency" from another reality: The One Who Watches from Silver.
- The Silent Resonator was their primary containment tool, a device meant to enforce stable reality.
- The Fracture and the creation of the Lake of Tears was the result of this device *failing* during a critical event, shattering the land and creating a permanent "wound" in reality (the Shattering Shine) where the entity's influence could leak through.
- The cult's Souring ritual is not the beginning of something new — it is the attempted completion of a ten-thousand-year-old accident.

**Treasure:**

- **Tome of the Unseen Visage** *(Legendary Wondrous Item):* Reading this tome takes 48 hours over 6 days. Upon completion, the reader's Understanding Tier permanently increases by 1, their maximum Sanity is permanently reduced by 20, and they gain the ability to cast *clairvoyance* once per day without material components.

- **Amulet of Still Reflections** *(Very Rare Wondrous Item, requires attunement):* Once per day as an action, the wearer's reflection vanishes from all surfaces for 1 minute. During this time, they are treated as invisible to any aberration with the "Shine" or "Reflection" descriptor and have advantage on saving throws against any effect that targets Sanity.

- **Shard of Resonant Control** *(Artifact — only obtainable via successful Recalibration):* A single-use item. When shattered as an action, targets a single ongoing ritual or zone of magical influence up to 1 mile in radius. For 1 hour, any creature within the area attempting to perform a ritual or cast a spell that would alter reality or summon an extraplanar entity must first succeed on a DC 22 spellcasting ability check or waste the action. All aberrations within the area have disadvantage on all attack rolls and saving throws.

---
## **Part IX: Events, Quests, and Secrets of the Shrouded Isles**

This chapter provides the DM with a rich tapestry of encounters, ambient events, and deeper mysteries to weave into the main campaign, making Stiltsmere feel alive, dynamic, and perpetually unsettling.

---

### Part I: Expanded Random Fog Events

**Universal Fog Events (Can occur on any island or on the lake):**

1. **The Sound Lag:** For the next hour, all sounds are delayed by a full six seconds. This imposes disadvantage on Perception checks that rely on hearing and requires active concentration to hold a conversation.

2. **The Scent of Memory:** The fog takes on a potent, specific smell deeply personal to one PC — the smell of their childhood home, their mentor's pipe tobacco, a lost love's perfume. The character must make a **DC 13 Sanity saving throw**. On a failure, they lose **1d6 Sanity** and suffer disadvantage on all attack rolls for 10 minutes.

3. **The Silent Choir:** A multi-part choir singing a beautiful, haunting melody that seems to come from everywhere and nowhere. Any character who actively listens must succeed on a **DC 14 Wisdom saving throw** or be charmed for 1 minute, compelled to stand still and listen, unable to take any actions.

4. **Reflected Injury:** One character sees their own reflection in a nearby puddle bearing a grievous injury. The vision deals **2d6 psychic damage** from the sheer horror.

5. **The Follower:** The party feels they are being followed. A successful **DC 17 Wisdom (Perception)** check reveals a tall, slender, humanoid silhouette moving at the absolute edge of the fog's visibility, always keeping pace, always just out of focus. It follows them for an hour before melting away.

6. **The Ground is a Mirror:** The fog becomes so thick and low to the ground that it takes on a perfect, mirror-like sheen, creating vertigo as if the party is walking on glass suspended over an endless drop. Each character must succeed on a **DC 12 Dexterity (Acrobatics)** check to navigate the next 100 feet; on a failure, they fall prone.

7. **The Fog Thins Upwards:** A bizarre weather phenomenon where the fog on the ground remains thick but the air above ten feet is perfectly clear. Creatures that can fly can use this to their advantage, diving out of the clear sky and retreating back into it, becoming impossible to target by characters trapped below.

8. **The Drifting Soursight:** A Soursight of an unknown person drifts silently by the party. All who see it must make a **DC 15 Sanity saving throw**, losing **1d10 Sanity** on a failure.

9. **The Cold Spot:** The temperature abruptly plummets to below freezing. The cold is unnatural and vanishes after 50 feet. For the next hour, affected characters are vulnerable to cold damage. This is a sign that a Fog-Dredge has recently passed by.

10. **A Moment of Clarity:** The fog parts unexpectedly, revealing a breathtaking vista. Each character may make a **DC 12 Wisdom (Insight)** check. On a success, regain **1d8 Sanity**. On a failure, the scale of the world and their own insignificance overwhelms them — lose **1d4 Sanity**.

**Island-Specific Fog Events:**

*Isle of Murk:*

11. **The Cannery's Breath:** The fog becomes thick with the greasy, cloying stench of the Gaping Maw Cannery regardless of distance. DC 11 Constitution save or be poisoned for 10 minutes.

12. **Echoes of the Docks:** The fog carries the sounds of a bustling dockside — shouting sailors, creaking ropes, a ship's bell. These are psychic echoes of the town's past, and sometimes a ghostly, open-mouthed figure can be seen striding through the mist before vanishing.

*Isle of Crags:*

13. **The Cliff's Edge Mirage:** The fog plays tricks with treacherous terrain. A solid, wide ledge may appear to be a sheer drop, or a deadly chasm may be obscured by a perfect illusion of solid ground. A **DC 15 Wisdom (Survival)** check sees through the illusion. Failure could be catastrophic.

14. **The Mountain's Whisper:** The wind whistling through the crags and fog sounds exactly like faint whispering. A character with **Understanding 1 or higher** can decipher fragments that are actually instructions for one of the cult's rituals or the password to a hidden door in the Whispering Caves.

*Isle of Reeds:*

15. **The Corpse Light:** Small, flickering balls of pale, phosphorescent light dance above the surface of the flooded fields within the fog, beckoning travelers off the safe paths and into deep, murky water where Glimmer Bloom Ambushers or Blight-fin Piranhas may lie in wait.

16. **The Stilt-Walker's Shadow:** The party sees a tall, spindly silhouette moving through the fog on stilts — but its movements are too fast, too jerky. It is a corrupted echo. Nearby Glimmer Blooms momentarily turn black as it passes. Following it leads to a submerged, hidden cult shrine.

---

### Part II: Interconnected Subplots & Modular Quests

**Subplot A: The Blackwood Family Secret**

- **Entry Point (Now Structurally Embedded in Act I):** The party discovers the shipping ledger in the cannery office during the initial murder investigation (see Act I description in Part IV). This is the campaign's primary embedded hook for the subplot.

- **(A1) The Stolen Ledger:** The ledger documents the Blackwood family's shipments of alchemical reagents — quicksilver, nightshade, powdered obsidian — to "private storage" on the Isle of Crags. This connects to the cult's hideout in the Whispering Caves.

- **(A2) The Worried Servant:** A former Blackwood servant, now a drunkard named Tomas at the Salty Squid, can be persuaded to talk (**DC 15 Charisma (Persuasion)** or several drinks). He speaks of old Master Blackwood's "illness," his obsession with mirrors, and days locked in the family's private library. He mentions the family's wealth originally came from "salvaging things from the lake that should have stayed sunken." This links to The City in Mercury.

- **(A3) The Confrontation:** If the party presents Elara with the ledger, she confides in them. The Blackwood patriarch, Lord Alistair Blackwood, was one of the founding members of the Cult of the Souring. He believed he could control it, using its power to unlock the secrets of The City in Mercury. But the influence of the Souring has driven him mad and made him a prisoner in his own home. The cult, now led by Greaves and the triumvirate, has moved beyond his control. Elara's true mission is to find a way to sever Alistair's connection to the cult and destroy it before it consumes everything. This transforms the party's mission from a simple contract into a morally complex operation.

---

**Subplot B: The Tainted Tinctures**

- **(B1) The Addled Farmer:** The party finds a Reed-walker farmer who has gone mad, attacking his own family. They find a small, empty vial on him. Master Corwin identifies the residue: Shinefruit liquor mixed with a psychic catalyst. He notes the only place to process such a thing would be a proper alchemical lab.

- **(B2) The Alchemist's Notes:** In the cult's hideout in the Whispering Caves, the party finds an alchemy lab with notes detailing the Sour-Sight Tincture's creation. The notes reference "the powdered resonance of a dreaming stone" as a key ingredient, and include delivery schedules with a large shipment destined for the Gaping Maw Cannery via "the Quartermaster."

- **(B3) The Quartermaster's Stash:** Investigating the cannery again reveals a hidden stash of tinctures in the quartermaster's office — definitive proof that the cult is operating within the cannery and giving the party leverage against Harbormaster Greaves. The purpose of the tinctures: the cult is using them to "soften" the minds of the populace, making them more susceptible to the Souring's influence and easier to sacrifice.

---

**Subplot C: The Fisherman's Final Catch**

- **(C1) The Wreckage:** Near the shore of the Isle of Crags, the party can find the wreckage of Leo's fishing skiff. A **DC 14 Wisdom (Survival)** check reveals heavy, dragging tracks leading up into the mountains toward the Whispering Caves.

- **(C2) The Journal Entry:** Within the cult's hideout, the party finds a cult initiate's journal describing the capture of a "young fisherman" who fought back bravely. He was taken to a "special chamber" because he was wearing a "strange, spiraling amulet."

- **(C3) The Soul-Jar:** In the cult's primary ritual chamber, Leo is alive but catatonic, strapped to a chair facing a polished obsidian mirror. His Amulet of Still Reflections heirloom is being used to focus psychic energy into the mirror — Leo is a psychic battery. Rescuing him requires not just freeing him, but shattering the mirror, which releases a hostile **Tangled Thought-Form** (creature 19). Rescuing Leo earns the party Silas as a permanent, loyal ally who knows secret coves and passages across the Lake of Tears.

---

### Part III: Island Secret Questlines — The Beast Master's Legacy

Each island hides a multi-stage treasure hunt providing a loyal animal companion with mechanical benefits.

>**DM NOTE:** Do not impose a fixed bond limit by default; if one investigator gathers multiple companions, let the table’s fiction and the animals’ temperaments create the pressure instead of a rule cap.

#### Isle of Murk: The Treasure of the Tide-Eater

**Reward:** A **Tide-Eater Crab Companion** — a loyal, dog-sized crab with a hardened shell resembling wet stone. It can breathe air and water, and its powerful claws can grapple enemies or retrieve objects from water. Once per day, it can perform a "Shatter Guard" action, interposing itself to take the damage from a single attack meant for its master.

**The Treasure Hunt:**

1. **The First Clue:** Master Corwin tells the story of "One-Eyed Jack," a legendary scavenger who left a map "written for those who see the town's true face." He quotes the limerick:

   > *"Where the Maw's black lung chokes the sky,*
   > *And the drowned god's coins lie dry,*
   > *Follow the gaze of the stone-faced fool,*
   > *To find my final, salty tool."*

2. **Deciphering the Clue:**
   - "Maw's black lung": The smokestack of the Gaping Maw Cannery.
   - "Drowned god's coins lie dry": Coins tossed for luck into the fountain inside Tide-Worn Trinkets.
   - "Stone-faced fool": A small gargoyle statue on the Harbormaster's office, facing directly away from it.

3. **The Path:** Drawing a line from the Cannery Smokestack to the shop fountain and then following the gargoyle's line of sight points to a specific piling under the main dock.

4. **The Treasure:** A **DC 16 Investigation** check on the piling reveals a rotten lockbox containing a silver whistle shaped like a crab's claw and a journal. Following the journal's instructions allows the party to summon and befriend a young Tide-Eater Crab.

---

#### Isle of Crags: The Quest for the Three-Horned Calf

**Reward:** A **Tripod Alpha Companion** — a young, exceptionally intelligent three-legged goat. It can carry significant gear and its third leg functions as a shortsword in combat. Its bonded master gains the *Sure-Footed* trait: advantage on saves against being knocked prone.

**The Treasure Hunt:**

1. **The First Clue:** After earning Kaelen's respect (perhaps via the Lost Herd quest), he shows them an ancient rubbing of a cave painting depicting a Crag-herder standing on "The Widow's Needle" peak during a full moon. The moon's light illuminates a previously unseen path down the cliff face.

2. **The Path:** The party must travel to the Widow's Needle peak during a full moon. When the moon is at its zenith, its light reveals near-invisible handholds and ledges on the sheer cliff face, leading to a hidden cave entrance.

3. **The Trial Cave:** A test of agility and bravery — difficult climbs (**DC 15 Athletics**) and narrow ledges over deep chasms. The final chamber is a small, high-altitude meadow open to the sky, where a small herd of Tripods lives, including a young calf with a small, budding third horn.

4. **The Taming:** The calf is protected by its herd. The party must either offer the rarest mountain herbs (**DC 17 Nature** check to find them) or protect the herd from an attack by two **Tripod Ravagers** (creature 8). If they succeed, the calf's mother allows it to approach and bond with one character.

---

#### Isle of Reeds: The Egg of the Murk-wing Matriarch

**Reward:** A **Murk-wing Striker Companion** from an uncorrupted bloodline — intelligent, loyal, usable as a scout. Once per day, it can perform a "Shriek" action targeting a single enemy: DC 13 Wisdom save or be frightened of the Striker for 1 minute.

**The Treasure Hunt:**

1. **The First Clue:** A reclusive artisan in Stilt-Town carves intricate depictions of local wildlife. He once saw a massive Murk-wing near "the Grove of Bitter Palms where the trees grow tallest and the fog never lifts." He gives the party a small, flawed carving of a Murk-wing feather.

2. **The Path:** The party must travel to the Grove of Bitter Palms using stilts to navigate the ground-level fog. In the center of the grove is a single, colossal palm tree twice the height of any other. The climb requires three successful **DC 16 Athletics or Acrobatics** checks.

3. **The Roost:** At the top is a massive nest containing three eggs. Two are cold and dead. One is warm and pulsing with a faint, inner light.

4. **The Guardian and the Hatching:** As the party reaches for the egg, the **Murk-wing Matriarch** returns (use Roc statistics, replacing attack damage with psychic damage from its terrifying presence). The party can either drive her away (reduce to 50% HP) or present the flawed feather carving. A successful **DC 18 Charisma (Persuasion)** check convinces the Matriarch to allow them to take the single viable egg. The egg hatches a few days later, imprinting on the first character it sees.

---

### Beast Master's Legacy — Companion Rules

The three island companion rewards are meant to feel like living allies, not loot with fur, feathers, or claws. Use the following shared rules unless a companion entry says otherwise.

**Initiative:** A companion acts on its bonded character's initiative, immediately after that character's turn.

**Commanding the Companion:** The bonded character can command the companion verbally or through established signals. Commanding it to take a complex or risky action requires a **bonus action**. Simple movement, staying close, hiding, carrying an object, or following a previously given instruction requires no action.

**Default Action:** If not commanded, the companion takes the **Dodge** action and moves to remain within 30 feet of its bonded character if possible.

**Combat Actions:** A companion may take the **Dash**, **Disengage**, **Dodge**, **Help**, **Hide**, **Search**, or **Use an Object** action. It may attack only if commanded with a bonus action or if defending itself from a creature that damaged it since its last turn.

**Scaling:** A companion's proficiency bonus equals the party's proficiency bonus. Its AC increases by +1 at character levels 5, 9, and 13. Its hit point maximum equals the higher of its printed HP or **5 + five times the bonded character's level**.

**Death and Injury:** When reduced to 0 hit points, a companion falls unconscious and begins death saving throws like a player character. A companion that dies can be restored by ordinary resurrection magic, but if it dies because of a Soursight or Shine effect, the resurrection also requires a meaningful grounding scene: burial rites, a remembered name, a Reed-walker cleansing, a Crag-herder song, or a similar act.

**Sanity:** Companions do not track Sanity numerically. Instead, when exposed to a major Sanity event, they make a Wisdom saving throw using the bonded character's proficiency bonus. On a failure, the companion becomes frightened for 1 minute or flees to safety if flight is more appropriate. A bonded character may spend their reaction to steady the companion, granting advantage on this save.

**Targeting:** Enemies may target companions when it makes tactical or narrative sense, but do not use them as cheap punishment. A companion should be endangered when the party exposes it to danger, not because the DM wants to tax the reward.

#### Tide-Eater Crab Companion

Use the statistics of a **giant crab** with the companion scaling rules above. It understands simple commands, can breathe air and water, and has advantage on checks made to retrieve objects underwater or hold onto slick surfaces.

**Shatter Guard, 1/day:** When a creature within 5 feet of the crab's bonded character hits that character with an attack, the crab may use its reaction to take the damage instead. If the damage would reduce the crab to 0 hit points, it remains at 1 hit point and becomes frightened until the end of its next turn.

#### Tripod Alpha Companion

Use the statistics of a **goat**, but increase its Intelligence to 6 and apply companion scaling. It has the **Sure-Footed** trait: advantage on Strength and Dexterity saving throws made against effects that would knock it prone, and it ignores difficult terrain from rocks, scree, slopes, and narrow ledges.

**Third-Horn Strike:** If commanded to attack, the Tripod Alpha makes a melee weapon attack using the bonded character's proficiency bonus + its Strength modifier. On a hit, it deals **1d6 + Strength modifier piercing damage**. At character level 5, this becomes **2d6 + Strength modifier**.

#### Murk-wing Striker Companion

Use the statistics of a **blood hawk**, but increase its Intelligence to 5 and apply companion scaling. It can scout, deliver small objects, and mimic simple whistle-signals.

**Shriek, 1/day:** As an action, the Striker targets one creature within 30 feet that can hear it. The target must succeed on a **DC 13 Wisdom saving throw** or become frightened of the Striker for 1 minute. The target can repeat the save at the end of each of its turns. If the party has completed the Reeds ritual site cleanly, increase the DC to 14.

---

## **Part X: The Hollow Mountain — A Descent into the Whispering Caves**

*A Mid-Campaign Dungeon Module for "The Souring of Stiltsmere"*

- **Designed For:** A party of 3–5 characters (Approx. Level 4–7).
- **Recommended Entry Point:** Act II, after the party has identified the cloth from the crime scene and followed Malakai's journal to the Isle of Crags.
- **Tone:** Claustrophobic paranoia, acoustic dread, cult infrastructure horror, and the creeping revelation that the mountain itself may be something other than stone.
- **Estimated Playtime:** 2–3 sessions.

---

### Module Introduction

The Whispering Caves are the Cult of the Souring's primary operational base on the Isle of Crags — the heart of their hierarchy, the seat of their leadership, and the origin point of every thread the party has been pulling since they arrived in Stiltsmere. The caves are not a simple dungeon. They are a living acoustic instrument, a place where the wind through the rock produces sounds that are indistinguishable from voices, where the cult has built its theology around the idea that the mountain is speaking, and where three Understanding Layers reveal progressively more terrible truths about what the mountain actually is.

At **Layer 0**, the Whispering Caves appear to be an extensive but mundane cave system that has been heavily modified by the cult — dormitories carved into the walls, ritual spaces hung with dark cloth and mirror fragments, an alchemical laboratory, and a deep sanctum where the Crag-herder shaman communes with whatever speaks through the stone.

At **Layer 1**, the caves begin to breathe. Faint sigils become visible on the walls, glowing with a cold, distant light. The fog drifting in from deeper passages takes on the shapes of faces — never formed enough to read, always present enough to unsettle. Watchers at the Threshold guard the entrances to the most sensitive areas.

At **Layer 2**, an entirely different architecture becomes visible overlaid on the cave's physical structure — a network of organic-looking conduits running through the rock, pulsing faintly with bioluminescent light, connecting every chamber like the vasculature of something impossibly large. The ritual chambers reveal spectral participants who never left.

At **Layer 3**, the truth is visible to those with the understanding to see it: the Whispering Caves are not caves at all. They are the interior of a petrified creature of colossal size, dead for millions of years, its fossilized anatomy mistaken for natural stone. The cult's ritual chambers are positioned within what was once the creature's primary sensory organs. The Voice of the Mountain has not been listening to the mountain. He has been listening to the corpse of something vast, something that was resonant with the Shine long before the Fracture, something whose death-echo is what the entity on the other side of the Shine is actually responding to. This revelation does not change the mechanics of the final confrontation, but it transforms its emotional texture entirely.

---

### Dungeon Overview & Navigation

The Whispering Caves consist of eleven areas connected by a non-linear network of passages. The party will not necessarily encounter all areas, and some areas are only accessible based on their current Understanding Tier. The DM should not feel obligated to run every room — the dungeon is designed for 2–3 sessions, and a party that moves with purpose may skip several exploration areas in favor of reaching the cult leadership.

**Key Navigation Rules:**
- The caves are lit by a combination of phosphorescent lichen (dim light in most areas) and cult-placed mirror-lanterns (bright light in occupied areas).
- Sound travels strangely throughout the caves. A loud noise in one area (a fight, a spell detonation) will be audible as a faint echo in every connected area within one minute, giving the cult advance warning.
- The deep passage to Area 8 (The Echo Chamber) requires either Walking Picks or a DC 16 Athletics check to descend safely. This is the intended mechanical reward for completing the Lost Herd quest before entering the caves.

---

### Area 1: The Entrance Maw

> *"The entrance to the Whispering Caves is not subtle. A wide, arched opening in the cliff face, roughly twenty feet across and twelve feet tall, exhales a constant, cold breath of air that smells of mineral dust, damp cloth, and something faintly organic — the smell of the caves' deep interior carried upward. The wind passing through the arch produces a low, mournful sound, not quite a howl, not quite a word, but hovering precisely in the register between. A rough-hewn wooden sign has been driven into the scree beside the entrance, carved with a single spiral symbol. Below it, someone has left three cracked, empty oil lanterns, as if for a procession that never returned."*

**Features:**
- **The Sign:** The spiral is the cult's mark. A **DC 13 Intelligence (Investigation)** check reveals fresh boot-prints in the scree leading inward, suggesting recent foot traffic within the last day.
- **The Acoustic Effect:** Any character standing in the entrance arch hears what sounds like a whispered conversation just beyond the threshold — two voices, distinct but unintelligible. This is the cave system's natural acoustic resonance, amplified by the arch's geometry. It is not supernatural, but it triggers a **DC 11 Sanity saving throw** for any character hearing it for the first time. On a failure, the character loses 1d4 Sanity.
- **Layer 1 Content:** Characters with Understanding 1 can see a faint spiral sigil traced in the stone above the arch entrance, glowing with a cold, barely-visible light. This is a warning marker placed by the cult — it alerts the Watcher in Area 3 when someone with sufficient Understanding passes beneath it.

**Connections:** Leads directly to Area 2.

---

### Area 2: The Dripping Gallery

> *"The passage beyond the entrance widens into a long, vaulted gallery perhaps a hundred and fifty feet in length. The ceiling is a riot of stalactites, some the size of fingers, some the size of a grown man, all weeping thin threads of mineral-heavy water that collect in shallow, perfectly still pools along the gallery floor. The pools perfectly mirror the stalactites above, creating a disorienting double-image — it is momentarily difficult to tell which direction is up. Phosphorescent lichen covers the upper walls in uneven patches, providing a dim, blue-green light that makes everything look slightly drowned."*

**Features:**
- **The Pools:** The water in the pools is slightly tainted by the cult's alchemical runoff from Area 6. A character who drinks from the pools must make a **DC 12 Constitution saving throw** or be poisoned for 1 hour. A **DC 15 Intelligence (Nature)** check identifies the faint chemical smell.
- **The Mirrors:** The stalactite-reflection double-image is disorienting enough to impose disadvantage on Perception checks made in this area for any character who has not passed through it before.
- **Hidden Passage:** A successful **DC 16 Wisdom (Perception)** check reveals a narrow crack in the eastern wall, roughly two feet wide. A Small creature or a Medium creature who removes their armor can squeeze through. This leads to a small alcove containing a dead cult initiate who appears to have been hiding — their journal (see Subplot B, clue B2) details a growing fear of what the cult is actually planning and a failed attempt to escape.
- **Layer 1 Content:** The pools reflect not the stalactites above but the faces of the Soursight — all the open mouths, all the staring eyes. A character at Understanding 1 who looks into a pool must make a **DC 12 Sanity saving throw** or lose 1d6 Sanity.
- **Layer 2 Content:** Characters at Understanding 2 can see that the larger stalactites are not pure mineral formation. They are partially hollow, and within them runs a slow pulse of bioluminescent fluid — the same organic-conduit network that runs throughout the entire cave system at this layer. The fluid appears to be moving *upward*, toward the ceiling, against gravity.

**Encounter:** Two **Glimmering Shards** (creature 2) are nesting in the largest pool at the gallery's far end, their pale light making them difficult to distinguish from the pool's natural phosphorescence until disturbed.

**Connections:** Leads to Area 3 (forward) and Area 7 (via the hidden crack, one-way).

---

### Area 3: The Congregation Chamber

> *"The passage opens into the cult's primary gathering space — a roughly oval chamber perhaps sixty feet across, with a ceiling that arches twenty feet above. The floor has been painstakingly leveled and covered with dark, woven matting. Rows of simple wooden benches face a raised stone dais at the chamber's far end, upon which stands an altar of polished obsidian featuring the cult's spiral symbol inlaid in silver mirror-glass. Behind the altar, a massive mirror — eight feet tall, perfectly flat, its frame made of fused Tripod horns — reflects the chamber back at the viewer, but the reflection is subtly wrong: in the mirror, the benches are full of seated figures, all facing forward, all with their mouths open."*

**Features:**
- **The Mirror:** The mirror is a persistent Soursight anchor. Any character who looks directly at it must make a **DC 14 Sanity saving throw** or lose 2d6 Sanity and be frightened of the mirror for 1 hour. The mirror can be shattered (AC 10, 15 HP, immune to psychic damage), which causes the seated reflection-figures to slowly turn their heads and stare at the character who struck it before the mirror goes dark. This triggers an additional **DC 12 Sanity saving throw**.
- **The Altar:** Contains cult documents — a roster of active initiates (which includes several names the party will recognize from Stiltsmere's docks and cannery), a ceremonial schedule listing upcoming ritual dates, and a hand-drawn map of the island showing three marked points. The map does not explain what the points mean, but a **DC 14 Intelligence (Investigation)** check allows a character to correlate them with known locations: the Grove of Bitter Palms, the deepest part of the Whispering Caves, and the Gaping Maw Cannery.
- **The Watcher:** A **Watcher at the Threshold** (creature 18) is stationed at the passage entrance leading deeper into the caves (toward Areas 4 and 5). It is activated by the sigil in Area 1 when someone with Understanding 1 passes beneath it, and is therefore only visible to characters at Understanding 1 or higher. Characters at Understanding 0 will watch their companions freeze in apparent terror at what looks like empty air.
- **Layer 2 Content:** The spectral figures visible in the mirror at Layer 1 are now physically present in the chamber, seated at their benches, solid enough to interact with. They are Lingering Echoes — not hostile, but deeply disturbing. Each one will slowly turn to face a character who approaches within 10 feet, and each must make a **DC 13 Sanity saving throw** or lose 1d8 Sanity.

**Encounter (If the Party Has Been Loud):** If the party triggered the acoustic warning in the entrance (Area 1), four **Sour-touched Initiates** (creature 11) and one **Grave-Maw Zealot** (creature 12) are waiting in this chamber when the party arrives, having been alerted by the sound. If the party was quiet, this chamber is empty.

**Connections:** Leads to Area 2 (back), Area 4 (dormitories, left passage), Area 5 (laboratory, right passage), and Area 6 (deep passage, center — guarded by the Watcher).

---

### Area 4: The Cult Dormitories

> *"A series of four interconnected chambers that have been carved or widened into sleeping quarters. Simple wooden cots line the walls, most bearing personal effects — a worn boot, a child's toy, a prayer-bead necklace. The walls between the cots have been marked with spirals and prayers in at least three different hands, some overlapping with older markings in a fourth. A smell of unwashed cloth and stale food hangs in the air. Someone has left a half-eaten meal on one of the cots — still warm."*

**Features:**
- **The Personal Effects:** Taking time to examine the personal effects (10 minutes of searching) reveals that many of the initiates sleeping here were ordinary islanders before their conversion — a fishing license, a letter from a child addressed to "Papa," a Murk-folk marriage token. This is intentionally humanizing and will complicate any party member who has been treating cult members as pure obstacles. Mechanically, a character who takes this time and reflects on it genuinely may make a **DC 13 Wisdom (Insight)** check to regain **1d6 Sanity** (see Part XII: Sanity Restoration for full rules on this method).
- **The Still-Warm Meal:** Whoever was eating recently heard the party coming. A **DC 14 Wisdom (Perception)** check detects a faint sound from behind a hanging cloth partition — a frightened young woman named Dara, barely twenty, who was recruited three months ago and has been having severe doubts. She is not a combatant. She knows the layout of the caves, knows that the Voice of the Mountain is in the Echo Chamber (Area 8), and knows that there is "something wrong in the deep place — the walls have started moving." She can be convinced to share information with a **DC 12 Charisma (Persuasion)** check and can serve as a guide if the party protects her.
- **Hidden Supply Cache:** A **DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation)** check finds a loose stone behind the third cot. Inside: 45 gold pieces (collected from initiates' personal funds), two *potions of healing*, a small vial of **Shinefruit Concentrate** (see Part XI: Magic Items), and a folded note in Greaves's handwriting authorizing "additional reagent shipments via the northern dock, to be received by the Quartermaster only." This is direct documentary evidence of Greaves's involvement.
- **Layer 1 Content:** The prayer-markings on the walls are faintly glowing at Layer 1, forming a coherent pattern that a character with Understanding 1 can follow. The pattern is a map of the caves — not the physical layout, but the Layer 1 layout, showing the positions of the two Watchers, the locations of the three hidden shrines, and a marking in the deepest chamber (Area 9) labeled in the cult's script: "the opened eye."

**Connections:** Leads back to Area 3 and forward to Area 5 (through a connecting passage).

---

### Area 5: The Alchemical Laboratory

*This is the Tender of the Bloom's primary workspace on the Isle of Crags — she maintains a second, more extensive lab on the Isle of Reeds, but this cave laboratory is where the Sour-Sight Tincture is formulated.*

> *"The passage opens into a chamber that is immediately, sharply different from everything else in the caves. It is clean. Obsessively, deliberately clean. The floor has been swept free of grit, the walls scrubbed down to bare stone, and every surface is covered with a systematic arrangement of glass vials, ceramic pots, copper tubing, and small spirit-burning braziers. The smell is overwhelming — a complex chemical symphony of Shinefruit brine, Glimmer Bloom extract, mineral acids, and something sweeter beneath it all, something that makes the eyes water and the mind feel briefly, pleasantly unfocused. Long tables run the length of the room. On them, dozens of small vials are arranged in careful rows. Each vial contains a pale, luminescent liquid."*

**Features:**
- **The Tinctures:** The vials contain finished Sour-Sight Tincture. There are approximately 200 doses here, representing the shipment destined for the cannery. Each dose, if consumed, forces a **DC 14 Constitution saving throw**: on a failure, the drinker loses 2d10 Sanity over the next hour (this can push them into a higher Understanding Tier, which is the intended effect). On a success, the drinker only loses 1d6 Sanity and experiences vivid auditory hallucinations for 10 minutes. The vials could be destroyed (each breaks easily) or taken as evidence or — theoretically — weaponized.
- **The Research Notes:** Senne's research notes are here in full (this is the Subplot B, clue B2 material). They detail the Tincture's formulation process, the delivery schedule, and — significantly — a section in a different ink, written much smaller, at the back of the book: her personal observations about the Tincture's effects on long-term users, which she has been conducting without the cult's knowledge. Her conclusion, written in clinical language but with a clear undertone of horror: "Prolonged use does not merely lower inhibition against the Sights. It appears to selectively suppress the identity-formation processes of the prefrontal cortex. Subjects do not merely become more susceptible to the cult's influence. They become less *themselves*." Below this is a single word: *Why.*
- **The Antidote:** Senne has been quietly developing an antidote to the Tincture's worst effects. It is incomplete, but a character with proficiency in Medicine or Alchemist's Supplies and a **DC 17 Intelligence** check can complete it using the available materials. A completed antidote administered to a tincture-addled NPC (like the farmer from Subplot B) will restore 2d10 Sanity and clear the psychic clouding over 48 hours.
- **The Tender's Presence:** Senne herself is not here when the party first arrives — she is in Area 8 meeting with the Voice of the Mountain. If the party has not yet encountered her, she may return to this room in the middle of their exploration, creating an opportunity for the Redemption Arc roleplay sequence from her stat block (creature 15).

**Connections:** Leads back to Areas 3/4 and forward to Area 6 (through a locked iron door, DC 15 Thieves' Tools or DC 18 Strength to force).

---

### Area 6: The Chasm Bridge & The Lost Herd

> *"The passage ends at the edge of a chasm. It is not wide — perhaps fifteen feet across — but looking down reveals only blackness, with no floor visible even by torchlight. A rope bridge spans the gap, old and frayed in places, with several planks missing. It sways slightly in a rising draft of cold, mineral air from below. From somewhere in the darkness beneath the bridge, a sound rises that takes a moment to identify: the muffled, distressed bleating of a goat."*

**Features:**
- **The Bridge:** Crossing normally requires a **DC 12 Dexterity (Acrobatics)** check. On a failure, the character slips but catches themselves — they are now prone on the bridge and must succeed on a **DC 14 Strength saving throw** or fall (60 feet, 6d6 bludgeoning damage). Walking Picks grant advantage on the crossing check, and a character with them who fails can still catch themselves on a second check at advantage.
- **The Goat:** Kaelen's lost Tripod goat — named, per his ledger, "Steadfast" — has fallen into a narrow ledge approximately 20 feet below the bridge, just visible by torchlight. Steadfast is unhurt but terrified and has been here for three days. A rope and a **DC 14 Animal Handling** check can coax him up to safety. Returning Steadfast to Kaelen completes the Lost Herd quest and earns the Walking Picks reward if the party hasn't received them yet.
- **Layer 2 Content:** Characters at Understanding 2 can see that the chasm is not a natural geological feature. The walls of the chasm are lined with what is unmistakably fossilized bone — massive ribs curving away into the darkness, each one forty feet long, belonging to a creature of almost incomprehensible scale. This is the first visible evidence of the Layer 3 revelation.
- **Layer 3 Content:** The chasm is revealed to be the interior of the fossilized creature's ribcage. Looking up from the bridge, the ceiling of the chasm resolves into fossilized cartilage. Looking down, the floor of the chasm — now visible — is a massive, petrified organ of some kind, smooth and dark. The organic-conduit network from Layer 2 is now visible in full, running along every surface, and the party can see that every conduit in the entire cave system converges on this point. The cult's ritual chambers in Area 9 are built inside what was once this creature's primary sensory organ. They are conducting their rituals inside an ear.

**Connections:** Leads back to Area 5 and forward to Area 7 (across the bridge).

---

### Area 7: The Cult's Cache

*A small chamber immediately beyond the chasm bridge, clearly used as a supply depot for the deeper sections of the cave.*

> *"A low-ceilinged chamber, roughly square, packed floor to ceiling with supplies — barrels of preserved fish, coils of rope, oil for lanterns, and several locked wooden chests. A crude rack on one wall holds six Walking Picks in various states of repair, all bearing the Crag-herders' ownership marks — these were not purchased. They were taken."*

**Features:**
- **The Stolen Picks:** Six Walking Picks, each clearly marked with a Crag-herder owner's sigil. A **DC 14 Intelligence (History)** check or Kaelen's guidance identifies at least three of the marks as belonging to Crag-herder families from Shepherd's Rest whose members have gone missing. This is evidence of cult abduction.
- **The Locked Chests:** Three chests.
  - *Chest 1 (DC 15 Thieves' Tools):* 320 gold pieces and a deed of sale for "three barrels, alchemical grade" signed by the Harbormaster's office — Greaves's signature again.
  - *Chest 2 (DC 16 Thieves' Tools):* Contains five doses of the **Shinefruit Concentrate** (magic item, see Part XII), a **Mirror-Ward Talisman** (magic item, see Part XII), and a letter from the Voice of the Mountain to Greaves detailing the timeline for the upcoming celestial alignment — this is the first concrete intelligence the party receives about exactly when the rituals will occur.
  - *Chest 3 (DC 18 Thieves' Tools, and also trapped — a needle coated in Shinefruit extract, DC 14 Perception to notice, DC 13 Constitution save on a failed check or lose 2d6 Sanity):* The Voice of the Mountain's private journal. Ossian's handwriting is precise and academic. The early entries document his genuine scholarly excitement about the cave acoustics. The middle entries become increasingly disturbed as he begins to understand the true nature of what he is hearing. The final entry, dated three weeks ago, reads: *"The Voice does not speak of liberation. It speaks of consumption. I have been a fool. There is no going back now."*

**Connections:** Leads back to Area 6 and forward to Area 8 (a steep, narrow descent requiring Walking Picks or DC 16 Athletics).

---

### Area 8: The Echo Chamber — Voice of the Mountain's Sanctum

> *"The descent ends at the entrance to a chamber that seems too large to fit within any mountain. The ceiling arches at least sixty feet above, hung with formations of translucent, crystalline mineral that catch any light and scatter it in prismatic fragments across the walls. The floor is perfectly flat, as if machined — not carved, but *grown*, a smooth, continuous surface of pale stone that produces no echo when struck. And yet the chamber itself is full of sound. Not echo, not reverberation — sound that originates from the walls themselves, a low, complex chord that shifts and changes in response to any noise introduced into the space. When you speak, the walls answer in your own voice, but slower. When you breathe, the chamber breathes back."*

**Features:**
- **The Acoustic Engine:** The chamber's geometry creates a self-referential acoustic loop — it responds to sound by producing a delayed, lower-pitched version of it. This makes conversation strange and navigation disorienting. Characters who shout or use thunder-damage abilities in this chamber cause a feedback loop: the chamber amplifies the sound and returns it as a **DC 14 Constitution saving throw** for every creature in the chamber, dealing 3d6 thunder damage on a failure (including the character who made the sound). The Voice of the Mountain is immune to this effect in his home chamber.
- **The Altar of Resonance:** A central formation of crystalline mineral that the Voice of the Mountain has shaped into an altar. It hums at a frequency that requires a **DC 13 Sanity saving throw** to remain adjacent to for more than one round; on a failure, a character loses 1d6 Sanity but gains a brief, involuntary vision of the Layer 3 truth — the creature, the creature's death, the Fracture, the Shine pouring through the wound. Characters who fail this save and do not yet have Understanding 3 or higher may become frightened, but they also receive a permanent **+2 bonus to Sanity saving throws** as their mind begins to build resistance to the resonance.
- **Layer 3 Content:** Characters at Understanding 3 see the chamber for what it is: the fossilized inner ear of the dead entity. The crystalline formations on the ceiling are calcified cilia — the hearing apparatus of a creature that, in life, could sense vibrations across continental distances. The cult's altar is built directly on the creature's tympanic membrane — a structure the size of a ballroom floor, petrified into grey stone. This is what Ossian has been "listening" to: the membrane still resonates with the last sound the creature registered before it died — the approach of the Shine's precursor reality. This is not metaphor. The membrane recorded the event. And the cult has been transmitting that recording outward, calling to the entity that killed it.
- **The Encounter:** The Voice of the Mountain (Ossian, creature 14) is here, meditating at the altar. If the party arrives quietly (**DC 16 Stealth** check for the whole party, with disadvantage if they lack Walking Picks), they may approach close enough for a conversation before combat is unavoidable. If his journal from Area 7 has been read, a player who presents this evidence of his private doubt can attempt a **DC 18 Charisma (Persuasion)** check to reach him. A success does not prevent a fight, but Ossian will hold back his most powerful abilities, fighting defensively rather than seeking to kill, and will surrender if reduced to 25% HP rather than fighting to the death. A surrendered Ossian is an invaluable source of information about the ritual timing, the other triumvirate members, and Greaves's full plan.

**Connections:** Leads back to Area 7 (ascent) and forward to Area 9 (a narrow passage behind the altar, concealed by a heavy curtain of dark cloth).

---

### Area 9: The Deep Ritual Hall

*The cult's primary working chamber, where the most significant rituals are performed. This is the climax of the dungeon for most parties.*

> *"The cloth curtain falls behind you and the passage opens into something that stops the breath entirely. A ritual chamber the size of a cathedral, lit by dozens of mirror-lanterns arranged in concentric rings on the floor, their reflections creating an infinite regress of light in every direction. At the chamber's center, a circle thirty feet across has been carved into the floor and filled with some dark, viscous substance that reflects nothing — a perfect, absolute void of light. Around the circle, sixteen stone pillars bear the spiral symbol, and from each pillar hangs a rope. Each rope holds a person. Eight are Crag-herder, seven are Murk-folk, one is a young man who, if Silas's description has been properly noted, can only be Leo."*

**Features:**
- **The Captives:** Sixteen captive islanders, all unconscious but alive, hanging in a state of induced psychic suspension. Leo is among them, wearing his Amulet of Still Reflections, which is connected by thin copper wire to the void-circle at the chamber's center. Cutting the wire releases the psychic energy in an uncontrolled burst — a **Tangled Thought-Form** (creature 19) erupts from the circle (as described in Subplot C). Properly disabling the connection requires a **DC 16 Intelligence (Arcana)** check to understand the circuit and a **DC 14 Dexterity (Sleight of Hand)** check to disconnect it cleanly, without triggering the release.
- **The Void Circle:** The dark substance in the carved circle is a Soursight anchor — a concentrated version of the Shine's reflective property forced into a physical medium. Any character who looks directly into the void-circle must make a **DC 16 Sanity saving throw** or lose 3d6 Sanity as they perceive, for just a moment, what the circle is actually showing: the other side of the Shine, and what is on that other side, looking back.
- **Layer 2 Content:** Characters at Understanding 2 can see that the void-circle is not a floor-level object. It extends downward for hundreds of feet — a shaft that pierces the fossilized creature's body all the way to its core. The captives are not being suspended by ropes tied to pillars. They are being held up by threads of Shine-energy that connect them to the shaft, using their living psychic output as fuel for something below.
- **Layer 3 Content:** The shaft leads to what was the creature's heart. The heart still beats — once every seven seconds, a barely perceptible vibration through the floor. It has been beating since the Fracture. The cult's ritual is not designed to open a new wound in reality. It is designed to use the creature's still-beating heart as an amplifier for the Shine's already-existing wound, making it large enough for the entity to push through. They are not summoning something. They are using a corpse as a loudspeaker.

**The Encounter:** If Ossian was defeated (or surrendered) in Area 8 before the party reached this room, the chamber is empty except for the captives and a standard guard of two **Grave-Maw Zealots** (creature 12) and four **Sour-touched Initiates** (creature 11). If the party bypassed Area 8, Ossian will retreat here when injured and attempt to complete a partial ritual using the captives as fuel — forcing the party to choose between stopping him and protecting the unconscious hostages.

**Connections:** Leads back to Area 8 and forward to Area 10 (a carved stone door bearing the cult's most complex sigil, requiring either a **DC 20 Intelligence (Arcana)** check or the obsidian key taken from Chest 2 in Area 7 to open).

---

### Area 10: The Petrified Heart

*This area is only fully accessible to characters with Understanding 3, though it can be physically entered by anyone who opens the door.*

> *"The door opens onto a passage that descends sharply for perhaps fifty feet before leveling into the final chamber. The air here is profoundly different from the rest of the caves — warmer, drier, and carrying a scent that cannot be identified, not quite organic, not quite mineral, something in between, something ancient beyond the capacity of any word you know. The chamber is small — barely twenty feet across — and perfectly spherical, as if it has been grown rather than carved. The walls are smooth, grey-brown, and faintly striated, like the grain of ancient wood or the layers of compressed sediment. And at the chamber's center, suspended in a cradle of copper wire and cult-placed mirror-glass, is a stone the size of a cartwheel. It is perfectly round. It is perfectly smooth. And every seven seconds, it thumps."*

**Features:**
- **The Heart:** The fossilized heart of the entity whose body forms the Whispering Caves. At Layer 0 and 1, it simply appears to be a large, unusual stone with a mysterious vibration. At Layer 2, the bioluminescent conduit network converges on it, and the pulsing rhythm becomes clearly biological. At Layer 3, the truth is unambiguous and overwhelming.
- **Sanity Impact:** Any character who enters this chamber must make a **DC 15 Sanity saving throw** immediately. On a failure, they lose 2d8 Sanity. On a success, they lose 1d4 Sanity. There is no version of entering this chamber that does not cost Sanity — the concentrated age and alien nature of the space is too much for even a grounded mind.
- **The Revelation:** A character at Understanding 3 who places their hand on the Heart and makes a **DC 18 Wisdom (Insight)** check receives the complete Layer 3 revelation as a vivid, involuntary vision — the creature in life, the Fracture, the Shine as wound, the cult's amplification plan, and — critically — the Heart's weakness. The Heart can be destroyed, which would destabilize the cult's ritual infrastructure and make the Act III rituals significantly harder to complete. Destroying the Heart (AC 18, 200 HP, immune to all damage except Force, Thunder, and Radiant) would require the party to make a significant resource investment here, but doing so reduces the DC of all skill checks made to disrupt the three Act III ritual sites by 4 and prevents the Echo of the Maw from summoning Shards of Sorrow during Phase 1 of the final encounter.
- **Layer 3 Reward:** A character who receives the full vision from the Heart permanently gains **+5 maximum Sanity** as their mind expands to accommodate a truth it has actually been able to process — a rare experience in this campaign. They also gain advantage on Intelligence (Arcana) checks related to the Souring's ritual mechanics for the remainder of the campaign.

**Connections:** Leads back to Area 9. There is no further passage.

---

### Area 11: The Inner Sanctum (Secret — Greaves's Council Room)

*This area is not marked on any map found in the caves. It is accessible only via a hidden door in Area 8 (DC 19 Perception to find, concealed behind a false stalactite formation) and is Greaves's private meeting space when he visits the caves.*

> *"A small, austere room that feels fundamentally unlike the rest of the caves — deliberate, controlled, bureaucratic. A heavy desk of dark wood bears several organized stacks of documents, a locked iron strongbox, and an oil lamp of good quality. Three chairs surround the desk. On the walls, not spirals, not ritual art, but maps — detailed maps of all three islands with notations in a precise, administrative hand, marking patrol routes, potential escape routes, and three red circles corresponding to the three ritual sites. This is where the Souring is managed, not worshipped."*

**Features:**
- **The Documents:** Greaves's operational planning documents in full — ritual site logistics, supply chains, cult member rosters by island, and a personal letter addressed to the Blackwood patriarch (never sent) that reads, in part: *"You were never in control. Not for a single day. I want you to know that. I need you to know that before the end."*
- **The Strongbox (DC 18 Thieves' Tools):** Contains Greaves's personal contingency plan — a set of forged identity documents, 500 gold pieces in assorted currencies, and a sealed vial containing a dose of pure, refined Shinefruit concentrate powerful enough to grant immediate Understanding 2 to any character who has not yet reached that tier. This is Greaves's own personal emergency escape from the Souring's consequences — he had no intention of being present when the entity came through.
- **Evidence Against Greaves:** The documents here, combined with the ledger from the cannery office and the deed of sale from Area 7, constitute definitive, legal-grade evidence of Greaves's leadership role in the cult — more than sufficient to bring to any authority willing to act on it.

**Connections:** Leads back to Area 8 only.

---

### Dungeon Summary for DMs

| Area | Key Feature | Layer Content | Encounter |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 — Entrance Maw | Acoustic effect, spiral sigil | L1: Sigil alerts Watcher | None |
| 2 — Dripping Gallery | Tainted pools, hidden initiate | L1: Faces in water, L2: Conduit network | 2 Glimmering Shards |
| 3 — Congregation Chamber | Horn-frame mirror, altar documents | L1: Watcher, L2: Solid echoes | 4 Initiates + 1 Zealot (if alerted) |
| 4 — Dormitories | Personal effects, hiding Dara | L1: Prayer-map | None (Dara is NPC) |
| 5 — Laboratory | Tinctures, Senne's notes, antidote | None | Senne (if not met earlier) |
| 6 — Chasm Bridge | Steadfast the goat, rope bridge | L2: Fossilized ribs, L3: Ribcage | None |
| 7 — Cult Cache | Stolen picks, chests, Ossian's journal | None | None |
| 8 — Echo Chamber | Acoustic engine, Altar of Resonance | L3: Inner ear revelation | Voice of the Mountain (Ossian) |
| 9 — Deep Ritual Hall | 16 captives, Leo, void circle | L2: Shaft to heart, L3: Amplification truth | 2 Zealots + 4 Initiates |
| 10 — Petrified Heart | The beating heart, full revelation | L3: Complete truth, optional Heart destruction | None (environmental hazard) |
| 11 — Inner Sanctum | Greaves's documents, strongbox | None | None (secret room) |

---

---

## **Part XI: Expanded Location Modules**

### The Seaside Cave: Malakai's Hideout (Act I Climax Dungeon)

*This is the introductory dungeon, the party's first real encounter with cult infrastructure and their first hard combat. It should feel claustrophobic and desperate — a place that was never meant to be a fortress but has been improvised into one by a terrified man running out of time.*

**Overview:** A natural sea cave on the rocky southern shore of the Isle of Murk, accessible only at low tide via a narrow, barnacle-covered passage. The cult uses it for small rituals and as a safehouse. Malakai is here when the party arrives, mid-ritual, with a kidnapped cannery worker named Pell tied to a makeshift altar.

**The Approach:** Navigating the tidal passage requires a **DC 12 Athletics or Acrobatics** check; a failure means the character slips, taking 1d6 bludgeoning damage and making noise that alerts Malakai. The cave mouth is barely large enough for a Medium creature to enter upright.

**Area A — The Tidal Antechamber:** A roughly oval space, twenty feet across, partially flooded at ankle-depth. The walls are covered in crude spiral drawings made with charcoal and what appears to be blood. Fishing nets are piled in one corner, along with a bedroll, empty bottles, and the remnants of several meals. A **DC 14 Intelligence (Investigation)** check finds a hidden journal detailing Malakai's gradual recruitment, his growing horror at what the cult was asking of him, and the specific order from a "superior" (unnamed, but described in terms that match Greaves) to "silence Finnian before the solstice." This is primary evidence for the murder investigation.

**Area B — The Ritual Chamber:** A deeper section of the cave, connected by a low crawlspace (Medium creatures must crouch). This is where Malakai is. The space is thirty feet across, with a natural stone shelf serving as an altar. Pell is bound to the altar, unconscious but alive. Malakai is completing a ritual that involves the void-circle pattern — a smaller, rougher version of the one the party will later find in Area 9 of the Whispering Caves.

**Malakai:** Use the **Sour-touched Initiate** stat block (creature 11) with the following modifications: he has 22 HP (reflecting his longer cult tenure), and on his first turn he uses his bonus action to throw Pell's bindings into the ritual circle if he sees he is losing — threatening to complete the sacrifice unless the party stands down. This creates a moral pressure point: attack Malakai and risk triggering the ritual, or negotiate. A **DC 13 Charisma (Persuasion)** check can convince him to stand down if the party offers him protection from the cult's retribution.

**Resolution:** If Malakai is defeated or persuaded, he reveals all he knows: the cloth is from the Isle of Reeds, the meetings happen "where the mountain whispers," and "the Harbormaster knows everything — he's the one who gave the order." He does not know Greaves by name in a cult context — he only knows him as the man in the administrative mask. This gives the party the threads to pull in Act II while leaving Greaves's identity as the twist to discover.

---

### The Submerged Shrine (Isle of Reeds — Act II Side Location)

*Discovered by following the corrupted Stilt-Walker's Shadow fog event, or by investigating the Blighted Field quest's source. This is a small, atmospheric location rather than a full dungeon.*

**The Shrine:** Located in the deepest section of the Blighted Field, submerged beneath four feet of opaque, black water. At the surface, there is nothing to see — just another patch of flooded marsh. A character with **Understanding 1 or higher** can perceive a faint glow beneath the water at this location. Investigation requires entering the water (Blight-fin Piranhas are a risk — DC 13 Dexterity to avoid the swarm on the way down and up, or stilts can be used to create a rope-guide descent).

**What Is There:** A circular stone platform, three feet across, covered in the cult's spiral symbols. At the platform's center, a carved stone basin contains the Heart of the Blight's feeding root — a dark, ropy tendril that runs from the basin down into the earth below. The basin itself contains a small, idol: a figure with no distinguishing features except a wide-open mouth, carved from a material that is neither stone nor wood nor bone, that the party cannot identify (it is The City in Mercury construction material, identifiable if the party later visits the sunken city).

**Mechanical Function:** The shrine is the baptism site referenced in Act II. Destroying the idol (AC 12, 20 HP, vulnerable to radiant damage) severs the Blight-fin swarm's corruption-tether and causes the Heart of the Blight to take 3d10 necrotic damage immediately, regardless of distance. The Blighted Field's corruption will begin to recede within 24 hours of the idol's destruction, which impresses the Reed-walkers enough that they immediately teach the party the Way of the Stilt without requiring the full Blighted Bloom quest to be completed.

---

### The Blackwood Estate (Act II Social/Investigation Location)

*The party will likely visit the Blackwood Estate after confronting Elara with the shipping ledger from the cannery. It can also be reached by breaking in, though this has social consequences.*

**The Exterior:** A large, stone manor house perched on the hill above Stiltsmere. Its windows are shuttered. The grounds are unkempt — the gardener left three months ago and hasn't been replaced. The front door is locked (DC 15 Thieves' Tools), but a groundskeeper named Holt — elderly, mostly deaf, fiercely loyal to the Blackwood name — tends the gardens and can be convinced to let the party in with a **DC 12 Charisma (Persuasion)** check and any plausible cover story.

**The Ground Floor:** A formal reception room with expensive furnishings covered in dust-sheets, a dining room that hasn't been used in months, and a kitchen where Holt eats alone. The walls throughout are hung with Blackwood family portraits — generations of stern, wealthy islanders. A **DC 13 Wisdom (Perception)** check in the reception room notices that every mirror in the house has been covered with black cloth.

**The Study:** Lord Alistair Blackwood's private study is locked (DC 17 Thieves' Tools). Inside: a desk covered in papers, a cold fireplace, and an overwhelming smell of unwashed wool. The papers reveal the early stages of the Blackwood family's involvement with the cult — Alistair's initial correspondence with the cult's founder (not Greaves — someone older, now dead), his private theory that The City in Mercury's secrets could be safely extracted and monetized, and his growing awareness that he had miscalculated. The final document is a letter to Greaves, dated eight months ago: *"I am withdrawing my support and my funding. This has gone far beyond what was agreed. I want nothing further to do with the Souring, the cult, or any of it."* Greaves's response, clipped to it: *"I'm afraid that's not possible, Alistair."*

**Lord Alistair Blackwood:** He is upstairs, in his bedroom, which he has not left in six weeks. He is alive, elderly, and in the late stages of Souring-induced madness — he has been exposed to the Shine's influence long enough that his Understanding is somewhere between Tier 2 and Tier 3, but his mind was not strong enough to integrate it. He is not violent. He sits in a chair facing the shuttered window and mutters. He will speak to the party if gently approached, and his conversation is fragmentary but lucid in unexpected bursts. He can confirm that Greaves ordered his isolation, that the cult's ritual is tied to the celestial alignment (he knows the exact date — it's in six days), and that Elara does not know the full extent of the family's involvement. He will ask, with sudden, painful clarity: *"Is she safe? My Elara?"*

**Alistair's Safe:** Behind the covered mirror in his bedroom (the irony is not lost on a character who spots it), a wall safe contains 600 gold pieces, the deed to the Blackwood Estate, a personal letter to Elara explaining everything he did and why and how sorry he is, and a small, ornate case containing the **Blackwood Family Signet Ring** — a magic item (see Part XII) that proves Elara's authority over the family's considerable assets and has the additional property of being recognized by Greaves as a symbol of the family's power, granting advantage on Charisma checks against him in a direct confrontation.

---

---

## **Part XII: The Stiltsmere Armory — Magic Items of the Shrouded Isles**

*The magic items of Stiltsmere are not glamorous. They are practical, strange, locally made, and deeply shaped by the environment that produced them. They smell of salt, or mountain stone, or the sweet rot of Glimmer Blooms. They are the kind of items that feel used — that have a history — and that could only exist in this particular place. The DM should introduce these items gradually and organically rather than in a single shopping-trip dump: as loot from the cave caches, as gifts from grateful NPCs, as rewards from the island questlines, or as purchases from Master Corwin's shop at appropriate prices.*

---

### Isle of Murk Items

**Fog-Reader's Compass** *(Uncommon, requires attunement)*
A palm-sized compass made from fused copper and tin, its needle replaced by a sliver of polished mirror-glass. The needle does not point north. Instead, it points toward the nearest area of naturally clear air — a gap in the Shattering Shine, a vent in the cliff face, a cave mouth. While attuned, the bearer always knows the direction of the nearest exit from any fog, regardless of how disorienting the Shine has become. Additionally, once per day, the bearer can use an action to cause the compass to emit a soft, silver pulse that neutralizes a single Soursight within 30 feet — the reflection simply becomes normal for 1 minute. If used in this way, the compass does not point true again until the next dawn.

*"Old Silas has one of these. He says he found it in his grandfather's tackle box, and his grandfather found it in the water."* — Master Corwin

---

**Brenna's Glass Eye** *(Rare, requires attunement by the person Brenna gives it to)*
A realistic glass eye, perfectly crafted, that Brenna the innkeeper will offer to one character — and only one, and only after they have significantly proven their trustworthiness to her — as a gift before the campaign's final act. She does not explain where she got it. The eye, when attuned, replaces any eye the bearer has lost, or can be worn in addition to their natural eyes if uninjured (it simply rests in the palm of the hand and can be held up to look through). While using the eye, the bearer can see through any reflective surface they have looked into within the past 24 hours as if using *clairvoyance*, for up to 10 minutes per day.
