The eye cannot be blinded, dazzled, or affected by any gaze attack that requires eye contact. Once per day, if the bearer is subjected to a Soursight, they can choose to "close" the glass eye, automatically succeeding on the saving throw.

*Brenna simply says: "Keep it. I've done enough looking."*

---

**The Canneryman's Hook** *(Uncommon, requires attunement)*
A prosthetic hook hand, crafted from the metal of the Gaping Maw Cannery's industrial machinery. It is not subtle. It replaces a missing hand, or can be attached over an intact hand as a weapon (though this prevents normal hand use while worn). It functions as a *+1 hand crossbow* when used to fire bolts loaded into a small mechanism in the hook's shaft. Its true value is environmental: the hook can be driven into any stone or wood surface as a bonus action, and while driven in, the bearer cannot be knocked prone, pushed, or forced to move against their will. Removing the hook from a surface is also a bonus action. The hook has been used by enough desperate people in enough desperate places that it has developed a single, minor magical property: once per day, when the bearer is reduced to 0 hit points, they can use their reaction to drive the hook into the nearest surface and stabilize automatically, rather than falling unconscious.

---

**Tomas's Lucky Bottle** *(Common)*
An empty bottle of Murk-folk spirit, sealed with wax, containing nothing but a small folded piece of paper. The paper reads: *"It could have been worse."* Once per day, when the bearer would fail a saving throw, they can shake the bottle and read the paper. The saving throw result improves by 2. This is not enough to turn most failures into successes, but it is occasionally just enough. Tomas, the drunk former Blackwood servant, can be convinced to part with it for the price of three drinks and a conversation.

---

### Isle of Crags Items

**Resonant Stone** *(Uncommon)*
A smooth, dark stone the size of a fist, harvested from a specific vein of mineral in the Whispering Caves that has absorbed decades of the cave's acoustic energy. As an action, the bearer can squeeze the stone firmly, causing it to absorb up to 30 thunder damage that would otherwise affect the bearer or any creature they can touch. The absorbed energy is stored in the stone. As a bonus action, the bearer can release the stored energy in a focused pulse, dealing the stored thunder damage to one target within 30 feet (ranged spell attack, using the bearer's Intelligence modifier). The stone can only store one charge at a time and crumbles to powder if it absorbs its maximum capacity twice without being released. Kaelen can provide one as an additional reward for completing the Lost Herd quest.

---

**Silence-Walker Boots** *(Uncommon, requires attunement)*
Boots made from the cured hide of a Tripod goat by a Crag-herder cobbler of considerable skill. They are thick-soled, ankle-high, and lined with a material that dampens sound in a way that feels physically wrong — stepping hard in them produces almost no impact noise. While wearing these boots, the bearer has advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks that rely on movement sound. Additionally, the boots grant the *Sure-Footed* trait from the Tripod Ravager: advantage on Strength and Dexterity saving throws made against effects that would knock the bearer prone, and the bearer ignores difficult terrain created by rocks, scree, or slopes. Available from a cobbler in Shepherd's Rest for 150 gold pieces, or gifted if the party has significantly helped the Crag-herder community.

---

**The Last Whisper** *(Rare, requires attunement)*
A small, stoppered vial made from cave crystal, containing a single captured acoustic event — a whisper recorded from within the Whispering Caves at a moment of particular resonance. Opening the vial releases the whisper as an auditory effect heard only by the attuned bearer. The content of the whisper is a fragment of truth about a subject of the bearer's choice — not a direct answer, but an oblique, cryptic clue delivered in a voice that sounds like their own, pitched slightly wrong. This functions as a *contact other plane* spell with no chance of intelligence loss, but the answer is always metaphorical and always limited to information about Stiltsmere's history, the cult, or the Souring. Once used, the vial is empty and the attunement ends. Found in the Whispering Caves (Area 7, Chest 2) or purchasable from Master Corwin for 400 gold pieces if he trusts the party enough.

---

### Isle of Reeds Items

**Glimmer Bloom Vial** *(Common, 5 available from Reed-walker merchants)*
A small glass vial containing preserved Glimmer Bloom extract, sealed with beeswax. When consumed as a bonus action, the liquid sheds bright light from the drinker's skin in a 10-foot radius and dim light for another 10 feet, for 1 hour. The light cannot be suppressed except by *darkness* or similar spells. The drinker also regains **1d6 Sanity** as the bloom's natural calming properties take effect. This is the simplest and most available Sanity restoration item in the campaign and should be reasonably affordable (8 gold pieces per vial) to ensure players have access to minor Sanity recovery early.

---

**Stilt-Master's Brace** *(Uncommon, requires attunement)*
A set of articulated iron bracers fitted with small, extendable spike-tips, inspired by the articulated arm-stilts of the Reed-walkers. When the spikes are extended (a free action), the bearer can drive them into soft ground, mud, or shallow water as a bonus action. While braced, they gain the same benefits as the Farming Stilts mechanic: they are elevated six inches above ground-level fog (advantage on saves against Soursights originating from ground-level fog patches) and cannot be grappled by creatures using attacks from below (like the Glimmer Bloom Ambusher's Grappling Vines) while braced. The braces do not help with stilted movement speed, but they make the wearer essentially immune to being knocked prone by ground-level effects while braced. Can be commissioned from the Stilt-Town artisans for 180 gold pieces.

---

**Shinefruit Concentrate** *(Rare, single-use consumable)*
A small, dark vial of refined Shinefruit distillate, concentrated far beyond what the Sour-Sight Tincture contains. Unlike the Tincture, which is designed for slow, cumulative degradation of the consumer's mental defenses, the Concentrate is a controlled, powerful, and deliberately dangerous tool. A creature that consumes the Concentrate makes a **DC 16 Constitution saving throw**. On a failure, they lose **3d10 Sanity** immediately and cannot benefit from Sanity restoration effects (see Part XIII) for 24 hours. On a success, they choose to lose any amount of Sanity up to **3d10** — the power to deliberately and precisely control their own descent. A character who uses this controlled loss to cross into a new Understanding Tier does **not** make the normal threshold-disorientation Constitution save. This is a tool for precise, consensual self-modification rather than involuntary damage. Found in the cult's caches; Senne can produce additional doses as part of an alliance.

---

**Grove-Ward Necklace** *(Uncommon, requires attunement)*
A necklace of woven reed, hung with small, dried segments of Glimmer Bloom stalk that have been treated with the Reed-walker's traditional preservation methods. The necklace smells faintly of the fields. While attuned, the bearer has advantage on Constitution saving throws against poison and the Poisoned condition, reflecting the Glimmer Bloom's natural antibiotic properties. Additionally, the bearer is resistant to the specific poison damage dealt by the Blight-fin Piranha's bite attack. Once per long rest, the bearer can hold the necklace and breathe slowly for 1 minute in a quiet space. If they are in a calm, non-threatening environment when they do this, they regain **2d6 Sanity** (see Part XIII: Sanity Restoration, Environmental Methods). The Reed-walker Elder offers these as gifts to those who complete the Blighted Bloom quest.

---

### Shine-Adjacent Items

**Mirror-Ward Talisman** *(Uncommon, requires attunement)*
A small, circular disc of hammered silver — the exact shape and proportion of a Soursight reflection — with a spiral groove cut into its front face and a rough-sanded back face that produces no reflection whatsoever. While attuned, the bearer has advantage on Sanity saving throws against Soursights specifically (the distorted-reflection experience, not other Sanity threats). Additionally, once per short rest, if the bearer would be affected by a Soursight and fails their saving throw, they can touch the talisman as a reaction to reroll the saving throw, taking the higher result. Master Corwin sells these for 200 gold pieces. The Whispering Caves cache (Area 7) contains one.

---

**The Still Shard** *(Rare, requires attunement)*
A fragment of the Shattering Shine itself, somehow captured and solidified — a piece of the fog that has been rendered permanent, roughly two inches across, that reflects everything perfectly and normally with no Soursight distortion. Attuned bearers can look into it and see an accurate reflection of their own face, which sounds simple but becomes genuinely precious in a campaign where every reflection is wrong. Once per day, the bearer can hold the shard up to a Soursight-afflicted area and use an action to "correct" the reflections in a 20-foot radius — all reflections in the area become accurate for 1 minute, suppressing the Sanity-drain effect of ambient Soursights in that zone. Any Reflected Stalker (creature 3) in the area when this is used is immediately revealed and loses its *Mimic Form* trait for the duration. Found only in The City in Mercury, specifically on one of the inert daises in the Resonator Chamber.
---

**Amulet of Still Reflections** *(Very Rare, requires attunement)*
A silver amulet shaped like an oval mirror whose surface never reflects the wearer. Once per day as an action, the wearer may cause their reflection to vanish from all reflective surfaces for 1 minute. During this minute, they are treated as invisible to aberrations with the **Shine-Born**, **Fog-Bound**, or **Soursight** traits unless those creatures can perceive them by a nonvisual sense. The wearer also has advantage on Sanity saving throws caused specifically by creatures spawned from the Shattering Shine.

If used during an active ritual, the amulet can hide one captive, anchor, or willing creature from the ritual's reflective targeting for 1 round. This does not end a ritual by itself, but it can buy the party one decisive turn.

---

**Fog-Walker's Lens** *(Uncommon)*
A small, perfectly circular lens of a material that appears to be glass but weighs almost nothing, mounted in a simple wire handle. Looking through the lens reveals the Shattering Shine as it truly is — not a solid, reflective fog but an extremely fine particulate suspension of crystallized Shine-matter, through which landmarks and hazards can be clearly seen up to 120 feet despite the fog reducing normal visibility to 5 feet. The lens does not help with Layer-based perception (it is a physical tool, not a psychic one), but it makes navigation through heavy fog dramatically safer. The lens also reveals any creature with the *Fog-Bound* trait (creatures 1 and 2) as glowing silver outlines regardless of their invisibility while in fog. Silas has one and will give it to the party if his grandson Leo is successfully rescued from the Whispering Caves.

---

### Cult-Derived Items

**Maw-Marked Blade** *(Uncommon, requires attunement)*
A shortsword or dagger (DM's choice based on where it's found) that has been used in cult ritual work — its blade is etched with the spiral symbol, and the hilt wrapped in dark cloth. While attuned, the weapon deals an additional 1d4 psychic damage on a hit. Additionally, when the wielder rolls a critical hit, the target must make a **DC 13 Sanity saving throw** or lose 1d6 Sanity as the blade's ritual history briefly resonates. This is not a comfortable item. A character who is attuned to the Maw-Marked Blade has unsettling dreams on any night they sleep within 30 feet of a reflective surface, and their own reflection occasionally appears one heartbeat behind their actual movement. These effects are cosmetic but should be roleplayed. Found on Grave-Maw Zealots or in the Whispering Caves.

---

**Blackwood Family Signet Ring** *(Uncommon, requires attunement by Elara or a character Elara designates)*
A heavy gold ring bearing the Blackwood family crest: three interlocking circles above a wave. This is a social item rather than a combat one. While attuned, the bearer is recognized as speaking with the authority of the Blackwood family on all three islands — the Murk-folk dockworkers who would otherwise turn them away, the Harbormaster's office staff who would delay them, and the more establishment-minded Crag-herders all treat the bearer with deference and cooperation. In a direct confrontation with Harbormaster Greaves, the ring grants advantage on all Charisma-based skill checks, as Greaves spent years cultivating the Blackwood relationship and the sight of the ring in hostile hands is deeply destabilizing to his composure. Found in Lord Alistair Blackwood's safe at the Blackwood Estate. Alistair intended it for Elara.

---

---

## **Part XIII: Sanity Restoration — Player Agency**

*One of the greatest risks in a Sanity-loss system is that players begin to feel passive — that their mental state is something that happens to them rather than something they can engage with. The Whispering Mender (creature 5) represents the campaign's hostile version of Sanity restoration: healing as a threat. But the campaign should also provide players with genuine, active, player-directed methods of recovering Sanity that feel earned, characterful, and consistent with the campaign's themes. The methods below are not safety valves designed to keep the party from ever descending — they are tools that create interesting choices about when and how to invest in stability versus understanding.*

**Design Principle:** No Sanity restoration method in this campaign is free or automatic. Every method requires either a genuine player-directed action, a meaningful investment of time or resources, or a deliberate sacrifice of something else of value. This preserves the weight of Sanity loss while ensuring players are never purely passive.

---

### Category 1: Rest-Based Recovery

**Short Rest — Processing Trauma (DC 13 Wisdom [Insight] Check)**
During a short rest (1 hour), a character can attempt to consciously process the events of the last few hours. This requires the character to spend at least 10 minutes of the rest sitting quietly and not performing any other activities (no tending wounds, no crafting, no conversation). At the end of this period, the character makes a **DC 13 Wisdom (Insight) check**. On a success, they regain **1d8 Sanity**. On a failure, the attempt to process is unsuccessful — the memories return too vividly — and they lose **1d4 Sanity** instead. Characters can only attempt this once per short rest.

*DM Note: The failure state on this check is intentional. Forcing oneself to confront horror can make it worse. Players should understand this before attempting it.*

**Long Rest — Anchoring**
A full long rest (8 hours of relative safety) automatically restores **1d6 + the character's Wisdom modifier** Sanity (minimum 1). This is the baseline recovery rate — reliable but modest. It does not require any action or check. However, a character who is in an actively hostile or deeply disturbing environment (the Whispering Caves, the Glimmer Fields at night, The City in Mercury) gains only half this benefit, rounded down, as the environment continues to erode mental stability even in sleep.

**Extended Rest — Sanctuary Rest**
If the party finds a genuinely safe, comfortable location and rests there for a full day and night without any hostile encounters or disturbing events, each character restores **2d8 + their Wisdom modifier** Sanity. The Salty Squid qualifies for this in the early campaign. After Act II begins in earnest, finding a genuine sanctuary becomes increasingly difficult and the extended rest becomes a meaningful strategic objective.

---

### Category 2: Social & Narrative Recovery

**Confiding in a Trusted NPC**
When a character takes at least 30 minutes to genuinely confide in an NPC they trust about something genuinely difficult they have experienced in the campaign, they may make a **DC 12 Charisma (Persuasion) or Wisdom (Insight) check** to gauge whether the NPC's response has been helpful. On a success, the character regains **1d6 + 2 Sanity**. On a failure, the NPC's response — however well-intentioned — doesn't reach them, and no Sanity is regained (but none is lost).

**Eligible NPCs and their specific bonuses:**
- **Silas:** +4 to the check. He has seen enough of the Shine to understand without judgment. His advice tends toward the practical.
- **Brenna:** +2 to the check. She doesn't offer comfort — she offers perspective. Her willingness to listen must be earned first (requires having helped her or won her respect).
- **Elara (post-Subplot A reveal):** +3 to the check. She is carrying her own weight and sharing it is genuinely mutual. Both characters restore Sanity.
- **Dara (the doubting initiate from the Whispering Caves):** +1 to the check, but on a success, both characters restore Sanity — she needs the connection as much as they do.
- **Master Corwin:** Cannot provide this benefit. His response to confiding will be to begin connecting the experience to historical precedents in a way that is intellectually stimulating but emotionally unavailable.

**Party Support — Mutual Grounding**
When one character uses their action (out of combat) to actively ground another character who is experiencing distress — not just speaking but making eye contact, holding their hands, reminding them of their name and where they are — the grounded character makes a **DC 10 Wisdom save**. On a success, they regain **1d4 Sanity**. On a failure, there is no effect (but no harm). This can only benefit each character once per short rest. It requires no check from the supporting character — it simply requires the intention and the time.

**Humor and Levity**
When the party shares a genuinely funny moment — not gallows humor about the horrors they're experiencing, but an actual moment of lightness, a joke that lands, an absurd situation that makes people at the table actually laugh — each character involved in that moment may regain **1 Sanity**. This is small. It is also the only Sanity restoration in the campaign that requires nothing but presence. The DM should be generous in recognizing these moments rather than gatekeeping them.

---

### Category 3: Environmental & Activity-Based Recovery

**Grounding Activities**
Mundane, skillful, absorbing activities that connect the character to ordinary life rather than cosmic horror. A character who spends at least 1 hour on one of the following activities during a rest period may attempt a **DC 11 Wisdom (Insight) check** to regain **1d6 Sanity** on a success:

- Fishing (on water that is not the Lake of Tears)
- Cooking a meal from scratch
- Repairing or maintaining equipment
- Practicing a craft or musical instrument
- Writing in a journal (see below)
- Any physical exercise performed in a non-hostile environment

These activities work because they require skill, produce something real, and occupy the mind with manageable problem-solving. They do not work in the Shattering Shine, in any Understanding-Layer-active location, or while the character is actively frightened or in danger.

**Journaling**
A character who maintains a written journal of their experiences and spends at least 30 minutes writing in it after a significant event may make a **DC 12 Intelligence (History) or Wisdom (Insight) check** to regain **1d4 + 1 Sanity**. On a success, the act of putting experience into language has organized it in the mind. Additionally, a journal-keeper who reviews their earlier entries when facing a situation they have previously documented gains advantage on the first Sanity saving throw they make in that type of situation. *Example: A character who has written extensively about encountering Soursights gains advantage on the first Soursight save of each session after their third journal entry on the topic.*

**The High Places**
The high-altitude meadow where the Tripod Alpha quest concludes (Isle of Crags), the lighthouse peak on the Isle of Crags in a moment of clear weather, and the upper platforms of Stilt-Town on a night when the Glimmer Fields are fully lit are all designated "high places" — locations of unusual clarity and perspective that are genuinely, naturally peaceful. A character who spends at least 10 minutes in quiet contemplation in one of these locations automatically regains **1d8 Sanity** without a check. No action is required beyond being present. Each location can only be used for this benefit once per long rest.

**Washing in Untainted Water**
The water of the Lake of Tears is tainted by the Shine. The flooded fields of the Isle of Reeds are tainted by the Heart of the Blight. But there are sources of genuinely clean, cold, clear water on the Isle of Crags — mountain springs that run from deep underground, untouched by either influence. A character who bathes fully in a clean-water source on the Isle of Crags for at least 10 minutes automatically regains **2d4 Sanity** and removes the effects of any short-term madness currently affecting them. This can be done once per long rest.

**Witnessing the Moment of Clarity**
When the fog event "A Moment of Clarity" occurs (Random Fog Event Table, entry 10), a character who makes a successful **DC 12 Wisdom (Insight)** check in response not only avoids the failure penalty but also regains an additional **1d8 Sanity** above the standard result — for a total potential gain of 2d8 Sanity from a single fog event. This rewards attentiveness and the willingness to engage with the beauty of the setting alongside its horror.

---

### Category 4: Item-Based Recovery

**Glimmer Bloom Vial** *(Common item, 1d6 Sanity, see Part XII)*
The most accessible Sanity restoration item in the campaign. Abundant in the early game, rarer after the Blight spreads.

**Grove-Ward Necklace** *(Uncommon, 2d6 Sanity once per long rest via meditation, see Part XII)*
A more powerful option tied to completing the Blighted Bloom quest.

**Amulet of Still Reflections** *(Very Rare, advantage on Sanity saves from Shine-creatures, see Part XII)*
Does not directly restore Sanity, but dramatically reduces incoming Sanity loss — functionally equivalent to a large restoration for players who use it proactively.

**The Still Shard** *(Rare, suppresses ambient Soursight drain in an area)*
Again, reduction rather than restoration — but area-wide, making it exceptionally valuable in the final act.

**Custom Potion: Elara's Steadying Draught**
After the Subplot A revelation and the party's alliance with Elara, she can provide up to three doses of a potion she has been brewing for Lord Alistair (unsuccessfully). Each dose restores **3d8 Sanity** when consumed. This is the most powerful single Sanity restoration item in the campaign and should feel significant — Elara gives them without expectation, having finally accepted that Alistair is beyond reaching with ordinary help.

---

### Category 5: Knowledge-Based Paradox Recovery

*These are special recovery methods unique to this campaign's thematic concerns — situations where understanding something deeply enough actually strengthens the mind rather than shattering it.*

**Successful Revelation Processing**
When a character receives the full Layer 3 revelation from the Petrified Heart in the Whispering Caves (Area 10) and makes their successful Wisdom (Insight) check, they permanently gain **+5 maximum Sanity**. This is the only method in the campaign that raises the Sanity ceiling rather than recovering within it. The logic: a truth that has actually been integrated does not continue to drain. It becomes a foundation.

**Defeating an Understanding-Layer Creature**
When a character is the decisive factor in defeating a creature that exists at a higher Understanding layer than their current one — a Reflected Stalker defeated by the character it was mimicking, a Whispering Mender destroyed before it could eject the party — that character may make a **DC 14 Wisdom saving throw**. On a success, they regain **1d6 Sanity** as the act of overcoming the impossible provides genuine psychological grounding. On a failure, nothing happens. This represents the value of demonstrated agency against cosmic horror.

**Completing a Quest with Genuine Emotional Investment**
When a character completes a quest that has clear personal meaning — rescuing Leo (completing Subplot C), convincing Senne to defect (Redemption Arc), returning Steadfast the goat to Kaelen — they may regain **2d6 Sanity** automatically, without a check. This does not apply to purely mechanical achievements (defeating a boss, finding a key). It applies when the party invested emotionally in an outcome and achieved it. The DM should apply this generously. The campaign earns its horror by also earning its moments of genuine human connection.

---

### Sanity Recovery Reference Table

| Method | Sanity Restored | Requires | Frequency |
|---|---|---|---|
| Short Rest Processing | 1d8 (or −1d4 on fail) | DC 13 Wisdom (Insight), 10 min quiet | Once per short rest |
| Long Rest Anchoring | 1d6 + Wis mod | Safety | Once per long rest |
| Sanctuary Rest | 2d8 + Wis mod | Full safe day | Rare |
| Confiding in NPC | 1d6 + 2 | 30 min, trusted NPC | Once per NPC per session |
| Party Grounding | 1d4 | Action, willing partner | Once per short rest |
| Humor/Levity | 1 | Genuine moment | As they occur |
| Grounding Activity | 1d6 | DC 11 Wisdom, 1 hour, mundane task | Once per rest |
| Journaling | 1d4 + 1 | DC 12 check, 30 min, journal | Once per rest |
| High Place Contemplation | 1d8 | 10 min, designated location | Once per long rest, per location |
| Untainted Water Bath | 2d4 | Isle of Crags mountain spring, 10 min | Once per long rest |
| Moment of Clarity (fog event) | 2d8 | DC 12 Wisdom success | As event occurs |
| Glimmer Bloom Vial | 1d6 | Consume item | Per item |
| Grove-Ward Necklace | 2d6 | 1 min meditation (attuned) | Once per long rest |
| Elara's Steadying Draught | 3d8 | Post-Subplot A alliance, consume item | 3 total per campaign |
| Petrified Heart Revelation | +5 max Sanity (permanent) | Understanding 3, successful check | Once per campaign |
| Defeating Layer-Creature | 1d6 | DC 14 Wisdom, decisive role in defeat | Per qualifying defeat |
| Completing Emotionally Invested Quest | 2d6 | Genuine investment + success | Per qualifying quest |
